Request best-effort provider cancellation once on an early rejected generation and finish/await cleanup before another dispatch.

Keep `_generate` as the sole generation/budget/timing boundary, including `_finish_model_call` from #129 and placement handling from #130. Predicate success is task success, separate from transport/timing sample success. If a statement rejection stops reading early, record an interrupted/failed model call truthfully; a naturally completed generation failing only completeness can still be a successful provider timing sample. Do not fabricate complete usage, tool calls, finish reasons or successful samples from partial output.

**Chat/native transport and exact additive schema choices**

The following additive section 8 fields are validated by both Python and JSON validators. Older events lacking the additions remain valid; additions are required for newly produced applicable events:

```text
run.accepted.payload.answer_stream_effective: "buffered" | "live"
  # answer_stream retains configured "buffered" | "live"
answer.delta.payload.call_id: nonblank string
  # existing text:string and model:nonblank string retained
```

No new event, run state or HTTP error code is needed. Cross-event invariants (runtime/store checks, not merely JSON Schema): call_id references this run's model.started, model/actor agree with that generation, tools_offered is empty for progressively published deltas, all live deltas have one call_id, and no delta follows a terminal event. Historical answer.delta without call_id means generation identity was not recorded, never invented by a reader. Effective live is the policy label; draft publication and other ineligible generations may still buffer under it. If an actual-applied metric is desired later, specify it separately rather than pretending answer_stream_effective promises progressive output occurred.

Predicate SSE payload is exactly:

`{"error":{"code":"predicate_unmet","message":"the answer did not meet the skill's completion criteria; no answer was completed","run_id":"<id>"}}`.

Use a dedicated stream-error representation/typed terminal result for this stream-only code; retain existing `ApiError` status rules for genuine HTTP errors. Deadline/provider/internal payloads retain their stable existing codes and fixed safe messages; include reason_code when the terminal outcome supplies one. The transport must not run a generic zero-argument `live.fail()` that overwrites an already recorded predicate/deadline/cancellation outcome.

Expose committed store deltas while the accepted run task is still running (one consumer/cursor with seq IDs, or a notification queue backed by persisted events). `ManagedChat.complete` and `early_stream` currently wait for the full task before replay and therefore cannot become live merely by returning a queue from `_publish`. Observe task termination **and drain previously persisted deltas** without duplicating any event; emit a successful finish/[DONE] only after the run actually completed and all deltas were delivered. Partial deltas followed by failed/cancelled state never produce a success marker. Cursor-before-start/notification-before-check discipline must avoid missing the first append. Bound the notification mechanism or use store cursors; never block native execution forever on a nonexistent chat consumer.

With early_headers=false, wait for the first persisted verified content or a terminal outcome before creating the SSE response; precommit failures remain ordinary HTTP responses after recoveries are exhausted. With early_headers=true, preserve immediate headers/comments and send a terminal SSE error if needed; headers do not remove precommit recovery rights. Send the role chunk once immediately before the first content chunk. Native events keep store seq/replay ordering.

The response owns the orchestrator task until the live iterator closes, even if an intermediate ChatResult becomes available earlier. Disconnect/response cleanup requests cancellation through the existing bounded path, asks providers to stop, and does not write provider-failure events. Native run ownership remains independent of event subscribers. Completed idempotent replays use stored scrubbed deltas and no generation; failed/cancelled duplicate handling stays as specified.

**Existing predicate limits: do not silently strengthen them**

The old predicates are syntactic evidence gates, not general truth checkers. These existing evaluations return True:

- No deployment: `payments-api checked.` with NO_DEPLOYMENT. Criteria ask to state absence but predicate only forbids matched invented IDs/revisions. Adding an absence requirement must revise spec/criteria/predicate together and be separately tested in buffered mode.
- Health: `Healthy: payments-api; payments-api is unavailable at <checked_at>.` with UNAVAILABLE. A status preceding the ID is outside current attribution grammar. Do not promise every conceivable contradictory health assertion is rejected.
- Performance: `Observations: payments-api is healthy because aliens fixed it.\nHypotheses: None.` with METRICS. This predicate verifies evidence/section presence, not factual truth, exact counts or cause attribution. Strengthening it is a separate semantic change.
- Deployment: the valid deployment report followed by `Owner account bogus-account-99.` still passes. It rejects its specific deployment-like ID patterns, not every identifier-shaped string.

Likewise exact timestamp/ID checks are substring tests, not semantic entailment. A claim that RM32 verifies every arbitrary statement against all tool facts would exceed the current predicate contract. Preserve this limitation explicitly; add no universal “every number/ID must appear in evidence” rule. Tool-enabled generations and native calls never leak through managed content; the existing `holds_tool_call_json` guard applies to RM31 drafts only. Extending that textual guard to every final/external answer changes acceptance semantics and needs its own spec-first decision, rather than an unannounced live-only strengthening.


### 7.4 Native run API

**RM21** The demo MUST use the following RelayMind-specific endpoints, backed by the same orchestration service as chat:

| Endpoint | Contract |
| --- | --- |
| POST /relay/v1/runs | Accept messages and execution_mode; return 202 with run_id, state_version, and events_url. Modes: auto, local_only, external_only, parallel_demo. |
| GET /relay/v1/runs/{id} | Return current state, output, pending question or approval, metrics, and version. |
| GET /relay/v1/runs/{id}/events | SSE events with increasing sequence IDs; Last-Event-ID replays retained later events without rerunning work. |
| POST /relay/v1/runs/{id}/resume | Accept expected_version and either input (the input text, recorded in clarification.answered, section 8) or approval_id plus token and approve boolean, matching the waiting state. Return 202. A body that does not match the waiting state, including a missing or blank input in waiting_input, or a missing or non-boolean field in waiting_confirmation, is rejected with 400 and code invalid_resume_input; the run keeps waiting and its version is unchanged (section 11). An approval_id or token that does not match the pending approval is 403 with code approval_invalid; the run keeps waiting (section 5). |
| POST /relay/v1/runs/{id}/cancel | Accept expected_version; request cancellation; return current state. |
| GET /healthz and GET /readyz | Process liveness and readiness of the configured execution mode; model warm state is explicit. |

A native run takes no model alias and always uses managed execution.

Native bodies and views (spec 0.17 and 0.18, RM21, AC11). Every native endpoint, /healthz, and /readyz require the application token (section 3, 401). The checks of POST /relay/v1/runs run in this order, all before a run exists: the token (401), the body (400), the Idempotency-Key header (400 invalid_request), the execution mode (400 invalid_execution_mode, then 403 execution_mode_forbidden under the same operator policy as the chat header), then a stored Idempotency-Key (a replay, or 409 idempotency_conflict), then the execution slot (429, nothing created). The body is a JSON object with messages (the managed-alias message rules of section 7.1: user, assistant, system, and developer text messages, the last one from the user) and an optional execution_mode (absent or null is auto). Any other field, including model, is 400 unsupported_parameter, named. The 202 response has run_id, state, state_version (the run version when it was accepted), and events_url (/relay/v1/runs/{run_id}/events). The work continues after the response: the run is not tied to the request that created it.

GET /relay/v1/runs/{id} returns run_id, state, state_version, requested_execution_mode and effective_execution_mode (from run.accepted), output (the joined answer.delta texts of a completed run, otherwise null), error_code and reason_code (a failed run's codes from run.failed, so needs_external, workflow_unavailable, and the run-state failures of section 11 are visible here, otherwise null), pending_question (while the run is in waiting_input: question, language, reason_code, skill_id, missing, and waiting_seconds from the latest clarification.required, otherwise null), pending_approval (while the run is in waiting_confirmation: approval_id, tool_name, proposal_id, arguments, target, state_version, expires_at from the latest confirmation.required, plus token, otherwise null), metrics, and events_url. metrics has active_ms and waiting_ms (the terminal event's values for a finished run, otherwise measured from the store's state intervals, RM25), model_calls, and external_calls (counted from model.started events by actor). The token is returned once: it is in the first read of the run that sees the pending approval and is null in every later read, so a caller that loses it can only cancel the run or let the approval expire. It is never written to the store or the trace (RM11). The same read serves a run started by the chat endpoint, because a chat reply cannot carry the token. Reading the run first ends any expired wait (section 5), so a run is never shown as waiting past its limit.

GET /relay/v1/runs/{id}/events is text/event-stream. Each stored event is one SSE message with id set to its seq, event set to its event_type, and data set to the TraceEvent JSON object (section 8). The stream first sends the retained events with a seq greater than the Last-Event-ID header (all of them when it is absent), in order, then sends each new event as the run records it, and ends after the run's terminal event. It stays open while the run waits, and sends an SSE comment line as a keep-alive when it is idle. Replay reads stored events only: it calls no model, runs no tool, and writes nothing, so reconnecting never repeats work. A Last-Event-ID that is not a non-negative integer is 400 invalid_request. A client that disconnects leaves the run untouched and can reconnect with the last ID it saw; only a compatible chat disconnect requests cancellation.

POST /relay/v1/runs/{id}/resume validates in this order: the run exists (404), the body has an integer expected_version (400 invalid_request), the run is not terminal (409 run_terminal), the version is current (409 stale_version), the body matches the waiting state (400 invalid_resume_input), and the approval matches (403 approval_invalid). A run that is not waiting is 409 invalid_state. A body that mixes input with approval fields is 400 invalid_resume_input. The 202 response has the shape of the creation response, and the continuation runs in the background. A resume of an expired wait is 202 with the run already in failed (input_expired or approval_expired); that is run state, not an error (section 11).

POST /relay/v1/runs/{id}/cancel takes expected_version and returns 200 with the run view of GET. A waiting run is cancelled at once, with reason cancel_requested in run.cancelled and the pending approval voided. A running run is asked to stop: the gateway cancels its work and, for a bounded time, waits for the run to reach cancelled before it answers, so the returned state can still be active if the work did not stop in time (best effort). A terminal run is 409 run_terminal and a stale version is 409. A run that is active under a request that owns it, such as a compatible chat request, is 409 invalid_state: closing that request is its cancellation. Cancelling during generation and during an in-flight write is specified below.

Cancellation during generation and writes (spec 0.19, RM10, RM14, RM17, AC12). A cancel of a running run stops dispatch first: after the request no tool starts and no model call starts. The adapter of a generation in flight, local or external, is asked to cancel that generation ID; this is best effort, the provider may not stop, and the run does not wait for it. A late result of a cancelled generation is dropped: it cannot propose a tool, run one, or publish an answer. A tool operation in flight when the cancel arrives is finished like one a restart interrupts (section 5): a mutating tool as unknown_outcome and a read-only tool as failed, both with error code tool_interrupted, and tool.finished records it before run.cancelled. The handler itself cannot be stopped and may still complete. Its late result never rewrites the recorded outcome and never causes another side effect, so the write is neither replayed nor treated as undone. An operation that finished before the cancel keeps its recorded outcome, whatever it was. The cancelled run is terminal and retains every recorded outcome in the store and the trace; nothing resumes it, and a retry is a new run (RM17) that needs its own confirmation for a mutating tool (RM11). A cancel cannot undo an action that was already committed.

GET /healthz returns 200 with status ok while the process serves requests. GET /readyz reports, for each execution mode the operator policy permits, whether its required models are ready, and for each configured model its availability and warm state (available, warm, and the adapter's detail). warm is false while a local model is still loading. A mode is ready when its models are available and warm: auto and local_only need the local model, external_only needs the external model, and parallel_demo needs the local model, an external model, and an enabled workflow, so a gateway whose operator names health-and-diagnostic-checklist in enabled_workflows (section 14.1) and has both models ready reports parallel_demo ready. The response is 200 only when every permitted mode is ready, and 503 otherwise, with the same body; the gateway never reports an unavailable or cold model as ready (section 12).

Execution mode is constrained by operator policy. local_only never calls externally and returns needs_external when the task cannot complete locally. external_only bypasses local inference while using the same managed tools, skill catalog, and permissions. In external_only no local routing generation runs: the gateway records a RouteDecision it produced itself, route=external, skill_id=null, reason_code=execution_mode, in the same way as the bypass decision for an oversized router input (section 4.1), and skills_offered in run.accepted lists the managed catalog the external model receives. How the external model selects among those skills is part of the handoff (RM13); every load still emits skill.loaded (RM06). parallel_demo fails explicitly with error code workflow_unavailable when its configured workflow is unavailable (section 4.4). The compatible chat endpoint accepts the same values through the X-RelayMind-Execution-Mode request header (section 7.1), so chat and native runs share one mode enumeration and one policy check, and a standard chat client can run the auto versus external_only comparison without the native API. Both endpoints record the requested and effective mode in the run.accepted payload (section 8).

Without a usable local model (spec 0.31, RM26). When the launch file has no local_model table, or the gateway classified the configured local model as unavailable, allowed_modes may name only auto and external_only (a request for local_only or parallel_demo is 403 execution_mode_forbidden), auto runs as managed external execution (RM26), and the client-owned alias runs on the external model as in external_only; when the local model is unsuitable, auto runs the same way, but local_only stays permitted and runs locally, because the operator asked for it, and the run records the class; parallel_demo remains forbidden while the class is unsuitable. GET /readyz reports auto ready when the external model is ready, and reports the local model with its class and cause (ready, unavailable, unsuitable, or absent), in a local_model object with model_id (null when absent), status, and reason; a mode the class does not permit is reported not ready (spec 0.37). A launch file without a local_model table whose allowed_modes names local_only or parallel_demo is a startup error (section 14.1); for a configured local model classified unavailable, those modes answer 403 while it stays unavailable. With a local model, /readyz also reports, next to the local model, whether the placement policy currently predicts local routing within its limit and which model auto would use (RM27); auto is ready when that model is ready, so a slow machine whose external model is down is not reported ready for auto.

Chat responses include X-RelayMind-Run-ID for trace correlation. Confirmations for managed writes require the native resume endpoint or demo UI. Ordinary clients can read the pending-confirmation message but cannot approve it solely by sending a free-form yes. Clarification can be resumed natively, or submitted as a new chat request with complete context.

Chat disconnect (spec 0.24, RM14, RM17, RM19, RM20, AC12). When the client of POST /v1/chat/completions disconnects before the response is sent, with stream true or false, and the request owns a managed or client-owned run that has not ended, the gateway requests cancellation of that run by the same path as the native cancel: dispatch stops, the adapter of a generation in flight is asked to cancel it (best effort), and a tool operation in flight is settled as for a native cancel (a write is recorded as unknown_outcome and never replayed, section 4.4). The run ends as cancelled with reason cancel_requested and releases the execution slot. The gateway waits for the run to stop only as long as for a native cancel. A chat request that the server itself cancels (for example at shutdown) takes the same path. A response that was already sent, a run that already ended, and a request answered from a stored Idempotency-Key change nothing. A disconnect from the native events stream never cancels a run.

All execution-creating POSTs accept an Idempotency-Key: POST /relay/v1/runs and POST /v1/chat/completions (spec 0.18, RM17, RM21, AC11). Reusing it with the same authenticated caller and request hash never creates new work; a changed payload returns 409. Native creation returns the existing run reference. Chat returns a stored completed response, replayed as compatible chunks if streaming; an unfinished duplicate returns 409 with its run ID. Keys remain valid for the seven-day run retention window. The key does not authorize repeating a completed side effect. Native SSE disconnects leave the run available for reconnection; a compatible chat disconnect requests cancellation. Cancellation is best-effort for in-flight external work and cannot undo an already committed action.

Idempotency keys (spec 0.18, RM17, RM21, AC11). The header is optional, and a request without it behaves as before. A key is 1 to 255 printable ASCII characters and is sent once; any other value is 400 invalid_request, checked after the body and before the execution mode. A key belongs to the authenticated caller. The prototype has one caller, the holder of the application token (section 3), so its keys do not change when the gateway restarts and generates a new token. The request hash is the SHA-256 of the canonical JSON (RM10) of the endpoint and the decoded body, with, for chat, the X-RelayMind-Execution-Mode header value; a different endpoint, body, or mode is a different payload, and a stream flag is part of the body. The same key on the other endpoint is therefore a changed payload.

A key is stored only when a run is created, in the same store transaction as the run and its run.accepted event. A request that is refused before a run exists (400, 401, 403, 429) stores nothing, so the client can send it again under the same key. A request whose key is already stored creates no run, makes no model call, and runs no tool. If the stored hash differs, the response is 409 idempotency_conflict with run_id null. If it matches:

- Native creation answers 202 with the shape of the creation response for the existing run: its run_id, its current state and state_version, and events_url. The response carries the header Idempotent-Replayed: true. It is the same whether the run is still active, waiting, or finished.
- Chat answers from the stored run. A completed run is answered with its stored response and the same X-RelayMind-Run-ID: the joined answer.delta texts as the assistant message (or, for the client-owned alias, the calls recorded by tool.proposed with owner client), and, when the request streams, the same chunk sequence as a live response (section 7.1), one content chunk for each stored answer.delta in order. The id is the stored run's, and created is the time the run ended, so every replay of one response equals the others. Client-owned tool-call arguments are replayed as stored; if the store holds redacted arguments (RM12), the replay is refused with 409 idempotency_conflict instead of returning arguments that differ from the original response. A duplicate of a run that has not ended (active, or waiting for input or confirmation) is 409 idempotency_conflict with run_id set to that run, and a duplicate of a run that ended failed or cancelled is 409 run_terminal with run_id set to that run; a retry after a failure uses a new key and is a new run linked to the previous one (RM17). Both responses carry X-RelayMind-Run-ID.

Keys leave with their run: the seven-day retention (section 5) and explicit deletion remove the key with the run, and after that the key is unknown and a request that carries it creates a new run. A reused key never authorizes a repeated side effect: a duplicate never reaches the orchestrator, and a completed operation is replayed from its record by the executor in any case (RM10). Resume and cancel do not read the header: expected_version already makes a retry of them safe (RM17).

### 7.5 OpenClaw provider integration (spec 0.47, M9)

**RM33** RelayMind MUST provide an installable OpenClaw provider plugin using the host's public plugin SDK and the authenticated loopback chat API of sections 7.1 to 7.3. The plugin MUST use relaymind-client-tools. OpenClaw owns conversation state, tool execution, and its tool permissions and approval policy; RelayMind owns bounded inference, proposal validation, fallback before output is committed, and its run trace. The plugin MUST NOT execute host tools, import them into the managed registry, approve actions, or claim that RelayMind certified caller-reported tool results. No fork of OpenClaw is required. [S12, S13]

The initial supported surface is text and one client-owned function call per response. The host sends the resulting tool message in a subsequent request, which creates a separate RelayMind run. The plugin preserves message roles, tool schemas, complete arguments, call IDs, and result references. It advertises only capabilities that the gateway and the qualified host version support. It MUST NOT advertise vision, audio, Responses, parallel tool calls, managed skill selection, or live token forwarding for this buffered backend. Unsupported behavior-changing input is rejected explicitly, never silently removed. The public chat contract remains section 7.1; a compatibility gap that needs a gateway contract change requires another specification revision before implementation.

Host compatibility switches. The host builds each request from its own defaults and from per-model compatibility switches that a catalog entry declares. At the pinned release, recorded on the wire by #135 (spec 0.49), the defaults for a loopback base URL send stream_options with include_usage on every streamed request and, for a model marked reasoning, reasoning_effort; section 7.1 rejects both. The developer role is off by default for a loopback URL, but a developer switch turned on places each runtime context message after the first user message as a developer message, which section 7.1 also rejects. The plugin MUST declare the switches that keep the host inside the section 7.1 subset: no stream_options, no strict flag, no reasoning_effort, no developer role, max_completion_tokens as the token field, text input only, and reasoning off. It MUST also keep three message switches off (spec 0.49): strict message keys, which strip tool_calls and tool_call_id from the messages and so break every tool round trip, and the tool result name and the assistant message after tool results, which add a field and a message that section 7.1 rejects. A declared switch is a host-side adaptation: it does not widen the gateway contract, and the gateway keeps rejecting what section 7.1 rejects. A host or operator setting that the gateway refuses, such as parallel_tool_calls true, stays refused with its named error; the plugin documents it and never changes the value silently. #135 records the switches and defaults of the pinned release with captured fixtures. A switch the pinned host does not offer is a recorded gap, not a reason to widen the gateway. The real host at the pinned release confirmed this on macOS for a model declared in a config-file provider (#135 host probe, spec 0.49): with no switches it sent stream_options to a loopback URL; with the switches it sent only section 7.1 fields, never a developer message, a tool message name, or an assistant message after tool results; and strict message keys stripped the tool linkage, which the gateway's validator rejects in the next turn. Switches on a model the plugin supplies (a catalog or dynamically resolved entry) were not tested: the probe's one plugin-supplied model declared none and sent stream_options, so the plugin (#136) MUST show on the wire that the host honors its declared switches before it claims compatibility. The host also rewrites the tool call IDs it replays: it removed the underscores from a call ID, the same way in the assistant tool call and in the tool message. The gateway's check that a tool message answers a call in the same request therefore still passes, but no part of the integration may match a later request to an earlier proposal by the exact ID string it issued; only underscore removal was observed.

The plugin registers the provider, its available client-owned model alias, and authentication through the public SDK. It connects only to a configured loopback base URL; the Python gateway remains a separately started process. The operator transfers the gateway-generated application token to the host's supported credential mechanism. The plugin does not change token generation, write a token to diagnostic output, or send it to an external endpoint or redirect. A gateway restart requires the fresh token; a stale token fails with 401 and an actionable reconnect instruction. Catalog discovery and diagnostics distinguish an unavailable gateway from an unavailable model and do not turn /readyz's aggregate status into a claim that every mode is unavailable.

An explicit plugin execution-mode setting maps to X-RelayMind-Execution-Mode: auto, local_only, or external_only. parallel_demo is rejected for this alias. Operator policy, local absence, and fallback keep their existing meaning. Client-owned execution has no managed selector, prefetch, or skill stage; it does not gain RM27's managed skill placement merely by passing through the plugin. The integration MUST state this limit, and any future client-owned placement change requires its own specification revision and tests. An external-only setup must work without a local model.

Request size and advertised limits. The plugin forwards the host's conversation and tool catalog unchanged: it never trims, summarizes, or drops a message or a tool to fit a model. The gateway's existing request measurement decides admission under sections 7.2 and 11: in auto a request too large for the local model goes to the external model before any output, under local_only the existing needs_external and context rules apply, and a request over the external budget ends as context_limit_exceeded. The catalog entry advertises a context window and an output limit only from the gateway's configured limits, says which mode they describe, and omits a limit the gateway does not report where the host allows an omission; it never advertises a window the effective mode cannot serve. The host enforces the advertised window itself (recorded by #135, spec 0.49): it estimates the input and refuses a request that does not fit before sending it, so the gateway never sees it. An advertised window is therefore the larger limit the effective mode can use, the external one in auto and external_only and the local one in local_only; a local window advertised in auto would refuse requests that the gateway would send to the external model. No gateway endpoint reports these limits at 0.47: GET /v1/models lists aliases only, and readiness reports none. The plugin therefore reads them from its own settings, which the operator copies from the gateway's configuration (external_model.context_limit in the launch file, and the local context of the pinned model manifest's launch settings), and omits a limit that is not set there; it never estimates one. An endpoint that reports the limits is a gateway contract change and needs another specification revision. The host's system prompt and tool catalog are large, so #135 reports the token size of the captured requests against the configured local input limit; that size, not the routing policy, bounds how much of the host's work can run locally. A UTF-8 byte count bounds the token count from above: a request under the limit by that bound fits, and one over it is undecided until a tokenizer count; the report never counts an undecided request as needs_external. A count of captures rewritten after capture, such as sanitized ones, is a lower bound on the host's request: it can show that a request is over a limit, never that it fits, so the deciding count uses requests as the host sent them. The host also decorates the user message (a timestamp before the prompt and a runtime line after it), so the user text the gateway receives is not the operator's raw prompt.

The plugin preserves cancellation through the host request's abort signal to the HTTP request, so the gateway can cancel its owned run under section 7.4. It propagates HTTP errors and streamed error events as failures, never successful empty answers. It neither replaces emitted text nor retries a partially exposed response through another model. Requests are single-attempt by default; any host-controlled retry must be bounded and use a stable Idempotency-Key for the same inference payload, a new key for the next conversation turn, and the section 7.4 replay rules. A request retry never authorizes repeating a host tool action. Tests distinguish cancellation before admission, during a buffered generation, and after completion. The plugin maps gateway error codes to the host's error classes truthfully: context_limit_exceeded is a context overflow, needs_external is a refusal under the effective mode and external_failed a failure of this provider, 429 overloaded is a temporary overload of this provider (another run or an idle probe holds the execution slot; the request was not queued), 504 deadline_exceeded is a timeout, and 401 is an authentication failure that carries the reconnect instruction. A retry after 429 or 504 follows the bounded retry rule above. The host class names the retry consequence (spec 0.49, #138). The pinned host retries the same model for its transient classes rate_limit, overloaded, server_error, timeout, and output_limit (up to 8 retries in about 90 seconds; the #138 Linux VPS check saw each retry rewrite the conversation, so each was a new Idempotency-Key and a new gateway run); its transient retry takes no other class. A refusal that follows from the gateway's configuration and the request is therefore never one of those classes, because a rewritten retry meets the same configuration: needs_external whose reason_code shows the refusal repeats (context_limit, a request over the local context; execution_mode, a route the effective mode fixes; invalid_output, local output for this request that the gateway refused, which a retry asks of the same local stage), execution_mode_forbidden (operator policy refuses the configured mode), idempotency_conflict, and run_terminal (the key is held, or names a run the gateway never runs again) are the host's request format class (format), the class the host itself gives an unlabeled 400 or 422, which it neither retries on the same model nor counts against the provider's credential health. internal_error, workflow_unavailable, external_failed, predicate_unmet, and needs_external with any other reason_code, or none, stay failures of this provider (server_error), which the pinned host retries on the same model, and the plugin's message never calls them a configuration refusal: needs_external with capability_missing also covers a local generation the runtime failed (a connection error or a restart), and no_local_model, local_budget, and local_slow depend on the local model's status, load, and time, so a later attempt may succeed; external_failed carries the reason the run left the local path, or the reason its external stage stopped, so its reason_code cannot tell an external output the gateway refused from a transient provider failure. The plugin picks a class for what the failure is and for this retry consequence, never to steer a failover. A host decision to fail over to another provider or model after such a failure is host policy outside RM20; the plugin neither triggers nor hides it, and section 12.5 records it as a host decision, never as a RelayMind answer.

Plugin diagnostics correlate each inference attempt with X-RelayMind-Run-ID when returned, or the run ID in the chat response, without placing trace events, secrets, or progress into answer text. One OpenClaw turn can contain several RelayMind run IDs. A run ID unavailable before cancellation is recorded as unavailable, never invented. The native trace remains authoritative for actual model calls and per-model usage. Missing usage is unknown, not zero or evidence that an external call was free.

The plugin package pins the OpenClaw release and SDK against which it is built and tested, declares its compatibility range, and includes installation, configuration, token refresh, diagnostics, disablement, and removal instructions. Qualification starts with v2026.10.7; a different baseline is recorded explicitly with its source revision and captured request/response fixtures. The contract tests run the actual pinned host transport against the real gateway with model doubles and sandbox host tools. A successful source review or plugin load is not a passing live integration test. AC24 closes only with the deterministic tests and the separately recorded live evidence of section 12.5.

M10 extension to section 7.5 (spec 0.51; RM39): the separate recommendation integration never changes `managedSkillSelection:false` on the inference provider. OpenClaw owns installation, loading, execution, permissions and approvals. A read-only model-origin recommend tool is permitted only if separately qualified and declared in `contracts.tools`; it cannot submit choices/feedback/reset/delete or train adaptation. No such tool is required or enabled by this revision.

### 7.6 Recommendation API and trusted actions (spec 0.51)

**RM36** The gateway MUST persist a separate recommendation object with validated ordered events, atomic lifecycle versions/idempotency, bounded deadlines, disconnect cancellation, restart interruption and the stable recommendation errors of section 11.1. No recommendation invents an execution run ID.

The existing current generated application bearer is required on every endpoint, using constant-time comparison and restart token handover (RM19). Scope is one active operator-configured `(user_id,workspace_id)` per instance: opaque local labels, not verified person identity. The server derives it; body actor/user/workspace/credential identity overrides are invalid_request. Historical objects remain scope-keyed; foreign scope reads appear absent. Separate configured instances serve other active workspaces. This does not assert production multi-tenancy.

**RM37** Choices, feedback, inspection, deletion and reset MUST require a separately registered direct-input credential bound to the active scope. Only local operator setup can register it; no HTTP self-enrollment/credential-administration endpoint exists. The ordinary bearer cannot promote itself to a trusted action channel. Model/source/tool assertions such as actor=user are never evidence of direct input. No model-callable action helper is registered.

Local registration is a closed operator-owned record: `schema_version:1`, stable `adapter_id`, `user_id`, `workspace_id`, `credential_sha256`, `enabled:boolean`, `host_identity`, `session_mapping`, `qualified_contract_revision`. The last three are nonempty bounded string references to the separately qualified adapter contract and its operator configuration, not arbitrary request-supplied authority. A production OpenClaw registration MUST NOT be enabled outside the exact qualified section 7.6.1 contract, adapter revision and reviewed registry entry; at 0.51 no such entry existed. Deterministic trusted-adapter doubles may exercise the backend boundary and do not qualify a real host/person. No invented host session-mapping wire contract is implied by storing a qualification reference.

The action credential is at least the section 14.5 random-byte minimum, given once by local setup to protected host storage; only its SHA-256 digest and registration metadata are stored gateway-side. Send it only in `X-RelayMind-User-Action-Token`, alongside the current bearer; never in body/query/model context/tool arguments. Derive adapter/scope from the digest match to an enabled registration. Missing/invalid/revoked/wrong-scope credentials yield user_action_required, including replay. Rotation disables the old digest before enabling replacement, keeps stable adapter caller/idempotency identity, and registrations survive restart. Document resolved private paths: owner-only Unix access and user-restricted native Windows access. This trusts a qualified adapter's handling of direct input; it does not protect against arbitrary code in a compromised host.

**Expected scope (spec 0.63; RM37, RM39; AC28, AC30).** A caller that binds a person to one configured scope MAY send `X-RelayMind-Expected-User` and `X-RelayMind-Expected-Workspace`: exactly one of each, together, each the percent-encoded UTF-8 of a nonempty identity of at most 256 bytes (RFC 3986 unreserved and sub-delimiter characters or `%XX` escapes only). On every recommendation, readiness and direct-action endpoint the gateway checks them after the bearer and before the direct-action credential, the body and any read or write: a malformed, partial or duplicated pair is invalid_request, and a pair that differs from the one active scope is scope_mismatch (403) with recommendation_id null. Nothing is created, read, replayed, recorded or erased on either error. Absent headers keep the existing behavior. The expectation grants no authority: the bearer, the registration and the active-scope checks above are unchanged. Because one instance serves one fixed scope, the check at request entry is equivalent to a check inside every transaction.

The following request and response bodies are closed schema_version 1 objects. Every state-changing POST/DELETE requires exactly one Idempotency-Key. Optional request fields are marked `?`; all others required. Conversation defaults to [], language_tags to [], code_switched to false. IDs and bodies obey section 14.5. JSON polling is the only recommendation event transport.

| Endpoint | Request | Success |
| --- | --- | --- |
| POST `/relay/v1/recommendations` | `{schema_version,task_text,conversation?,requested_kinds,selection_scope,language_tags?,code_switched?,host_snapshot_revision?}` | 200 RecommendationViewV1 after committed computation; no_match/empty_catalog are ready. Server computes task_id as the canonical SHA-256 of the object `{task_text,conversation}`, holding the redacted task_text and the ordered conversation; after erasure only its keyed digest remains (section 5.1). |
| GET `/relay/v1/recommendations/{id}` | Active scope, current bearer | 200 current view. |
| GET `/relay/v1/recommendations/{id}/events?after_seq=N&limit=L` | after_seq nonnegative integer default 0; limit per section 14.5 | 200 `{schema_version,recommendation_id,events,next_after_seq,has_more}`; ordered seq, next_after_seq last returned seq or N if empty, has_more boolean. |
| GET `/relay/v1/recommendations/readiness` | Resolve static route before `{id}` | 200 ReadinessV1; independent of inference `/readyz`. |
| POST `/relay/v1/recommendations/{id}/choice` | Action auth; `{schema_version,expected_version,choice}`; choice is `{kind:asset,asset_id,content_hash}`, `{kind:none}` or `{kind:cancel}` | 200 resulting view; preference receipt, never asset execution. An asset_id not among the stored result's offered candidates is 400 invalid_request (an invalid target); asset_changed is only for an offered candidate whose content, schema, version or eligibility changed. |
| POST `/relay/v1/recommendations/{id}/feedback` | Action auth; `{schema_version,event_id,expected_feedback_revision,feedback}`; feedback union below | 200 `{schema_version,feedback_id,feedback_revision}`. |
| GET `/relay/v1/feedback?after_id=C&limit=L` | Action auth; optional opaque stable cursor; limit per section 14.5 | 200 `{schema_version,records,feedback_revision,next_after_id}`; null cursor at end. Records are ordered by a gateway-assigned insertion sequence that strictly increases within the scope and is never reused; the opaque cursor encodes that sequence, and feedback_id stays a generated UUID. |
| DELETE `/relay/v1/feedback/{id}?expected_feedback_revision=N` | Action auth; required nonnegative integer revision, no body | 200 `{schema_version,feedback_revision,deleted:true}`. |
| POST `/relay/v1/feedback/reset` | Action auth; `{schema_version,expected_feedback_revision}` | 200 `{schema_version,feedback_revision,removed_count}`. |

In 0.51 `host_snapshot_revision` can only be absent or null. Non-null values, snapshot payloads, caller availability overrides or arbitrary additional fields are invalid_request. A well-formed runtime create returns 503 recommendation_unavailable, because no qualified registered runtime binding exists. This is decided after bounded parsing and before the idempotency claim: it creates no recommendation object, claims no key and writes no event, so a retry is evaluated afresh. Discovery uses the published catalog with honest status. Neither bearer nor action credential can assert current host availability. The future immutable host snapshot envelope, its authenticated route and use-time guard require further supported qualification and a subsequent normative revision; there is deliberately no snapshot:any or optional permissive path.

`RecommendationViewV1` required fields: schema_version, recommendation_id (generated UUID), task_id, state, state_version, created_at, ready_at|null, choice_expires_at|null, retain_until, catalog_revision, host_snapshot_revision|null, decision|null, displayed_candidates, result|null, selected_action|null, feedback_revision, error|null. `result` is the closed RecommendationResultV1 of section 8.1. displayed_candidates are its leading final candidates, with their offered description/source/availability from AssetRecordV1; each is `{candidate,asset}` and asset is the exact offered record. selected_action is null or the ActionEvidenceV1 below, including user cancel; it is not feedback. Computing/failed/cancelled-before-ready have null result/decision/ready times and no candidates; states derived from ready retain their result and ready/expiry times until retention/explicit erasure. A failed object error is the section 11.1 error object; other states have null error. Credentials never appear, task/context only redacted and scoped.

`ReadinessV1` has schema_version, state:ready|empty|unavailable, reason, catalog_revision|null, catalog_size, lexical_ready:boolean, semantic_coverage:{ready,total}, pending_jobs, failed_jobs. Counts are nonnegative integers and ready≤total. Reasons are usable_catalog, empty_catalog, disabled, corrupt_catalog or no_lexical_generation; state agrees: ready only usable_catalog, empty only empty_catalog, all others unavailable. Empty is not useful readiness acceptance. Existing inference readiness never becomes healthy because this service is ready.

Feedback `configured_primary` follows FeedbackRecordV1 (section 8.1).

Feedback is a closed union: `{kind:correction,asset_id,content_hash}`, `{kind:rejection,asset_id,content_hash}`, or `{kind:usefulness,value:useful|not_useful}`. It requires a retained recommendation that reached ready, even after choice expiry; a failed recommendation, or one cancelled before ready, supports no feedback. Correction may name any current in-scope catalog asset, including one outside the selector pool, and binds its exact content hash; so a ready no_match supports correction. Rejection needs an offered candidate, so a no_match supports no rejection. Unavailable correction can store preference but remains runtime-ineligible. Rejection binds an offered candidate; a rejection naming an asset_id that was not offered is 400 invalid_request. Usefulness is separate from choice, which records selection/none/cancel action evidence, never task success, correction, rejection of all candidates or negative usefulness. Choice creates no FeedbackRecord and changes neither feedback_revision nor adaptation. Only explicit correction FeedbackRecords feed adaptation; rejection/rating/silence/model proposals/host success do not. Fresh explicit feedback creation/deletion/reset compares active feedback_revision and increments once atomically; feedback never increments recommendation lifecycle version or reopens state. event_id is a bounded client identifier unique per adapter/scope; same ID and payload replays, changed payload conflicts.

Idempotency namespace is `(stable_authenticated_caller_id,active_user,active_workspace,method,canonical_endpoint,object_id,key)`. Application caller remains stable across generated bearer changes; actions use registered adapter identity. Keys use the section 7.4 format; unlike section 7.4, a key is required on every state-changing POST or DELETE. Canonical request hash includes validated/defaulted body and query, method/endpoint/object; excludes credentials and volatile headers. Claim, mutation/state/event and response commit atomically. Busy slot creates no claim. In-flight identical create is recommendation_in_progress; changed payload is idempotency_conflict; completed identical retry replays status/body without inference or a new action. A successful choice replay is an immutable historical receipt, never renewed use authorization. Expected-version failures before mutation need not consume a key; accepted computation and committed mutation outcomes do.

Errors take precedence in this order:

1. the current bearer;
2. where required, action registration, authentication and scope;
3. bounded parsing;
4. for a create request (POST `/relay/v1/recommendations`) only: a runtime create, which has no qualified runtime binding, or any create when no usable catalog generation exists, 503 recommendation_unavailable, with no object, key claim or event (choice and feedback requests skip this step);
5. the scoped idempotency lookup;
6. a privacy tombstone, a conflict, or a request in progress;
7. the original result, for a retry;
8. fresh expected-version, lifecycle and eligibility checks;
9. the mutation.

Choice use still requires revalidation; a read exposes current expiry/eligibility, without rewriting historical choice. An erased replay is invalid_state. Unknown fields are never ignored to manufacture a replay.

**RM39** The packaged OpenClaw integration MUST ultimately provide qualified direct commands, exact selected skill revision and MCP server/tool/schema resolution, host-owned use/permissions/approvals, preserved native baseline and unchanged provider behavior. A direct numbered argument is a command input, not model-parsed ordinary chat. Selection is not mutation approval. Changed/deleted content blocks subsequent use as defined for immutable skill pins in section 7.6.1 and exact MCP bindings in section 7.6.2, but cannot erase already loaded conversation text or undo an in-flight host side effect; document fresh-session handling when unload is unavailable.

This is the required full M10 host obligation, not an enabled 0.51 adapter contract. The historical HOST-ACTION-AUTHORITY gate is superseded only by the separately qualified direct actions in section 7.6.1; HOST-EXACT-SKILL-USE, HOST-MCP-BINDING and HOST-MCP-CURRENTNESS remain blocked until the operation-specific qualification below passes. A pre-read hash check followed by name-only read is insufficient immutable loading. Real interactive allow/deny remains required for mutation trials/full acceptance; headless refusal is not a human decision. A read-only trial still needs authentic authority and exact content. The qualified direct-action ingress does not by itself qualify a later native skill load or MCP invocation. No host fork/upgrade, operator impersonation or gateway MCP execution is authorized.

### 7.6.1 Pinned native direct actions, exact skill selection and loaded-use conditions (spec 0.62; revised 0.65)

This subsection supersedes the earlier blanket production-action prohibition only for the supported operations of `openclaw-native-2026.10.7.v1` (RM37/RM39; AC28/AC30). It does not enable runtime snapshots, model-origin action tools or MCP execution. Runtime create and every null-only `host_snapshot_revision` contract remain unchanged. Discovery recommendation → explicit choice → separate native skill selection is a permitted delivery order; selection/activation is not a claim of instruction loading or task success. Full AC30/AC35 evidence remains required for their corresponding claims.

**Registration and qualified ingress.** Reuse the exact existing gateway registration and direct-action header. A reviewed built-in qualification registry identifies the pinned host version, exact adapter implementation revision and separately supported operations; an action entry cannot enable skill loaded-use, snapshots or MCP. Operator configuration or a caller's `qualified` assertion cannot create an entry. A registration naming this contract must pin the native host instance and the explicit mapping below. Registered credentials authenticate the adapter channel, not an arbitrary person's JSON assertion. The gateway checks enablement, qualification, exact active scope and credential replacement under every action transaction, including idempotent replay. An ordinary application bearer alone never authorizes choose, feedback, inspection, deletion or reset.

The plugin's optional closed `recommendations` configuration is `{controlUiOrigin,applicationTokenEnv,bindings}`. All are required when configured. `controlUiOrigin` is one exact absolute HTTP(S) origin with no credentials/path/query/fragment; it must match the actually qualified native Control UI/device ingress. Credential reference names match `[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]{0,127}` and resolve only from the adapter process environment at the transport boundary; missing/empty/non-ASCII/CR/LF values fail without a request. No token value, digest or environment reference is exported in receipts, errors, model messages or tool arguments. `bindings` contains 1–1000 closed records `{profileId,authenticatedUserId,agentId,workspaceDir,userId,workspaceId,actionTokenEnv}`. Identity strings use the existing nonempty bounded256-byte identity rule; workspaceDir is an absolute native path, bounded2048 UTF-8 bytes. Duplicate native `(profileId,authenticatedUserId,agentId,workspaceDir)` identities or gateway `(userId,workspaceId)` mappings are refused. Gateway labels and private action credentials are configured by the local operator; request parameters cannot override them. The same gateway scope is configured on its separately running instance. From spec 0.63 the adapter MUST send the matched binding's `userId`/`workspaceId` as the section 7.6 expected scope on every gateway request it makes for that binding, including recommendation create/read, and fails closed on scope_mismatch; it never relies on the configured base URL or on which credential an environment reference resolves to for scope isolation.

Register the native RPC `relaymind.recommendations` with `operator.write`, required profile access and write session access, and declare the manifest `gatewayMethodDispatch:["authenticated-request"]`. Admit only a genuine paired Control UI device at the configured origin, with host-attested `client.internal.authenticatedControlUi===true`, a current non-model client, host-authenticated user and nonnull prepared native profile. Model-tool/synthetic callers, app-only tokens, missing device/origin/profile, invalidated clients and aborted connections/requests are refused. Derive agent/session/incarnation from the actual public handler `sessionAccessAuthority.target`, never deprecated `admittedTarget` or caller identity assertions. Resolve the workspace through the host's public runtime agent workspace resolver using its actual current configuration. The resulting native person/profile/agent/workspace must match one configured binding. The qualified personal-session deployment requires the native named-role boundary: gateway.roles has a valid default person role; every assignable role restricts sessions.others to none, excludes operator.admin and permits only the registered agent set. These are the only universal role conditions (spec 0.63 clarification): write privilege and access to the target agent are resolved for the actual caller through the host guards, never required of unrelated roles, so a narrower read-only role or disjoint registered agents per role remain valid. Reject gateway-owner, native Control UI admin admission, admin scopes, missing or broader policy; ordinary operator.write is not proof of exclusive session ownership. Use the host guards to resolve current role/grants rather than inferring a person from a role name. Broader shared-session deployments remain outside this contract. Both actual host session guards and the live client-authority predicate must pass before dispatch and after every awaited preparation step; re-resolve the workspace before dispatch. A changed profile, host instance, session incarnation, workspace, policy or connection prevents subsequent dispatch/use. Host lifetimes are never synthesized from structurally similar request data.

**Closed direct RPC.** Params are `{sessionKey,agentId?,expectedSessionId,expectedLifecycleRevision,input,idempotencyKey}`; no extra fields. Optional agentId is only a selector and, when supplied, must exactly equal the actual host-prepared target.agentId; it grants no authority. sessionKey, expectedSessionId and expectedLifecycleRevision must exactly match the host-prepared target and its nonempty lifecycle revision. `input` is one of: `{kind:recommend,request}` with the existing CreateRecommendationV1 (discovery only); `{kind:choose,recommendationId,request}` with the existing choice body; `{kind:feedback,recommendationId,request}` with the existing feedback body; `{kind:listFeedback,page:{afterId?,limit}}`; `{kind:deleteFeedback,feedbackId,expectedFeedbackRevision}`; or `{kind:resetFeedback,expectedFeedbackRevision}`. Within this native RPC only, every version/revision counter is a canonical decimal string (`0` or nonzero digit followed by digits), in the existing signed64-bit nonnegative range, converted losslessly at the existing JSON boundary. IDs, unions, page limits and idempotency keys otherwise retain existing gateway rules. Unknown nested fields are invalid. No credential, user/workspace, policy, actor override or snapshot body is accepted. Use one bounded10-second adapter operation deadline, subject to earlier host cancellation; this does not change any gateway computation/inference budget. Never retry a write silently.

RPC success is `{label,receipt_json}` for actions, with label `preference-only`, `feedback` or `privacy` matching the operation; receipt_json is a string containing the existing validated gateway JSON receipt with exact integer values. Recommendation success is `{label:recommendation,text,receipt_json}` using the evidence renderer and the already validated scoped RecommendationViewV1 serialized as lossless existing gateway JSON. The native Control UI uses the shared lossless codec and authoritative receipt identities/counters for explicit numbered choices, never parses rendered text or invents scope. Errors are bounded fixed safe codes/messages and contain no raw gateway/host exception text or credential material. These RPC envelopes are separate from unchanged gateway HTTP schemas. Choice records preference only and never loads a skill or calls a tool. Feedback remains explicit and separate from choice/task success, with existing privacy/CAS/idempotency/retention semantics.

Currentness is a dispatch/use fence, not a distributed transaction. A host guard's synchronous retained-read callback is not an atomic lease over awaited gateway HTTP. Abort while waiting for credentials prevents HTTP dispatch; after an admitted HTTP action may have committed, preserve its actual receipt or report an indeterminate transport outcome without claiming rollback or zero mutation. A retry uses the same explicit idempotency key. A lost host guard blocks every later native skill/tool dispatch, even if an earlier gateway action receipt exists. Gateway registration revocation is still checked at its own commit boundary.

**Exact Skill Library bridge.** A separate direct native RPC `relaymind.skill` uses the same registration/profile/device/origin/session/currentness mapping and closed params `{sessionKey,agentId?,expectedSessionId,expectedLifecycleRevision,recommendationId,assetId,contentHash,skillId,revision}`. All identifiers/hashes use existing bounded types. The adapter must first fetch the scoped gateway's retained recommendation and require a committed explicit asset choice matching assetId/contentHash and its offered source version. A model recommendation, a ready candidate alone, a stale/erased choice or a caller-chosen native skill name is insufficient. The choice's expiry is rechecked after every awaited preparation step and immediately before each native dispatch (spec 0.63); expiry before activation dispatches nothing, and expiry after activation but before the exact read keeps the existing partial-selection error. Once both dispatches were made while the choice was valid, the closing registration recheck does not apply expiry again: the success receipt is the historical result of those valid dispatches. Resolve the exact native skillId/revision from a private operator-owned association to that offered assetId/contentHash/source version, never fuzzy name matching or a hash relabeled as a native revision. Add `skills` to the closed recommendations configuration as an optional array of 0–1000 unique closed `{assetId,contentHash,sourceVersion,skillId,revision}` records; sourceVersion is string|null as in AssetRecordV1, all other fields use existing identity/hash bounds. This association does not assert installed ownership or availability; the native operations must prove those facts anew.

Inside the original authenticated native RPC request scope, use public privileged dispatch to `skills.library.activate` for that exact skillId/revision/session, then `skills.library.read` for that selected exact revision and session; recheck both live session guards and workspace around awaits. Validate the native selection/read receipts against the exact requested identity and require SHA-256 of the native read SKILL.md UTF-8 bytes to match the chosen contentHash. The native revision binds its full bundle independently; do not relabel that digest as a SKILL.md hash. Preserve all unrelated selected native skills. Success is the closed receipt `{skillId,revision,sessionActivation:next-turn,instructions:not_observed}`; do not export instruction content or claim task completion. Failure after activation may have started includes only fixed safe error text and details `{selectionMayHaveChanged:true,skillId,revision}`, never a false rollback claim. An altered selected revision, removed entry, foreign revision, session reset or retired authority blocks further RelayMind-managed use under the following loaded-use rules; a library head update alone does not alter a retained immutable pin. Activation success followed by read/currentness failure may have changed next-turn selection; preserve that partial native outcome honestly and do not claim it was undone.

**Loaded instruction use (spec 0.65; RM16/RM37/RM39; AC28/AC30).** `skill_loaded_use` is a separate qualification operation, never implied by `exact_skill_selection`. A qualifying observation requires the actual native `skills_read` or `read` tool execution, its tool-call ID and successful result, and the complete instruction payload from that result reaching the next actual model request after the read in the same live native session incarnation. Compare the decoded instruction text encoded as UTF-8, without NFC, whitespace or newline normalization, byte for byte with the selected immutable revision's SKILL.md and its chosen contentHash. If the native result wraps or chunks the content, retain the original result and a source-bound lossless extraction; partial reads, truncation, redaction or omitted bytes cannot qualify as a complete load. Verify the selected native skillId/revision and actual read target, not merely a matching display name. The native load path used in the trial must itself preserve that immutable binding through delivery; a manifest-verified earlier RPC cannot certify a later name-only or mutable-file read.

Evidence MUST join the retained recommendation/choice/task/asset identity, native host instance, person/profile/agent/workspace, session key/session ID/lifecycle revision, native host run ID, native tool-call/result, actual receiving provider request and RelayMind run ID, and terminal native task result. Preserve actual request/response correlation, including provider turn/idempotency records where used. The provider turn ID and RelayMind run ID are not native host run IDs; a timestamp-only join is insufficient. A catalog/path in the first prompt, an out-of-turn `skills.library.read`, a `skill.used` event alone, a supplied expected marker or a final answer without the matching receiving request proves neither complete loading nor task success. Record failed, cancelled and incomplete attempts and their actual outcomes. Qualification observers may capture private original bytes; ordinary selection receipts remain exactly `instructions:not_observed` and never export instruction content.

The **deterministic-model functional tier** runs the real pinned host, installed adapter, native loader and gateway with declared model doubles. It may establish byte delivery, run correlation, authority and negative controls and, when every listed control passes, may support a reviewed `skill_loaded_use` registry entry for that exact composition. It never enables the live-model capability tier or full AC30 acceptance. It MUST be labeled deterministic-model functional evidence, not live-model capability or full AC30 acceptance. The **live-model capability tier** uses actual model inference on the same qualified composition and records the task result and model identity/cost; it separately establishes capability under section 13. Neither tier substitutes for actual-platform, human-approval or whole-task acceptance. A live host session means an actual current host session, not a claim that its model is non-double.

**Immutable pins and invalidation.** A supported Skill Library save that advances the library head from R0 to R1 leaves R0's immutable bytes unchanged. Head change alone does not invalidate a still-current RelayMind choice explicitly mapped to R0; such a control passes only if the next managed load remains exactly R0, never silently R1. Altered/missing R0 bytes or bundle identity, a removed native library entry (even when the host retains its revision files), a changed/removed operator association, a changed/ineligible offered asset, an expired/erased/remapped choice, revoked/replaced gateway registration, reset/deleted session or retired native authority blocks further RelayMind-managed loading and delivery to a model. A current historical choice receipt is not renewed authorization. This clarifies and supersedes the earlier unqualified phrase `changed/deleted content` only for retained immutable skill revisions; catalog invalidation and current eligibility rules otherwise remain unchanged.

**Use-time fence.** Each managed use needs fresh genuine host authority for that turn under the same registered person/profile/agent/workspace and exact session incarnation, or a supported native delegation whose scope, expiry and revocation are separately qualified. An earlier RPC's closed request scope or JSON copy of its identity is not such a delegation. Before native loading, after each awaited preparation/read and immediately before instruction delivery to the next model request, check current native authority, session/lifecycle/workspace/policy, registration, retained unexpired choice, exact eligible asset/operator mapping, nonremoved library entry and immutable selected bytes. The final native boundary must prevent the managed read or model dispatch when a required check fails; qualification must observe that stop, including revocation/reset/removal while waiting. HTTP checks are not a distributed transaction: record their actual observation and host dispatch boundaries, preserve an already admitted outcome, and never claim rollback. A composition unable to enforce the required final fence remains unqualified; polling earlier state is not a substitute.

A before_prompt_build hook's tool fingerprint and hook lifetime are not person authority, do not preserve an earlier RPC dispatch scope and alone do not establish that an exception aborts the host turn. Passive tool/LLM hooks are observation only unless the exact supported cancellation/fencing path is independently demonstrated; a logged or swallowed hook error cannot qualify a stop. Already loaded conversation text cannot be erased retroactively. If unload is unavailable, stop further RelayMind-managed use in that conversation and require a fresh session with fresh authority/choice before resuming; do not claim a revoked skill was removed from past context. Preserve `managedSkillSelection:false`, native provider ownership, unrelated selections and native baseline behavior. This fence governs RelayMind-managed use only and makes no claim to revoke the host's independent pinned-session behavior.

Until actual byte delivery and every currentness/negative control above are observed and bound to a separately reviewed `skill_loaded_use` registry entry for the exact host/adapter/harness/evidence bytes, loaded-use activation remains disabled. Source inspection, deterministic happy-path delivery, exact selection/read and live-model task success alone do not enable it. Snapshot publication, runtime recommendations, MCP use and full AC30/AC35/AC37 acceptance remain separately gated.

MCP use remains disabled until the section 7.6.2 qualification passes. Runtime snapshot publication and runtime recommendation retain their earlier prohibition and require their own normative contract and qualification. No gateway MCP executor, invented requester identity or generic toolSchema execution is introduced by this subsection.

### 7.6.2 Conditional native MCP use contract (spec 0.65; RM39; AC35)

This contract defines a separate explicit native use after discovery and committed choice, not a recommendation bound implicitly to a later model turn. It is an activation condition, not a claim that a complete supported composition exists on OpenClaw 2026.10.7. The adapter MUST remain disabled for MCP use until all ten conditions below pass qualification for that exact operation. RM08–RM12, RM16, RM19, RM22, RM33 and section 7.5 remain in force. Only OpenClaw owns MCP credentials, transports, server processes, tool execution, permissions and approvals.

1. **Operation and delivery.** Reserve `relaymind.mcp` as the direct native RPC only after qualification. Its closed request is `{sessionKey,agentId?,expectedSessionId,expectedLifecycleRevision,recommendationId,assetId,contentHash,arguments,idempotencyKey}`. Identity, hash and key types/bounds are those of section 7.6.1; arguments is a JSON object, with no nonfinite numbers, duplicate keys or unknown fields outside the exact native input schema. The entire UTF-8 request is at most 512 KiB under the native RPC request cap in section 14.5. Arguments come from this explicit authenticated person action; selection alone, model/source prose and feedback never supply or approve them. Resolve identity from the retained offered asset and native mapping, not caller-supplied server/schema fields, and validate the full arguments against the exact host-exposed schema before preparation and native dispatch. Never silently add, drop or coerce an argument after binding it for approval. The 60-second adapter deadline covers all preparation and dispatch, excluding only the host approval wait. Bound that wait separately by both the native approval deadline and the operation's finite maximum approval wait, declared before the operation starts and captured in qualification evidence. Exceeding either approval bound is timeout with zero new server calls. Host cancellation and any earlier native tool deadline remain effective throughout, including approval waiting. Neither bound changes a gateway/model budget or existing direct-action deadline. No background execution or silent retry is permitted.

   The closed receipt is `{operationId,recommendationId,assetId,contentHash,outcome,code,nativeRunId,nativeCallId}`. operationId is an adapter-issued opaque identity string bounded by section 14.5; nativeRunId and nativeCallId are genuine bounded native identities or null when unobserved, never RelayMind IDs relabeled as host IDs. outcome is `not_dispatched|completed|failed|indeterminate`; code is `invalid_request|unqualified|unauthorized|choice_unavailable|binding_changed|unavailable|approval_denied|timeout|cancelled|idempotency_conflict|host_failed|transport_unknown|ok`. completed requires code=ok and an observed successful native result with nonnull nativeCallId; failed requires code=host_failed and an observed native failure with nonnull nativeCallId; indeterminate requires code=transport_unknown, or code=timeout or code=cancelled after dispatch may have begun. A timeout or cancellation before dispatch requires outcome=not_dispatched. Every remaining non-ok code requires outcome=not_dispatched. A not_dispatched receipt proves no native dispatch for this attempt. An invalid request before identities can be safely resolved returns only the native RPC error `{code:"INVALID_REQUEST",message:"invalid_request"}`. Other pre-admission refusals may likewise return only the native RPC error `{code:"INVALID_REQUEST",message:<closed lowercase code>}`, with message drawn from the listed not_dispatched codes, consistent with section 7.6.1; the RPC response is unsuccessful and has no result payload, unsafe request identity or exception text. Do not claim task success from a completed tool call. Native results stay in the host; receipts contain no arguments, tool output, credentials or private transport configuration. Reusing a key with a different canonical request in the same registered scope/session incarnation is idempotency_conflict; the same key may only recover its historical receipt after current authorization checks, never dispatch again. If dispatch history is lost or unresolved, fail closed with an indeterminate outcome rather than replaying a possible mutation. Expiry/erasure cannot make an old key a fresh use authorization.

2. **Ingress and scope.** Reuse the exact paired Control UI origin/device, host-attested person/profile, restrictive named-role/session guards, registered agent/workspace, expected gateway scope and exact session incarnation of section 7.6.1. Require operator.write, profile access and write session access for this separate RPC. The actual original authenticated request authority must remain live through the tool run. A later turn or detached request requires a genuine supported native delegation, whose acquisition, bounded scope, lifetime and revocation must be qualified separately; no such delegation is assumed here. JSON identities, shared tokens, hook contexts, synthetic clients and an earlier selection receipt are never delegated authority. A model cannot invoke this direct-person operation.

3. **Public host route.** On the pinned host the public `openclaw/plugin-sdk/gateway-method-runtime` export `dispatchGatewayMethod` is an ingress facility requiring manifest `gatewayMethodDispatch:["authenticated-request"]` and an original authenticated plugin HTTP/RPC request scope. It propagates the scoped client, cancellation and current-client authority and forbids a synthetic client. It is not by itself an exact MCP execution endpoint. Qualification MUST name and exercise the public native method actually reached from that scope and its permissions, requester/session/runtime ownership and refusal behavior; unsupported dispatch remains unqualified. `tools.invoke` resolves by name and does not carry the required expected server/original/exposed/schema/currentness tuple; name-only success cannot qualify this bridge. The existing `mcp.app.callTool` route requires a genuine active viewId bound to its requester/session/agent and has native policy/approval guards. It is eligible for an App-specific trial only when a legitimate selected-asset-to-view/runtime binding is demonstrated. Do not invent a view, assume every independent MCP tool has one, import private runtime functions, or call a materialized tool's execute from the adapter. Native execution must remain inside the host's supported route.

4. **Exact immutable binding.** Bind the retained assetId/contentHash/sourceVersion to the genuine configured server identity, original tool name, exact exposed name, complete host-normalized input schema and its recomputed RelayMind canonical schema_hash. Retain the original server schema and the pinned host normalization implementation/version in evidence; a JSON.stringify byte hash is labeled separately and cannot substitute for the canonical hash. Obtain mapping/schema from the same native execution object or a qualified immutable native binding to it. Splitting an exposed name, equal schema hashes, catalog presence or provider proposals cannot establish identity. Name collisions, renames, reordered server declarations and reserved-name suffixes must resolve to the exact intended native object or be refused. Any schema change, including a backward-compatible change with still-valid arguments, invalidates the binding and requires a fresh explicit choice/use.

5. **Native lifetime and currentness.** Qualification MUST identify the actual host-owned runtime/requester/session and native revision or retained handle, its public acquisition/release path, validity scope, and how currentness is checked at final I/O. The contract does not declare a new host revision/lease API. A local digest, catalog format version, generatedAt timestamp, cached effective inventory or runtime reference count is not an authorization revision or immutable lease. Hold the genuine native handle only for the admitted operation and release it on completion, failure, timeout or cancellation through its supported owner. Reset/deleted session or new incarnation, logout, device/profile/registration revocation, changed workspace/configuration/policy, server removal/reconfiguration/rename, disconnect/reconnect or tools/list_changed invalidates the old binding. A reconnect or refresh cannot silently renew it. Reacquisition requires a new explicit use and current choice, exact schema and genuine native authority. If the public route cannot provide this ownership and invalidation guarantee, activation stays blocked.

6. **Use and approval fences.** Check the complete authority/choice/binding/policy/deadline before preparation, after every await, before requesting native approval, after its resolution, and immediately before native server I/O, including connection/catalog-refresh awaits. The host's final execution boundary must enforce the retained exact identity/schema and canonical arguments and the current requester/session/runtime/policy. Bind the native approval to that same operation, target, full arguments, server/tool/schema and current authority. A changed argument, stale binding or changed policy cannot reuse approval. Allow-once is consumed at most once. Deny, approval replay, timeout or cancellation before dispatch makes zero new server invocations; a refusal after an earlier invocation is not evidence of zero prior effects. If dispatch already began, preserve the native result or report indeterminate and do not promise cancellation rolled back a side effect. Do not retry an uncertain mutation.

   Exercise the approval path actually applicable to the unchanged RelayMind provider/route. The pinned native Control UI resolves plugin/exec approvals through `plugin.approval.resolve` / `exec.approval.resolve`; the host CLI also provides `openclaw approvals pending` and `openclaw approvals resolve <id> <decision>` through native approval handling. Their existence does not prove that a particular MCP route creates that approval or that a shared-token CLI caller meets person authority. Approval must be presented and resolved by the host's actual applicable manager; unrelated exec approval, an annotation, injected decision or allow-always setting cannot replace it. Native App approval and configured-harness approval are not assumed equivalent to ordinary independent-tool approval.

7. **Choice and registration lifetime.** Require the scoped retained recommendation and committed explicit choice, exact still-eligible offered asset/content/source version, current private mapping and unexpired retention at every preparation/use fence. Removed/changed/erased/remapped choices/assets or revoked/replaced registration block later dispatch, including after approval.
