Revalidate gateway scope and registration through the authenticated gateway path; an earlier historical choice replay cannot renew use. Preserve existing private retention, reset and erasure semantics for linked operation/receipt/idempotency data. Record where gateway HTTP revalidation and native dispatch occurred: they are not one distributed transaction, and a host guard callback cannot freeze an awaited gateway transaction. Qualification must demonstrate the supported final fence and intervening invalidation controls; a composition that only checks before an unguarded wait stays blocked. A late invalidation prevents subsequent work but cannot undo an already admitted host effect.

8. **Runtime discovery boundary.** This narrow direct-use bridge retains discovery-only recommendation requests and null-only host_snapshot_revision fields. It does not publish runtime snapshots, infer current availability from an imported asset, narrow the native prompt/tool catalog or change managed selection v1/provider behavior. Unknown catalog availability remains unknown; exact native availability must be established at use. Enabling runtime recommendation requires a separate normative snapshot/eligibility/error-precedence contract and its own qualification.

9. **Qualification, evidence and privacy.** A closed reviewed registry must separately name `mcp_use`, exact installed host/SDK/adapter/harness bytes and reviewed evidence digests, permitted public route, transport and tool/App scope. This registry operation name is separate from the `mcp_use` lifecycle step in AssetPlatformReportV1; a platform step does not qualify a registry operation. Configuration and direct_actions/exact_skill_selection entries cannot enable it. Review original source-bound native request/result, identity/schema/argument/currentness observations, actual approval records, server effects/counters and RelayMind traces proving zero gateway execution; supplied pass flags are insufficient. Required controls include colliding names, foreign person/session, changed schema with still-valid arguments, policy/server removal, disconnect/reconnect, pending-approval reset/revocation/expiry/erasure, altered arguments, approval replay, cancellation, timeout and uncertain dispatch. A positive case must reach exactly the intended server and preserve the native/provider baseline; refused pre-dispatch controls must show zero new calls/effects. Missing evidence is unknown/not_run and blocks qualification; known violations fail. Retain originals only in authorized private evidence storage, with sanitized public receipts, existing erasure/retention and no secret hashes or instruction/tool-output leakage.

   Real native approval UI exercised by automation is **automated functional approval evidence**, explicitly labeled with the driver and actual native decisions/effects. It is never human approval. Actual human allow and deny observations, with genuine person authority and interaction provenance/timing, remain required for mutation trials/full acceptance. A real-host/stdio run in the deterministic-model functional tier, an automated UI run and a live-model capability run retain their separate labels; none closes missing whole-task, human or platform gates.

10. **Supported scope and retained gates.** A passing qualification enables only the exact reviewed operation/route/transport/tool class. Disposable stdio evidence does not qualify HTTP/SSE/OAuth; an MCP App trial does not qualify ordinary independent MCP tools. If no supported exact composition passes on the pinned host, MCP activation remains blocked in every environment. No private bypass, fork/upgrade, operator impersonation, gateway executor, substituted dataset or reduced M10 scope is authorized. Preserve unrelated native catalog/policy/provider behavior. Following invalidation, require fresh authority and a fresh explicit managed use; use a fresh session where already delivered tool/instruction text cannot be unloaded. Actual Apple M1 8 GiB/macOS trial, named native Windows/full platform requirements, independent corpus/whole-task evidence and all AC35/AC37 gates remain as specified in section 12.6.

## 8 Internal data and event contract

The following objects define required fields. Implementations MUST publish equivalent versioned JSON Schemas and validate them at boundaries; examples are illustrative data, not execution evidence.

| Object | Required fields and constraints |
| --- | --- |
| RouteDecision | schema_version=1; route enum; skill_id or null; language en or zh; reason_code; clarification or null. Unknown fields rejected. |
| ToolProposal | proposal_id; actor local, external, or gateway (spec 0.31: a prefetch call); tool_name; arguments object; skill_version; run_state_version. |
| ToolResult | operation_id; tool_name; status succeeded, failed, or unknown_outcome; validated data or null; error or null; observed_at; duration_ms; source_ref; truncated boolean. |
| HandoffEnvelope | schema_version=1; run_id; original_messages (role and text, in order); skill (name, version, content_hash) or null; completed_operations (operation_id, tool_name, arguments, status); evidence (for each completed operation: operation_id, source_ref, truncated, and the ToolResult); local_proposals (each labeled as a proposal); pending_work; allowed_tools; reason_code (an RM04 reason code); remaining_budgets; language en or zh. Unknown fields rejected. |
| handoff.started payload | reason_code (the RM04 reason for the escalation); model (the external model); skill (name, version, content_hash) or null; operation_ids (the completed operations the envelope carries); allowed_tools; request (size, limit, and method, tokenizer or utf8_bytes, of the measured external request); degraded_mode (true) and local_failure (a non-blank cause) only when the local model was unavailable and the operator policy permitted degraded mode (section 11); budget (the name of the section 11 limit, such as active_seconds, that stopped the local stage) only when a budget stop caused the handoff (spec 0.48). |
| TraceEvent | schema_version=1; run_id; seq; timestamp; elapsed_ms; event_type; actor; payload. |
| run.accepted payload | requested_execution_mode (the header or native value, or null when absent); effective_execution_mode (the mode applied after defaults and policy); model_alias (the virtual alias from the chat request's model field, relaymind or relaymind-client-tools, or null for a native run (section 7.4)); budgets (the section 11 limits in force, RM03); skill_roots (the configured roots in order); skills_offered (array of name, version, and content_hash for every skill in the catalog offered to the router, or in external_only to the external model (section 7.4), empty for the client-owned alias).; original_messages (spec 0.20: the request's system, developer, user, and assistant text messages as role and content, in order, redacted by secret field name per RM12; it is the caller's own text, written when the run is accepted and before any secret value of the run is known, so a value typed in the request text is stored as typed, with the run's seven-day retention (section 5); the demo UI shows the original prompt from it, section 9); answer_options (spec 0.22: an object of the answer options the gateway applies to the answer generations of the run, with only the keys that apply: max_completion_tokens, the effective token budget (a legacy max_tokens is recorded under this name, section 7.1), temperature, and top_p; a key is absent when the run has no such option, so a run without options records {}, and so does a native run, which accepts none (section 7.4); a default-valued option that the adapters do not accept is not applied and so not recorded; the recorded values are what shaped the answer, which lets a report show its sampling settings from the trace, section 12.2; a run stored before spec 0.22 has no answer_options, which means the options were not recorded, not that there were none; the demo UI shows them, section 9); routing_cache (spec 0.26: optional boolean, true when the routing-result cache was enabled for the run, section 10; absent or false means the run is a cache-off baseline run, and a run stored before spec 0.26 has none). |
| run.accepted additions (spec 0.31; 0.41) | answer_stream_effective (buffered or live, selected before acceptance; answer_stream remains the configured policy, section 7.3.1); local_model (the configured local model_id, or null when none) with local_model_status (ready, unavailable, unsuitable, or absent) and local_model_reason (the measured cause, or null; RM26); placement_policy (local_first, device_aware, or expected_wait, RM27), with placement.margin, placement.probe_after_seconds, and selection.timeout_seconds recorded inside budgets next to the section 11 limits, under those dotted names (RM03; spec 0.36); calls_per_generation (one or many), prefetch (boolean), and early_headers (boolean), the performance options in force; answer_pass and answer_stream (the performance options in force, RM31, RM32); selection (the configured selector chain, in order, RM28), example_match_score and example_match_margin (the effective thresholds, section 14.1); prefetch_declarations (an object keyed by offered skill ID, whose values are arrays of declared prefetch calls, including empty arrays for skills with none, RM29). A run stored before spec 0.31 has none of them, which means they were not recorded. |
| route.selected payload | decision (the RouteDecision); mechanism (native_function_calling, routing_cache, bypass, execution_mode, degraded_mode, clarification_resume, client_owned, or, from spec 0.31, a selector's mechanism label, or, from spec 0.36, selector_chain for the gateway's decision when the selector chain chose nothing); model, call_id, input_tokens, and model_language for a routing generation; routing_cache hit or miss when the cache is enabled; and from spec 0.31: selector (name, version, mechanism, elapsed_ms, and attempts, one per selector tried, each with its name and outcome: selected, none, timeout, error, inadmissible, or not_placed when the placement policy did not run a model-based selector's generation (RM27); RM28; spec 0.36: name, version, and mechanism are null and elapsed_ms is the whole chain's when the chain chose nothing, and that route.selected is the gateway's own decision with mechanism selector_chain), tools_narrowed (the selector's narrowing intersected with the skill's allowlist, in allowlist order, when the selector narrowed; spec 0.36), a route.selected of model_router keeps the local model's actor and the fields of a routing generation, and the choice of any other selector has actor gateway, so it is not a model action (section 12.1), and placement (the model the routing generation ran on, or none, with the reason, predicted_ms and its inputs, and deadline_ms; RM27); and from spec 0.37, local_model_status (absent, unavailable, or unsuitable) on every route.selected of an auto run in managed external execution (RM26): the selector's choice, the gateway's clarification, the chain's failure, a clarification resume, and the client-owned alias's decision. |
| model.started and model.finished additions (spec 0.31, revised in 0.33) | model.started: placement (role, reason, predicted_ms, deadline_ms; RM27) and request_options (the operator-declared provider options sent, section 6), present only when at least one option is declared, so a local call and an external call without declared options both leave it out. model.finished: first_token_ms (integer milliseconds from the request start on the run's monotonic clock, null when no token arrived); inside usage (null when the source returned no usage), next to input_tokens and output_tokens, cached_tokens and reasoning_tokens, each null when the source reported none and never a defaulted 0; predicted_ms, deadline_ms, and deadline_missed. |
| tool.proposed additions (spec 0.31) | mechanism: model (a model's proposal, the default), prefetch (RM29), or the reserved legacy workflow value with step, the step ID (retired RM30; no package runner emits it); a gateway-made proposal has actor gateway and model null. tool.started and tool.finished of a gateway-made call carry the same mechanism (prefetch or workflow) and model null; a null model is valid only with actor gateway and one of those mechanisms, so a model-made call that lost its model is still refused (RM22). |
| Retired workflow package events | Spec 0.47 removes the unimplemented requirement for workflow.started, workflow.step.started, workflow.step.finished, and workflow_steps (retired RM30). The fixed parallel workflow summary below remains required. Reserved workflow tool-mechanism fields in existing schemas remain readable; they do not establish package execution support. |
| timing sample | Spec 0.38 clarifies RM27: schema_version=1; model; actor (local or external); purpose; kind (run, warm_up, or probe, replacing the earlier proposed warm_up flag); input_tokens, cached_tokens, first_token_ms, and output_tokens (each a non-negative integer or null when unreported); duration_ms (non-negative integer); succeeded (boolean); run_id (nullable link to a run; warm-up and probe have none); recorded_at (UTC timestamp). Kept in the run store, never a trace event. Call identity may be retained internally to prevent duplicate samples. |
| clarification.required payload | question (the clarification text in the run's language); language; reason_code; skill_id (the clarify decision's skill_id: the skill whose required identifier the gateway found absent, or null for a clarification the model requested, per the cross-field rules below); missing (a non-blank name of the input the run waits for: the argument name of the absent required identifier when skill_id is set, or skill_selection when skill_id is null, because the model requested clarification before any skill was chosen and the gateway knows no narrower input); waiting_seconds (the section 11 waiting limit in force). |
| confirmation.required payload | approval_id; tool_name; proposal_id; arguments (canonical, redacted per RM12); target (the value of the tool's confirmation target argument); state_version (the run version the approval is bound to); expires_at (UTC time, five minutes after issue); model (the proposing model). Never the token or its hash. |
| clarification.answered payload | input (the resume text, redacted per RM12); missing (copied from the clarification.required it answers); waited_ms (monotonic time from clarification.required to the accepted resume, RM25). |
| run.completed payload | final_answer_model (RM16); model_calls (every actual model call: call_id, model, actor, purpose; RM16); external_calls (how many of those calls went to an external model, RM01); active_ms and waiting_ms (non-negative integers, milliseconds from the monotonic clock, RM25: active execution time and the sum of the recorded waits, reported separately, section 12.1). |
| run.completed additions (spec 0.32, completing RM31) | answer_pass_applied (draft or final, the actual pass that produced the answer, including final when a draft guard required it). Required when the answer uses RM31; absent when no RM31 answer pass applies, such as a client-owned completion. Older events without the field mean the pass was not recorded; readers MUST NOT infer it from the configured answer_pass. |
| run.cancelled payload | active_ms and waiting_ms as in run.completed; model_calls and external_calls when the run made model calls; reason (user_denied for a denied confirmation, cancel_requested for a native cancel request) and approval_id (when a confirmation was pending). |
| run.failed payload | error_code (the terminal error code, section 11); reason_code (the RM04 reason code when the failure has one, for example needs_external or missing_input); model_calls, external_calls, active_ms, and waiting_ms as in run.completed. A run that restart recovery ends has error_code interrupted, interrupted_state, and settled_operations (an array of operation_id and status; section 5). |
| cache.hit payload | Spec 0.26 (section 10): originating_version (the skill version under which the cache entry was stored: the version of the skill the cached route chose); originating_run_id (the run whose routing generation produced the entry); skill (name, version, content_hash of the chosen skill, which equal the current registry entry, because an entry for another hash is never a hit); age_ms (non-negative integer, monotonic time since the entry was stored); key (the SHA-256 digest of the cache key, never the key's parts, because they hold the request text). Actor gateway. The gateway writes every field; only originating_version is required by the schema, and a field that is present must have the type given here. |
| Workflow branch fields | Spec 0.25: model.started, model.finished, tool.proposed, tool.started, and tool.finished carry an optional branch (local_health, external_checklist, or synthesis) in a run that started the parallel workflow (section 4.4). handoff.started at the join carries workflow, the workflow ID. |
| workflow summary | Spec 0.25: the optional workflow object of run.completed, run.failed, and run.cancelled for a run that started the workflow: workflow_id; branch_wall_ms (monotonic time of parallel_running, non-negative integer); local_health_ms and external_checklist_ms (monotonic time from the start of each branch to its end, non-negative integers); extra_call_ms (the summed duration_ms of the synthesis model calls, or null when no synthesis call ran); abandoned_calls (the call_ids of local_health and external_checklist calls that ended cancelled; a synthesis call is never abandoned). Restart recovery writes no workflow object (section 4.4). |

Timing storage and estimates (spec 0.38, RM17, RM25, RM27). The gateway records one sample for every actual generation, including failed and cancelled calls, across managed, client-owned, and fixed-workflow execution. Counts come from reported usage; an unreported count stays null, including input tokens on a failed or warm-up call. A selector's self-report is not an actual gateway call or a timing sample. Warm-up and probe samples have no user run and do not affect user budgets. Samples follow the store's run-retention duration (seven days by default); explicit run deletion removes linked samples. At most 1,000 samples per model and purpose remain, newest by recorded_at and then insertion order. Estimate queries ignore expired samples even before maintenance purges them.

Rejected optional measurements (spec 0.38.1, RM14, RM16, RM25, RM27). When an actual generation reports cached_tokens greater than its known input_tokens, the cached count is unavailable: the gateway records cached_tokens null in both model.finished usage and the timing sample (and in a warm-up result), retaining the valid input/output counts and actual monotonic timings. Null means unavailable, whether unreported or rejected, never a fabricated zero. The gateway emits exactly one warning for that observation through the standard logger with the fixed diagnostic `invalid_cached_tokens: cached_tokens exceeds input_tokens; recorded as unknown`. The warning contains no provider text, raw values, model/run identifiers or credentials. This normalization applies to managed, client-owned, fixed-workflow, warm-up and probe calls; it preserves execution success, failure, timeout and cancellation rather than allowing optional measurement validation to replace them. Strict stored/imported ModelTiming validation still rejects contradictory counts; seed files are not normalized. Other measurement constraints and database errors are not suppressed.

Estimates group samples by actual actor, model, and purpose, so local and external calls cannot mix even when model names match. They use successful samples only. Cached tokens above zero identify a warm prefix, zero identifies a cold prefix, and null identifies neither. Warm and cold effective prefill rates are separate medians of input_tokens / first_token_ms, in tokens per millisecond, using positive known terms. Effective decode rate is the median of output_tokens / (duration_ms - first_token_ms), again using positive known terms. These rates are prediction inputs, not claims of exact internal model throughput. Missing usable samples give an unavailable rate, never a fabricated constant. Expected output and external call duration are medians of the relevant successful observed counts and durations; zero reported output remains a measurement, while null is omitted from that median. A purpose's output cap is the expected-output fallback, not a fallback duration. Unknown prefix measurements may contribute duration/output medians but never silently become cold samples.

Skill outcomes are materialized once when a run becomes terminal, without scanning complete event histories when placement reads them. Local success counts a selected skill completed without external handoff after at least one non-routing local generation. Local escalation counts an actual handoff after such an attempt, even if external execution later fails or is cancelled, or an auto run that ends needs_external after that attempt. Routing-only runs, warm-up/probes, unplaced skills, pure cancellations, and local_only failures do not enter that completed-versus-escalated denominator. A parallel workflow run (section 4.4) is not a local placement (spec 0.38.1): its local branch generation is not a local attempt, and its planned synthesis join (handoff.started with a workflow) is not an escalation, so that run enters neither count, even when its local branch fails; its draft and synthesis still count as the skill's actual external generations when the run completes. Local outcomes group by configured local model and selected skill. Expected external generation counts group by the actual external model and selected skill, use completed runs with external calls, and do not mix different external models or unselected-skill catalog handoffs. Run deletion and retention remove the run's outcome contribution. The local success rate and median external generation count are unavailable when their respective sample sets are empty; expected-wait applies only with the required measurements and uses at least one expected external generation.

RouteDecision fields MUST also satisfy these cross-field rules:

- route=clarify requires a non-blank clarification. Every other route requires clarification=null.
- A non-null skill_id MUST name a skill in the catalog offered to the router for this run (skills_offered in the run.accepted payload).
- In managed execution, route=local requires a non-null skill_id. route=parallel requires a non-null skill_id whose registry manifest declares a parallel-workflow ID that operator configuration enables, and an effective execution mode that admits the workflow (section 4.4, RM15); in local_only and external_only route=parallel is invalid. route=external allows skill_id null; when set, it carries the selected skill into the handoff (RM13). route=clarify carries a skill_id only when the gateway found a required identifier of that skill absent (RM05); a clarification the model requests carries skill_id null, and its clarification.required names skill_selection as the missing input.
- In client-owned mode the offered catalog is empty, so skill_id MUST be null; route=parallel is invalid (section 7.2).
- reason_code follows the route: route=local carries simple_skill; route=clarify carries missing_input; route=parallel carries parallel_workflow; route=external carries the reason for leaving the local path: complex_skill, parallel_workflow, context_limit, execution_mode, capability_missing, or, from spec 0.31, no_local_model, local_slow, or selection_failed; a skill selected without a local model routes external with its skill_id and reason no_local_model (RM26), and a skill whose stage the placement policy hands off routes external with its skill_id and reason local_slow (RM27). local_budget, invalid_output, and tool_failure are escalation reasons recorded in the trace (RM04), not routing decisions.
- language: the first RouteDecision of a run fixes the run's language; every later decision of that run carries the run's language, which the gateway sets before validation (section 5), so the validator never sees a conflicting value.
- A decision the gateway produces itself, the oversized-input bypass (section 4.1), the external_only decision (section 7.4), and the external route of a selected parallel skill whose workflow is not admitted (section 4.4), follows the same rules.

A decision that breaks any rule is invalid output (reason code invalid_output): it never triggers a tool and is handled under RM03/RM04. The RouteDecision validator at the routing boundary owns these rules and checks every decision before route.selected is recorded; the skills_offered membership check reads the run.accepted payload, whose shape the run.accepted validator guarantees. The validator checks the decision it is given; the language rule holds because the gateway sets the language of every later decision (section 5), so a resumed routing generation cannot flip it.

Reason codes include simple_skill, complex_skill, missing_input, local_budget, invalid_output, tool_failure, context_limit, capability_missing, parallel_workflow, and execution_mode (a route fixed by the effective execution mode, section 7.4). Spec 0.31 adds local_slow (a local generation the placement policy predicted over its limit, or that missed its deadline, RM27), no_local_model (a gateway without a usable local model: absent, unavailable, or unsuitable, RM26), and selection_failed (a selector chain that chose nothing, RM28) to the reason_code enum of RouteDecision and HandoffEnvelope under the same schema_version, an additive change like the fields spec 0.26 added to run.accepted; a decision stored earlier never carries them. They are operational labels, not generated chains of thought.

answer.delta additionally carries call_id (nonblank string) for progressively generated output (spec 0.41, section 7.3.1). Historical and gateway-rendered output may omit it; readers never invent a generation identity.

**RM22** Required events are run.accepted, route.selected, skill.loaded, model.started, model.finished, tool.proposed, tool.started, tool.finished, clarification.required, clarification.answered, confirmation.required, handoff.started, answer.delta, run.completed, run.failed, and run.cancelled. clarification.answered follows every resume that supplies input text and records the input and the waiting time (section 5); run.completed and run.failed report active time and waiting time separately as active_ms and waiting_ms (table above, section 12.1); run.cancelled carries the same timing fields. Cached routes, when enabled, emit cache.hit with the originating version (payload in the table above), immediately before the route.selected that uses the cached skill choice; that route.selected has actor gateway and mechanism routing_cache, and carries no model, call_id, or token count, because no routing generation ran. Every tool event includes its operation or proposal ID and originating model, or actor gateway with model null for a prefetch call (spec 0.31). run.accepted is the first event of a run; its payload is defined in the table above.

Example event:

```json
{
  "schema_version": 1,
  "run_id": "run_demo_001",
  "seq": 7,
  "timestamp": "2026-10-20T03:00:00Z",
  "elapsed_ms": 420,
  "event_type": "tool.proposed",
  "actor": "local",
  "payload": {
    "proposal_id": "proposal_01",
    "tool_name": "get_service_health",
    "arguments": {"service": "staging-api"},
    "skill_id": "check-service-health"
  }
}
```

The 420 ms example is a schema illustration, not a measured latency. Events containing tool results MUST be redacted before persistence and rendering. Redaction is by configured secret field name, at any depth. A value (a string or a number) found under a secret field is also replaced wherever the same whole token is repeated in the data or error message of that result (for example a log message), and in later tool results, model text, proposed tool-call arguments, and resume input of the run, so a secret that sits in a log line next to its field, or that a model copies, is not stored or shown. The result envelope fields (tool name, status, timestamps, source reference) are not free text and are never scrubbed. Redaction runs before the first write, and the model sees only the redacted result. Tool output stays data: text in it, such as an instruction to call another tool or to print a credential, never changes the allowed tools, the caller permissions, or an approval (RM08, RM11, RM12, AC06). Large results use artifact references with explicit size and truncation metadata; a truncated result cannot silently satisfy a skill that requires the complete result.

### 8.1 M10 catalog, recommendation, job and feedback evidence (spec 0.51)

RM22's existing TraceEvent remains schema_version 1 and unchanged. New objects below have their own versioned schemas; no fake run/model-call IDs are introduced. Paths in this index are **planned files**, created by the corresponding product issues after the 0.51 merge, not shipped schemas or links.

| Object | Planned path | Contract |
| --- | --- | --- |
| EmbeddingManifestV1 | `src/relaymind/assets/schemas/embedding_manifest.v1.json` | Closed bounded local verified artifact/runtime/device contract, section 6.1. |
| AssetHybridConfigV1 | `src/relaymind/assets/schemas/asset_hybrid_config.v1.json` | Closed deterministic representation/index/fusion/confidence contract, section 14.5.1. |
| AssetAdaptationConfigV1 | `src/relaymind/assets/schemas/asset_adaptation_config.v1.json` | Closed qualified similarity and bounded ranking contract, section 14.5.2. |
| AssetMetadataImportV1, AssetMetadataEntryV1, AssetMetadataRecordV1 | `src/relaymind/assets/schemas/asset_metadata_import.v1.json` | Closed independent input and original-byte integrity/source/removal rules, section 4.6.1; never a published snapshot or live host envelope. |
| AssetRecordV1, AssetCatalogSnapshotV1 | `src/relaymind/store/schemas/asset_record.v1.json`, `asset_catalog_snapshot.v1.json` | Section 4.6; snapshot below. |
| PreparationRequestV1, PreparationResponseV1 | `src/relaymind/selection/schemas/asset_preparation_request.v1.json`, `asset_preparation_response.v1.json` | Optional explicit loopback index preparation; section 12.6.4. No change to selection v2. |
| SelectionRequestV2, SelectionResultV2 | `src/relaymind/selection/schemas/selection_request.v2.json`, `selection_result.v2.json` | Section 4.6; recommendation consumer only. |
| RecommendationViewV1, RecommendationResultV1 | `src/relaymind/store/schemas/recommendation_view.v1.json`, `recommendation_result.v1.json` | Sections 7.6 and this section. |
| ReadinessV1 | `src/relaymind/store/schemas/recommendation_readiness.v1.json` | Section 7.6. |
| ActionEvidenceV1 | `src/relaymind/store/schemas/action_evidence.v1.json` | This section; used by RecommendationViewV1.selected_action and the chosen/cancelled events. |
| RecommendationEventV1, IndexJobEventV1 | `src/relaymind/store/schemas/recommendation_event.v1.json`, `index_job_event.v1.json` | Separate ordered streams below. |
| AssetCatalogSetV1, AssetCasesV1, AssetLabelsV1, AssetLockV1 | `src/relaymind/evaluation/schemas/asset_catalog_set.v1.json`, `asset_cases.v1.json`, `asset_labels.v1.json`, `asset_lock.v1.json` | Frozen retrieval input, raw double annotation, adjudication and lock closure; section 12.6.1. |
| AssetDiagnosticV1 | `src/relaymind/evaluation/schemas/asset_diagnostic.v1.json` | Source-grounded development benchmark cases and reference judgments; section 12.6.3. Separate from independent qualification. |
| AssetBenchmarkConfigV1, AssetBenchmarkReportV1, AssetBenchmarkComparisonV1 | `src/relaymind/evaluation/schemas/asset_benchmark_config.v1.json`, `asset_benchmark_report.v1.json`, `asset_benchmark_comparison.v1.json` | Closed retrieval run/config/report and compatible paired comparison; section 12.6.2. |
| AssetEpisodesV1, AssetAdaptationReportV1, AssetHostTasksV1, AssetHostReportV1, AssetPlatformReportV1 | `src/relaymind/evaluation/schemas/asset_episodes.v1.json`, `asset_adaptation_report.v1.json`, `asset_host_tasks.v1.json`, `asset_host_report.v1.json`, `asset_platform_report.v1.json` | Planned episode/whole-host/platform exports and their nested freeze/companion definitions; section 12.6.5. No host activation. |
| FeedbackRecordV1, FeedbackAuditV1 | `src/relaymind/store/schemas/feedback_record.v1.json`, `feedback_audit.v1.json` | Scoped records/erasure audit below. |

All filename-only entries in the same cell share its preceding directory. Core schema fields are closed under section 4.6 conventions. All duration/elapsed fields are finite nonnegative milliseconds unless explicitly nullable; observed counts are nonnegative integers. An identity is `{name,version}`, with bounded nonempty strings. `CostV1` is `{input_tokens,output_tokens,cached_tokens,reasoning_tokens,calls,cpu_ms,peak_bytes}`; each count is a nonnegative integer or null, cpu_ms finite nonnegative or null. Null means unknown, never zero. A cost observation is `{provenance:observed|reported,value:CostV1}`. Resource measurements state coverage; reported selector cost is never summed as if gateway-observed inference.

`AssetCatalogSnapshotV1` required fields: schema_version:1, catalog_revision, published_at, sources, assets. Sources is an ordered unique-source array of closed `{source_id,source_version,content_hash,captured_at,last_success_at,stale,complete,accepted_count,rejected_count,exclusions}`; source_version, content_hash, captured_at and last_success_at nullable where never observed, exclusions is an array of `{asset_id:string|null,reason}` with bounded identity/reason strings. complete is boolean and cannot be true for incomplete acquisition or excluded coverage. Assets is an array of AssetRecordV1 with unique IDs. Source hashes describe their captured generation, not host availability. Snapshot/index publications are atomic under section 14.5.

`SelectorAttemptV1` required fields: attempt (positive integer), selector identity, mechanism, outcome:ranked|no_match|not_ready|unavailable|stale|invalid|timeout|cancelled, reason:string|null, observed_ms, reported_ms|null, index_revision|null, cost_observations (array), result:SelectionResultV2|null. A result is nonnull only when a syntactically valid selector result exists; subsequent admission can still reject it. Dispatch-order attempt numbers are consecutive; fixed safe reason codes never contain exception/credential text. `EmbeddingObservationV1` has model identity, artifact_hash, representation_revision, device, input_bytes, input_tokens|null, duration_ms, cost_observations and outcome:succeeded|failed|cancelled|not_ready. It dates actual query/background inference separately from selector elapsed claims; no inference occurred when not_ready.

`RecommendationResultV1` required fields: schema_version:1, decision:confident|choice_required|no_match|empty_catalog, catalog_revision, host_snapshot_revision:null, attempts, primary_attempt:integer|null, base_candidates, base_decision, final_candidates, final_decision, fallback_reason:string|null, adaptation, admission, observed_ms, embeddings, host_run_ids. Base output is the usable primary or gateway lexical floor before adaptation; primary_attempt is the 0-based array index in attempts of the configured primary's attempt (so attempts[primary_attempt].attempt is that attempt's 1-based dispatch number), never the lexical floor relabeled. Candidate arrays use section 4.6 candidate shapes; base_decision/final_decision use the decision enum and final_decision equals decision. Empty catalog has no attempts or candidates, null primary_attempt and matching empty_catalog decisions; no_match has no final candidates. `adaptation` is null when not applied: disabled, empty_catalog, or no usable configured-primary output (including a usable gateway lexical floor). Otherwise it is the existing `{identity,feedback_revision,changed:boolean}`; its input/output are base_candidates/base_decision and final_candidates/final_decision. Valid configured-primary no_match and a no-history no-op stage may record changed=false without inference. An enabled applicable stage's failure never becomes a ready result with adaptation=null. `admission` is `{outcome:accepted,rechecked_catalog_revision,checked_at}` for a ready result; failed admission is recorded in attempts/failure evidence and never called accepted. `embeddings` contains EmbeddingObservationV1; `host_run_ids` is an array reserved for real captured host inference correlations; it is [] in the 0.51 core, which has no enabled host correlation adapter. A later adapter revision must define its write boundary; it never implies host execution. observed_ms is service elapsed, independent of summed attempt claims. All ordered attempts/base/final output/fallback/adaptation are retained within privacy/retention limits. Feedback revision belongs to service/adaptation evidence, never to the third-party primary request.

`ActionEvidenceV1` required fields are adapter_id, scope:{user_id,workspace_id}, recommendation_id, task_id, choice (section 7.6 union), catalog_revision, host_snapshot_revision:null, recorded_at, state_version. Asset choice binds asset_id and content_hash. The gateway checks them against that candidate's offered record in the stored result, which keeps its source_version; ActionEvidenceV1 has no separate source_version field. This authenticates the registered channel, not a person or execution grant. All three action kinds persist this evidence with one matching lifecycle event and idempotency receipt, no feedback event/row/revision change.

`RecommendationEventV1` required fields: schema_version:1, recommendation_id, seq, timestamp, elapsed_ms, event_type, actor:gateway|selector|adapter, erased:boolean, payload. Sequence starts at 1 and increases per object in the state/event transaction; elapsed_ms is monotonic active service time, excluding later user waits, which timestamps preserve. Payload is null only after applicable privacy erasure and erased=true; all original sequence/envelope identity remains. When feedback is deleted or reset, each matching feedback.recorded event is erased this way (its feedback_id goes with the payload), and feedback.deleted carries only feedback_id_digest. Otherwise erased=false and the event-specific closed payload is:

| Event type | Required payload |
| --- | --- |
| accepted | configured_primary:{name,version}, task_id, redacted task_text, ordered redacted conversation, trusted scope, budgets:RecommendationBudgetsV1, config_digest, catalog_revision, host_snapshot_revision:null, requested_kinds, selection_scope, language_tags, code_switched |
| selector.started | attempt, selector identity, mechanism, remaining_ms |
| selector.finished | attempt_record:SelectorAttemptV1, embeddings array |
| adaptation.applied | adaptation (nonnull shape above) |
| ready | state_version, result:RecommendationResultV1, ready_at, choice_expires_at |
| failed | state_version, code (section 11.1), reason:interrupted\|deadline\|unavailable\|internal\|corrupt_catalog, active_ms, attempts array |
| cancelled | Compute cancellation: state_version, origin:gateway, reason:disconnect\|shutdown, action:null, active_ms, attempts array. User choice: state_version, origin:direct_user, reason:user_cancel, action:ActionEvidenceV1, active_ms, attempts array. |
| chosen | state_version, action:ActionEvidenceV1 (asset or none) |
| expired | state_version, choice_expires_at |
| feedback.recorded | feedback_id, feedback_revision |
| feedback.deleted | feedback_id_digest, feedback_revision |

`RecommendationBudgetsV1` is closed: total_ms, primary_ms, candidate_limit, display_limit, max_catalog_records, max_catalog_bytes, max_result_bytes, max_create_bytes, max_task_bytes, max_conversation_entries, max_conversation_bytes, choice_seconds, retention_days, fallback_enabled. Values map directly to section 14.5 (fallback_enabled boolean, others positive integers; primary_ms≤total_ms). `IndexJobBudgetsV1` is closed: max_requests, max_records, max_received_bytes, max_asset_bytes, max_metadata_bytes, read_buffer_bytes, exchange_seconds, job_seconds, workers, cpu_batch_ms, background_gpu_with_llama (false). Values map directly to section 14.5. For accepted, configured_primary is the actual configured built-in or HTTP implementation name/version persisted at acceptance, and budgets is RecommendationBudgetsV1; queued jobs carry IndexJobBudgetsV1; config_digest hashes the redacted effective configuration. Lifecycle events occur in transition order; selector events cannot occur after terminal commit. Retained-record feedback/audit may follow terminal and never changes lifecycle state/version. feedback.recorded occurs only for explicit feedback creation, never choice. Scope reset is never a fabricated recommendation event. Cancelling a ready record retains previously recorded ready/result evidence; failed/cancelled retains prior attempts rather than erasing failure cost.

Query `EmbeddingObservationV1.representation_revision` is exactly `asset_fields.v1` for hybrid-primary embedding work and `task_text.v1` for adaptation similarity work. This fixed representation-to-stage mapping distinguishes stages even when they share the same model/artifact/device. Complete semantic digests bind settings separately; do not append a digest to these representation labels, forge a model identity, or add a stage field. Background document embeddings stay exclusively in IndexJobEventV1 batch evidence and are not copied into a recommendation's costs.

For each actually invoked query/similarity batch, capture the input byte count, exact token count when known, measured monotonic duration, actual available usage/cost and outcome. A local embedding call is inference. On failed/cancelled inference, retain available observations; unavailable returned token/resource counts stay null. not_ready means no inference invocation occurred and must not hide an already invoked failure. Existing embedding outcomes remain succeeded|failed|cancelled|not_ready; an owned embedding deadline uses failed (or cancelled when cancellation wins), never a new timeout enum. Do not fabricate observations for no-match/no-history work that did not invoke a backend. A pre-invocation busy refusal may record not_ready with truthful zero calls; unknown costs remain null.

The coordinator may defer primary `selector.finished` until applicable post-primary observations are available. Keep the primary's valid result, outcome, reported_ms and reported cost unchanged; adaptation failure is not a primary transport/selector failure. Its attempt observed_ms covers dispatch through the coordinator's finish observation, including that deferred interval; it is not a claim of primary inference duration. Embedding durations identify actual stage inference separately. Primary cost observations cover primary work only; adaptation cost appears separately in its embedding observations and is never added to primary reported usage. For aggregate inference accounting, primary embedding observations explain the primary's inference cost rather than being counted again; add adaptation's `task_text.v1` observed inference once. Observed and reported versions of the same usage are alternatives with coverage, never summed together. Lexical primary may truthfully retain zero primary inference while adaptation has nonzero local inference.

One successful applicable stage records, in order, `selector.finished` with the immutable primary attempt and the available primary/adaptation query observations, then `adaptation.applied`, then `ready`. Ready's existing embeddings array contains the same query observations in invocation order. On failure/cancellation after valid primary output, record its available finish evidence **before** the existing failed/cancelled event, without adaptation.applied for an incomplete stage. Failed/cancelled payloads retain attempts; embeddings are retained in the preceding selector.finished event, because those terminal payloads have no embeddings field. The store owner must make available observation flush and terminal fencing atomic/ordered so cancellation cannot terminalize first and lose already observed cost or relabel a completed primary cancelled. A terminal path may flush evidence without authorizing further work after deadline/cancellation. No selector/adaptation event may appear after terminal commit. A backend result arriving only after fencing is discarded; late private/cache writes are forbidden and unobserved usage is not invented. Native cleanup/resource coverage remains an actual qualification obligation.

`IndexJobEventV1` required fields: schema_version:1, job_id, seq, timestamp, event_type, payload. Sequence is per job. Jobs are separate from execution runs and have queued→started→published|failed|cancelled; batch.finished repeats only while started. Closed payloads are:

| Event type | Required payload |
| --- | --- |
| queued | source_id:string\|null, catalog_revision, config_digest, job_kind:lexical\|embedding\|refresh, budgets (effective job limits of section 14.5) |
| started | catalog_revision, index_revision:string\|null |
| batch.finished | batch (positive integer), accepted_count, rejected_count, received_bytes, duration_ms, embeddings array |
| published | catalog_revision, index_revision:string\|null, accepted_count, rejected_count, duration_ms |
| failed | reason:interrupted\|deadline\|size_limit\|record_limit\|request_limit\|invalid_data\|source_unavailable\|index_error, duration_ms, retained_catalog_revision, received_bytes, accepted_count, rejected_count |
| cancelled | reason:shutdown\|operator, duration_ms, retained_catalog_revision |

Durations/counts are nonnegative; nullable source identifies a derived-only rebuild. Background inference occurs only in embeddings observations attached to job batches; query embeddings belong to recommendation attempts. Both record model artifact/device/input/usage or unknown. Restart marks in-flight jobs interrupted before deriving replacement work; it does not fabricate a successful completion or redownload all originals.

`FeedbackRecordV1` fields: schema_version:1, feedback_id (generated UUID), recommendation_id, task_id, scope:{user_id,workspace_id}, adapter_id, event_id, recorded_at, retain_until, feedback_revision, feedback (section 7.6 union), redacted_task_text, asset_content_hash:string|null, configured_primary:{name,version}, catalog_revision. configured_primary is copied from the accepted effective built-in/HTTP primary name/version, never from the configuration current at feedback submission, and not inferred from calls. On lexical fallback it remains the configured primary; only linked actual attempts/base result identify the usable producer. On empty_catalog it remains configuration provenance with no dispatched producer. Targetless usefulness has null asset hash; targeted feedback matches the exact union hash. `FeedbackAuditV1` fields: schema_version:1, audit_id, scope, adapter_id, timestamp, operation:delete|reset|retention, feedback_revision, removed_count, idempotency_digest:string|null. It contains no request/asset text. event_id deduplication is scoped to registered adapter and canonical feedback payload including parent/expected revision; changed reuse conflicts even with a different Idempotency-Key. Deletion/reset retains only content-free receipt/tombstone data under section 5.1.

Persist all evidence after configured secret-field redaction, including matching discovered secret values, before hashes of redacted requests or derived private representations. Original source-byte hashes remain independent. Source headers/env, application/action credentials and credential digests never enter model requests, catalog text, events, benchmark exports or API views. Fixed safe error reasons replace raw exception text.

## 9 Demo skills and tools

**RM23** The demo MUST expose real gateway function executions against a clearly labeled sandbox data service. Model decisions must be live in live mode. Recorded replay and deterministic model doubles are separate modes with persistent visible labels.

| Skill | Tools | Expected route |
| --- | --- | --- |
| check-service-health | get_service_health(service) | Local completion from status and observation time. |
| lookup-latest-deployment | get_latest_deployment(service) | Local completion from deployment ID and timestamp. |
| summarize-incident-counts | get_incident_counts(service, window_minutes) | Local summary of returned counts, without inferred causes. |
| investigate-performance | get_latest_deployment, get_metrics, get_recent_logs | Local preparation or immediate escalation; external evidence-based assessment. |
| delete-demo-database | list_demo_databases, delete_demo_database(database_id) | Resolve target, require confirmation, mutate only the sandbox fixture. |
| health-and-diagnostic-checklist | get_service_health | Explicit parallel-demo workflow, followed by external synthesis. |

Prefetch of the demo skills (spec 0.31, RM29). check-service-health and lookup-latest-deployment declare their one tool as a prefetch call with service from the request; investigate-performance declares get_latest_deployment and get_recent_logs (limit 50, a cap that does not depend on the request) as prefetch calls, and not get_metrics, whose window is semantic (a request may ask for several windows, as a corpus case does), so the model proposes it; summarize-incident-counts declares none, because window_minutes is semantic; delete-demo-database declares none, because its tools lead to a confirmation. health-and-diagnostic-checklist keeps the fixed parallel workflow of RM15, declared by parallel_workflow. The section 1 demonstration that the local model selects a skill, chooses tools and arguments, and finishes a task runs in the baseline configuration (prefetch off, one call per generation, final pass), which every corpus run includes.

Tool schemas MUST use explicit service or resource IDs. Time windows, log limits, and service values are bounded. get_recent_logs returns a capped list with timestamp and source. Tool outputs carry fixture scenario and observation metadata. delete_demo_database only modifies a disposable local fixture; it never accepts a production connection string.

The initial tool contract uses service IDs staging-api and payments-api, windows of 15, 60, or 1440 minutes, UTC timestamps, and additionalProperties=false for input objects. These are synthetic demo identifiers. The following tool-specific payloads sit inside the common ToolResult envelope from section 8.

| Tool | Input fields | Output data |
| --- | --- | --- |
| get_service_health | service, required enum | service; status enum healthy, degraded, unavailable; checked_at |
| get_latest_deployment | service, required enum | service; deployment_id and deployed_at, nullable together; revision, nullable when no deployment exists |
| get_incident_counts | service; window_minutes | service; window_minutes; total, critical, warning, info as nonnegative integers; category sum equals total |
| get_metrics | service; window_minutes | service; samples array containing timestamp, p95_latency_ms, error_rate from 0 to 1, and nonnegative request_count |
| get_recent_logs | service; limit integer from 1 to 100 | service; entries with timestamp, level, and message; truncated flag |
| list_demo_databases | Empty object | databases array with database_id, display_name, and integer revision |
| delete_demo_database | database_id from lookup; expected_revision | database_id; deleted boolean; resulting fixture revision |

All listed input fields are required unless explicitly described otherwise. A health tool's unavailable status is a successful observation of the service state; failure to contact the tool itself produces a failed ToolResult. The deletion handler compares expected_revision atomically and returns a conflict without mutation if the fixture changed. The pending approval binds that revision as well as the database ID. delete_demo_database declares database_id as its confirmation target (RM11).

Each skill includes English examples and completion criteria. Chinese examples are best-effort: they are kept where they exist, but they are not required. For health checks, the final response must preserve the exact returned health state and time. For diagnostics, it must distinguish observations from hypotheses and may report insufficient evidence. Native Chinese review is not required. Evidence must state honestly when Chinese material has not been natively reviewed (RM16). Family-level multilingual claims do not replace task evaluation.

The UI shows the original prompt, selected mode, active model, loaded skill, tool name and arguments, execution result, escalation reason, final answer, and timing. The comparison view runs equivalent inputs in auto and external_only modes against reset fixtures. A user can cancel a run and inspect failed operations.

Demo UI (spec 0.20, RM23, RM25, #28). The gateway serves the native demo UI itself. GET /ui, GET /ui/app.js, and GET /ui/app.css are static: they need no token, hold no run data, and are sent with a content security policy that allows only the gateway's own origin. The operator types the application token into the page; the page keeps it in memory only, never in browser storage or a URL, and sends it as a bearer token. Every other UI route requires the token (401). The UI is a thin client of the native run API (section 7.4): it creates, resumes, and cancels runs through the native endpoints, and reads evidence from the trace of the run.

- Data mode. The operator declares the data mode when the UI is enabled: live (model decisions come from the configured live adapters), double (scripted deterministic model doubles), or replay (recorded runs already in the run store are shown; no model is called). There is no default: the UI is not served without a declared mode. The mode is shown in a banner that stays on screen in every view, on the shell and in each rendered trace, and it is never inferred from a run. The gateway cannot tell a double from a live adapter, so the label is the operator's declaration; each trace also names the models its own events recorded. In replay mode the UI offers no way to create, resume, or cancel a run, and POST /ui/comparisons is refused with 409 invalid_state. The UI also shows the sandbox label of the fixture service when the operator configured one (section 9, RM23).
- Run trace. GET /ui/runs/{id} returns the rendered trace of one run as an HTML fragment (404 run_not_found). It is built only from the run's stored events, the result artifacts those events reference (section 8), and its run state, so it shows what a native reader can retrieve and nothing else, and it escapes every value: tool output is data, never markup (RM12). It shows the original prompt (original_messages of run.accepted; a run stored before spec 0.20 shows that the prompt was not recorded), the requested and effective execution mode, the answer options (answer_options of run.accepted; a run stored before spec 0.22 shows that they were not recorded, and a run without options shows none), each model call with its model, actor, purpose, and branch, the active model (the model of the latest model call while the run is active, and final_answer_model once it ended), the loaded skill (skill.loaded), the route decision (route.selected, with its mechanism), every tool proposal with its arguments and admissibility, every tool operation with its status, error, duration, and whether it was replayed (a result stored by reference is read back from its artifact and marked as stored by reference with its size; when the artifact is no longer retained, the operation shows the status of its tool.finished event, the reference, and that the result is not retained, and no result data), the escalation reason (handoff.started, or the reason_code of run.failed), the clarification question and the answer, the pending or decided confirmation, the final answer (the joined answer.delta texts with their model), and the run state with its error and reason codes. Operations that failed or have an unknown outcome are also listed together as failed operations. For a run that started the parallel workflow (spec 0.25), the trace also shows a workflow section with the local_health branch, the external_checklist branch, and the synthesis call as separate actors, each with its model, call, outcome, and duration (the branch time, or for the synthesis the extra call latency, of the terminal event's workflow object, so a branch's duration includes its tool run; a run without that object shows that it was not recorded), and the extra call latency, the elapsed time of the parallel stage, and the abandoned calls; every proposal and operation names its branch. The run form offers parallel_demo with the other modes. The UI never renders reasoning that the events do not contain.
- Timing. The latency figure is model action latency: the elapsed_ms of the first validated model-produced route, clarification, or tool proposal (a route.selected the gateway made itself, recorded with actor gateway, such as mechanism bypass, execution_mode, degraded_mode, clarification_resume, client_owned, or routing_cache, is not a model action, and neither is a tool.proposed the gateway rejected, admissible false; a clarification the model requests is its route.selected with route clarify), labeled first model action, and shown as not yet seen while there is none. It is never labeled or presented as the latency of the completed answer (section 12.1). Run active time and waiting time are shown separately from it, as active_ms and waiting_ms of the terminal event, and not at all while the run has no terminal event. The page also shows the browser time at which the user submitted the request, labeled as the browser's clock, next to the server's run.accepted timestamp; it never subtracts one from the other (RM25).
- Clarification and confirmation. While a run is in waiting_input the trace carries a form for the answer, and while it is in waiting_confirmation it shows the tool, arguments, target, and expiry with approve and deny controls. Both submit to POST /relay/v1/runs/{id}/resume with the state version the trace was rendered for. The trace never contains the approval token: the page reads it from the native run view (section 7.4), which returns it once, so the UI adds no second way to see it. The approve and deny controls are rendered disabled, and the page enables them only once it holds the token, so a click can never send a resume without it (spec 0.27). A run in any non-terminal state also carries a cancel control that submits to the native cancel endpoint. A run in a terminal state carries no control.
- Comparison. POST /ui/comparisons takes the body of a native run creation without execution_mode (messages only; another field is 400 unsupported_parameter) and runs the same input in auto and then in external_only against reset fixtures. The operator must configure a fixture service that can reset; without one the request is 409 invalid_state, and both modes must be permitted by the operator policy (403 execution_mode_forbidden). The fixtures are reset before each of the two runs, so the second run never sees a change the first made. The first run ends before the second starts, because one run is active at a time (section 11). The fixtures are reset only while no run is active: a comparison sent while another run is active is 429 and resets nothing. A run that comes to rest waiting for input or confirmation is cancelled by the comparison (reason cancel_requested, a pending approval voided, nothing mutated), because a comparison collects no input and must not change the fixtures after the reset; its trace keeps the question or the approval. A run that does not come to rest within the operator's arm limit is asked to cancel and is reported in the state it has. When it is still active after the cancel's bounded wait (best effort, section 7.4), the comparison does not reset the fixtures or start the next run under it, and the response holds only the arms run so far. The response is 200 with arms, in order, each with execution_mode, run_id, state, state_version, and the fixture label and scenario that the reset produced. Each run is an ordinary run with its own trace. The UI shows the two traces side by side. The pairing is held by the page, not stored by the gateway.
- Page script test (spec 0.27, #89). The page script (GET /ui/app.js) is run by an automated browser test in the default suite, against the real gateway app over model doubles and a disposable sandbox, never live model output (section 13). The test opens a run by ID, starts a run, answers a clarification, approves and denies a confirmation, cancels a waiting run, and runs the comparison, and it checks that each action reaches the native endpoint with the values the trace carried, that the data mode label is visible in the shell and in every trace the page opens, that the rendered text is escaped once and not twice, and that the browser time of a submission is shown, labeled as the browser's clock. It also opens runs that show the recorded answer options, the branch and synthesis actors of the parallel workflow, and a route taken from the routing cache, so the page shows what the stored events record. The browser is an installed development dependency; a machine without it fails the test rather than skipping it, and the CI jobs for all three platforms install it.
- Phase 2 labels and progress (spec 0.31). The shell shows, next to the data mode, the configured local model or its absence, its local_model_status and local_model_reason (RM26), and the placement policy, answer pass, and answer stream in force, read from run.accepted. A trace shows the selector and placement of route.selected (which selector chose, by which mechanism, and where the routing generation ran, with its prediction and its actual time), each prefetch call as a gateway call, the first-token time of each model call, and for a live-streamed answer the statements forwarded so far. The UI never renders a prediction as a measurement: a predicted time is labeled predicted and shown next to the actual duration once there is one.

## 10 Caching policy

**RM24** Skill parsing and template loading MAY be cached immediately by content hash. Routing-result caching MUST be disabled for the first model benchmark. If enabled later, it uses exact request and context matching, a five-minute TTL, and a maximum of 256 entries.

Skill parse cache (spec 0.26). The gateway keeps parsed skill files (the SKILL.md text and the manifest) and the decoded text of declared resources (the templates and examples a skill loads) in a cache keyed by the SHA-256 of the file's bytes. A file with the same bytes is not parsed or decoded again when the registry is loaded again (section 4.2); a file whose bytes changed has a new key and is parsed, so a stale parse cannot be returned. The cache holds only parse results of well-formed files: an invalid file is never cached and is refused on every load. Every package is still validated against the registered tools, predicates, and fixed parallel workflows on every load, so the cache changes cost, not what is accepted, and the skill content hash and version (RM07) are the same with and without it. The cache is on, in memory, and not configurable. Reloading a registry (SkillRegistry.reloaded) re-reads every package from its roots and gives a new registry; a run keeps the version snapshot it started with (RM07).

The routing cache key includes the effective messages, language, caller permissions, skill and tool hashes, model revision, routing prompt version, and policy version. Only local read-only route choices are eligible. No semantic cache, authorization cache, mutation route cache, or cross-user reuse is included. A cached route still performs current authorization and executes required fresh tools; it is not a cached answer.

Routing cache policy (spec 0.26). The cache is controlled by the operator setting cache.routing in the launcher file (section 14.1). It is off by default, and it is off in the benchmark configuration (section 12.2): a gateway, script, or test that does not name the setting runs without a cache, and the first model benchmark runs with it off. When it is off, no entry is read or written and no cache.hit is recorded.

- Key. The exact messages of the run as the router receives them (role, text, the tool_call_id of a tool message, and the tool calls of an assistant message with their call IDs, names, and arguments, in order), the run's language, the execution mode, the caller's permissions and the tool names the operator policy enables, the name, version, and content hash of every skill offered to the router and the schema hash of every tool those skills allow (so a changed skill, manifest, resource, or tool schema is a different key), the identity of the local model (its model_id, which names the pinned artifact revision, section 6), the routing prompt version (the digest of the routing prompt and the routing functions offered, so it changes whenever either changes, with no manual step), the selector chain in force (each selector's name and version, spec 0.31, RM28), and the cache policy version (a constant that changes whenever this section changes; spec 0.31 changes it). The caller is the one authenticated caller of the gateway (section 7.1), so no entry is shared across users. The key is hashed with the canonical form of RM10 and RM17.
- Eligible entries. An entry is stored only after a routing generation chose a skill, the gateway built a validated route=local decision from it, and every tool the skill allows is read-only (no mutating tool, RM11). Clarify, external, parallel, and gateway-made decisions (bypass, execution_mode, degraded_mode, client_owned, clarification_resume, selector_chain) are never stored, and neither is a skill another selector chose (RM28). What is stored is the skill choice, not the decision, the answer, or any tool result.
- Hit. A hit needs an unexpired entry under the same key (five minutes by the monotonic clock, section 12; at most 256 entries, the oldest stored entry is evicted for a new one). On a hit the gateway skips the routing generation and the tokenizer count of the router input, and builds the decision from the cached skill choice with the same gateway rules as a resume (section 5): identifier provenance (RM05) is checked again and the route comes from the current registry entry (RM04). A result that is not route=local is a miss and the entry is dropped. The run then continues exactly as an uncached run: the skill is loaded at the version run.accepted recorded (RM07), the tools are the intersection of skill allowlist, operator policy, and the current caller permissions (RM08), every call is validated (RM09), and every tool executes fresh (RM10); no tool result is cached. A cached route therefore cannot grant a permission or skip an authorization check, and a run whose current permissions allow none of the skill's tools ends as capability_missing like an uncached run. A run counts the hit like any local run: zero external calls (RM01) and no routing model call in model_calls.
- Invalidation (AC13, RM07). Before each lookup and each store the cache drops every entry whose recorded skill hashes differ from the registry the run uses (a skill changed, was added to the offered set, or was removed) or whose recorded permissions or enabled tools differ from the current ones. A changed skill or permission therefore ends the dependent entries at once, not at the TTL, and the next request routes with a model generation under the new key. Before each lookup and each store it also removes every entry older than the TTL, counted as expired, so eviction counts only entries removed for space. The cache also exposes counters (hits, misses, stores, expired, invalidated, evicted) so an experiment can report them; a hit the gateway cannot use (not route=local) is counted as a miss.
- Labels. run.accepted carries routing_cache true when the cache is enabled for the run (section 8). Every route.selected of a run with the cache enabled carries routing_cache hit or miss (a hit has mechanism routing_cache, section 8); a gateway-made route.selected (bypass, execution_mode, degraded_mode, clarification_resume, client_owned, selector_chain) is a miss, because no cached choice was used. The demo UI shows the route mechanism, so a cached route is visible as one. A route.selected with mechanism routing_cache is made by the gateway, so it is not a model action for the first-model-action metric (section 12.1).

Prompt prefix reuse (spec 0.31, RM27). The routing request's first several hundred local tokens (the routing prompt and the skill functions) are the same for every request of a gateway, and the skill stage's instructions are the same for every request of a skill. The gateway requests cache_prompt explicitly, records the cached prompt tokens of every call (section 6), and lays prompts out so that the constant part comes first. In the external request the rules, the skill instructions, and the fixed completion criteria come first, and the run-specific values (the run ID, the remaining budgets) last. The skill stage keeps the skill description and the tool to call first and the skill body after the request (spec 0.35): putting the body before the conversation was measured on the pinned model on 2026-10-23 and lowered local completion and tool correctness (`docs/EVIDENCE.md`), so the layout changes only on a new measurement. Whether the runtime or the provider reuses the prefix is measured, never assumed: the latency experiment reports cached tokens per call (section 12.2), and a count the adapter did not read is reported as not measured. The pinned launch settings do not change for this; a slot pin or a slot count change would be a manifest revision under RM18.

Prefix or KV caching is controlled by the inference runtime or provider and is recorded separately. Reusing a prompt string in SQLite does not imply reuse of inference computation. Cache-on results MUST be labeled and reported separately from the uncached baseline: an exported statistics file (RM25, section 12.1) names the routing cache setting of the run that produced it (routing_cache on or off, off when the exporter was not told), and cache-on figures are never merged with, or presented as, the baseline. A cache-on measurement is an experiment of its own; the hypothesis and gates of section 12.3 are stated for the cache-off configuration. [S8]

M10 invalidation (spec 0.51; RM24, RM34, RM37–RM43): catalog/index caches key exact source/content/schema/catalog and representation/model/config revisions; offer projections key catalog_revision, selection_scope and the requested_kinds set, and a stale, availability or eligibility change increments catalog_revision (section 14.5, spec 0.52). Feedback/adaptation caches also key active scope and feedback_revision. Changed/deleted content and policy invalidate eligibility immediately; expiry/delete/reset remove derived private effects and stored replay copies under section 5.1. Failed rebuild keeps the last compatible generation and filters old versions. These caches never cache tool results or change the managed routing-cache policy above.

## 11 Budgets and failure behavior

Initial engineering defaults are listed below. They are configurable limits, not predicted timings; from spec 0.31 the launch file sets them in its budgets table (section 14.1), and every run records the values in force (RM03).

| Budget | Default |
| --- | --- |
| Routing input for fast experiment | At most 2,048 local tokens |
| Routing output | At most 128 tokens |
| Local execution | Four seconds active time; routing plus three further generations; at most two tool executions |
| Entire run | 60 seconds active time; at most eight model generations including routing and repair; at most six executed business tools |
| Tool timeout | Two seconds unless a tool declares a shorter timeout |
| External generation | At most 30 seconds per generation and six generations (external_seconds, external_generations), also bounded by the remaining run budget |
| Simultaneous requests | One active run in the baseline; reject additional work with 429 rather than hide queue time |
| Waiting state | Five minutes per wait, recorded as waiting_seconds and excluded from active execution time |
| Clarification rounds | Two per run; a third needed clarification ends the run as failed with missing_input |
| Selection time limit | Two seconds per selector that is not a model (RM28); a model-based selector is bounded by its generation's limits |
| Admitted calls per generation | Up to the per-stage tool limit of read-only calls in a local stage, the remaining entire-run tool limit in the external stage; a mutating call alone; a set whose new executions the remaining tool budget has no room for is rejected whole before anything runs (section 4.3, spec 0.34) |
| Local generation deadline | The placement prediction times placement.margin (default 2), capped by the remaining stage budget (RM27) |

The Local execution row is a per-stage limit. A run's first local stage starts at routing; a resume after a clarification opens a new local stage with the full per-stage limits, so a resumed run that routes again has its routing generation (section 5). The Entire run row bounds the sum of all stages, and waiting time counts toward none of them. The two model branches in parallel_demo are allowed within a single active run (section 4.4): the local branch uses the local per-stage limits, the checklist draft and the synthesis call each count as an external generation and in the entire-run generation limit, and the draft is bounded by external_seconds. The local-first per-stage limits do not constrain external-only baseline routing, but the total run limits apply to every mode. A skill that requires more local work is escalated instead of quietly increasing budgets.

Error contract. Every error response has the JSON body {"error": {...}}, where the error object has a stable code, a message, run_id (the run reference, or null when no run exists), and reason_code only when the failure has an RM04 reason code. The code is the contract; the message is for people. The statuses and codes are:

| Status | Codes | Cause |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 400 | invalid_request, unsupported_parameter, invalid_execution_mode, invalid_resume_input, context_limit_exceeded | A validation failure, including a resume whose body does not match the waiting state (section 7.4) and evidence over the external context limit (RM13) |
| 401 | unauthenticated | A missing or invalid application token |
| 403 | execution_mode_forbidden, approval_invalid | Denied access, including an approval ID or token that does not match (section 5) |
| 404 | run_not_found | A run that does not exist |
| 409 | stale_version, run_terminal, invalid_state, idempotency_conflict | A stale state version (RM17), a resume of a terminal run or a chat duplicate of a failed or cancelled one, a resume in a state that cannot take it, a reused idempotency key with a changed payload, or an unfinished chat request under it (section 7.4) |
| 422 | needs_external | A task that cannot complete under the effective execution mode (section 7.2) |
| 429 | overloaded | Another run holds the execution slot; the request is rejected, not queued |
| 500 | internal_error | An unexpected failure |
| 503 | workflow_unavailable, external_failed | An unavailable required model, or in parallel_demo an unavailable workflow (section 4.4) |
| 504 | deadline_exceeded | An exhausted time limit before streaming starts |

A failed run maps to this table by its error code: needs_external is 422, context_limit_exceeded is 400, workflow_unavailable is 503, internal_error is 500, and external_failed is 503, except that an external_failed run stopped by an exhausted time limit (the entire-run active time, or external_seconds) is 504 with code deadline_exceeded; its run.failed event keeps error_code external_failed and names the limit. predicate_unmet (spec 0.31, RM32) occurs only after a live stream forwarded statements: the stream ends with the mid-stream error event of section 7.3 carrying that code, the native API reports it as run state, and a request without stream never sees it, because a buffered answer takes the correction loop instead. A run that ended as missing_input, input_expired, approval_expired, or interrupted, or as cancelled, is not an error response to the request that created it: the native API reports it as run state with its error code (section 7.4). Internal errors carry a fixed message and omit credentials, exception text, and secret values (RM12).

After streaming starts, an unrecoverable failure is signaled as an error and connection termination, and recorded as run.failed. It MUST NOT produce a successful finish marker. The compatible client behavior for this failure is part of integration tests because SDK handling may vary.

An unavailable local model, meaning a missing local capability or a failed local generation, may route directly to external only when the operator policy permits degraded mode and external dispatch is permitted (an effective mode other than local_only, a configured external model, and room in the entire-run limits). Degraded mode is off by default. When it is on, the run hands off with reason code capability_missing, with the evidence it has so far; when the failure came before any routing decision, the gateway records its own decision first, route=external, skill_id=null, reason_code=capability_missing, in route.selected with mechanism degraded_mode. The handoff.started payload then carries degraded_mode true and local_failure (the cause), so the trace records degraded mode. Without the policy, or when dispatch is not permitted, the run ends as needs_external with reason code capability_missing. An unavailable external model does not make a complex request locally solvable. If external use is disabled, return needs_external with partial results. Read-only retries are limited to one for a transient failure within all budgets; model repair also counts toward generation limits. A gateway with no configured local model is not in degraded mode: it runs managed external execution with reason code no_local_model (RM26), and a selector chain that chose nothing hands off with reason code selection_failed (RM28).

An external stage that cannot finish ends the run as failed with error code external_failed. Causes are an adapter error, an unreachable endpoint, an answer that does not meet the completion predicate within external_generations, and an exhausted entire-run limit. The partial evidence stays in the trace, no local answer replaces the external one, and no result is invented (AC07). The run.failed event carries an RM04 reason code: the reason the external stage stopped when it stopped on one, otherwise the reason for the escalation. Evidence over the external context budget is a separate code, context_limit_exceeded (RM13). The HTTP mapping of both codes is in the error contract above.

### 11.1 Recommendation budgets and errors (spec 0.51)
