Recommendation budgets/slots/job limits in section 14.5 are separate from all execution-run limits above. Never silently increase either. A custom effective budget is recorded and cannot claim standard acceptance unless its frozen protocol matches. There is no automatic retry of interrupted recommendation computation. Service elapsed starts at receipt of the authenticated create and includes body parsing/transport preparation through committed response serialization; the primary cap starts at its dispatch within that remaining clock. Validation before acceptance consumes that same total, not a fresh budget. Relative dispatch deadlines avoid cross-process wall-clock comparison.

Recommendation errors use the section 11 error contract with run_id null and an added recommendation_id (UUID or null): `{error:{code,message,run_id:null,recommendation_id}}`. Existing run and chat error bodies, codes and statuses are unchanged. Shared codes, including unauthenticated (401) and internal_error (500), keep their section 11 status. Messages are fixed safe text, never raw exception or credential text. This table lists the recommendation-only codes and the recommendation causes of shared ones:

| HTTP | Code | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 400 | invalid_request | Malformed/unknown/oversized field, invalid enum/number/key/target. |
| 403 | user_action_required | Missing/invalid/revoked/wrong-scope direct-action registration, including replay. |
| 403 | scope_mismatch | The caller's expected scope (spec 0.63, section 7.6) differs from the active scope; nothing is read or written. |
| 404 | recommendation_not_found | Absent or foreign-scope recommendation. |
| 404 | feedback_not_found | Absent/foreign-scope/purged feedback. |
| 409 | stale_version | Expected lifecycle/feedback revision differs. |
| 409 | idempotency_conflict | Scoped key or event ID reused with changed canonical payload. |
| 409 | recommendation_in_progress | Identical accepted operation is still in flight; no extra compute/wait. |
| 409 | recommendation_expired | Fresh ready choice after expiry; committed choice replay remains historical. |
| 409 | asset_changed | Offered content/schema/version or current eligibility changed; fresh offer required. |
| 409 | invalid_state | Fresh incompatible terminal action, or replay payload erased by privacy policy. |
| 429 | overloaded | Separate nonqueued recommendation slot is busy. |
| 503 | recommendation_unavailable | Disabled/no usable catalog/lexical generation, unsupported runtime binding, or interrupted create replay; stored reason distinguishes causes. |
| 504 | deadline_exceeded | Total budget exhausted before ready commit. |

Authentication/parsing/replay precedence is section 7.6. Expiry transitions once before fresh choice checks; stale expected versions are checked before fresh lifecycle/eligibility errors. Cancellation wins simultaneous timeout and prevents later attempts. A terminal create failure replays its recorded error; interrupted recovery uses recommendation_unavailable with internal reason interrupted, not another inference attempt.

## 12 Performance and quality evaluation

### 12.1 Metrics

**RM25** Timings MUST use a monotonic clock for elapsed durations and UTC for event timestamps. Record both server arrival and UI submission; never subtract timestamps from unsynchronized hosts.

| Metric | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| First transport event | First received byte or status event; reported as transport latency only. |
| First model action | First validated model-produced route, clarification, or tool proposal available to the client. |
| First useful content | First displayed result supported by task evidence, or the first necessary clarification question. Generic acknowledgments do not qualify. |
| First answer token | First nonempty answer-content delta in the compatible stream. |
| Completion latency | Request to successful final answer; active time and human waiting time reported separately. Waiting time is the sum of every recorded wait of the run, reported as waiting_ms in run.completed and run.failed (section 8), never taken from client timestamps: each clarification contributes the waited_ms of its clarification.answered event, so a run with two clarification rounds (section 11) sums two values; each confirmation contributes the monotonic time from confirmation.required to the resume that approved or denied it. A wait that ends the run (input_expired, approval_expired, user_denied) counts toward the waiting_ms of its terminal event. |
| Local completion rate | Simple-task cases successfully completed with zero external inference calls, divided by all simple-task cases. |
| Tool correctness | Correct tool and semantically correct arguments against fixture expectations; schema validity reported separately. |
| Escalation quality | Missed necessary escalations and unnecessary escalations, reported separately by class and language. |
| External usage | Actual requests and per-model input and output tokens, including abandoned parallel calls. An abandoned branch call is in model_calls and in external_calls of the terminal event. |
| Parallel extra call | The duration of the synthesis call or calls of the parallel workflow, from the monotonic clock, reported as extra_call_ms in the workflow object of the terminal event (section 8), next to the elapsed time of each branch. |
| Generations per completed run | Spec 0.31: the model calls of a completed run, by actor and purpose; the measure of RM29, RM31, and the admitted-calls rule of section 4.3. |
| Wasted local time | Spec 0.31: the local active milliseconds of a run that ended with an external answer or as needs_external, reported as a distribution per configuration (RM27). |
| Prediction error | Spec 0.31: predicted_ms minus the actual duration of each local generation, as a distribution; a prediction is never reported as a measurement (RM27). |
| First token per call | Spec 0.31: first_token_ms of every model call, by model and purpose, from the monotonic clock (section 6); the fixed cost of an external call is reported from it, never inferred from total durations. |
| Selection accuracy | Spec 0.31: the share of corpus cases for which a selector chose the expected skill, per selector, language, and class, against the sidecar file of section 12.2 (RM28); and, on the selection benchmarks of section 12.4, precision, recall, and Jaccard per selector as the main metrics, with the none-rate and selection latency. On the corpus the expected set has one skill, or none for a case that expects a clarification first. |
| Corrections per completed run | Spec 0.31: external answer generations the completion predicate rejected, per completed run, with their summed duration; the measure of the correction cost (section 4.4). See finding F6 of the 2026-10-23 analysis in `docs/EVIDENCE.md`. |
| Streamed failures | Spec 0.41: export configured answer_stream and answer_stream_effective per case, terminal error_code and task success; summarize each effective policy separately and retain every failure. The paired live/buffered corpus comparison uses one final revision and frozen timing prior, and is required before changing defaults. Spec 0.31: live runs that ended predicate_unmet; reported with task success next to buffered, which is the gate of RM32. A forwarded-then-rejected count is zero by construction and is not reported as a result. |
| Gateway completion | Retired planned metric (spec 0.47, RM30): there is no workflow render-step deliverable. A host tool result or a client-owned call proposal is not a gateway-generated answer or a completed host task. |
| Prefetch correctness | Spec 0.31: resolved prefetch calls that match the case's acceptable calls. Tool correctness (above) grades model-made proposals only and reports its n, which may be 0 for a simple skill with prefetch on. |

The 500 ms hypothesis is p95 first model action at or below 500 ms for the warmed, short-context, concurrency-one experiment with routing-result caching disabled. The UI displays this as model action latency, never as completed-answer latency. Report whether it is met on each tested machine; do not average a slow Windows result into a fast Mac result. A missed target is a finding, not grounds to fabricate an early response.

### 12.2 Reproducible experiment

The correctness corpus contains 40 English scenarios: 12 simple, 10 complex, 6 ambiguous, 6 requiring mutation confirmation, and 6 adversarial or fault scenarios. Simplified Chinese versions of the scenarios are optional and best-effort; when present, results are reported per language and never merged into the English results. Each case includes fixture state, acceptable tools and arguments, required evidence, expected permissions, and a grading rubric. Freeze the corpus before comparing candidates and keep development examples separate.

Run auto, external_only, and local_only against the same fixture snapshots. Baselines use the same skills, tool descriptions, execution rules, and output criteria. Select identical relevant external data where the route permits it. Count provider calls; do not infer cost savings from latency. Monetary estimates require actual provider rates and a dated rate source and are optional.

For the latency experiment, use 12 short English prompts, including routing, clarification, and simple tool tasks. Perform three warm-up requests per model configuration, then ten measured runs per prompt in randomized order. Record errors and timeouts rather than dropping them. Chinese prompts may be added on a best-effort basis and are reported per language. Report p50, p95, sample count, and timeout fraction. Report cold startup separately, including model load time. Exact-route caching stays off (cache.routing false, section 14.1) and the exported statistics say so; runtime prefix-cache behavior is explicitly recorded.

Record OS version, CPU, GPU, RAM, backend, thread settings, model hashes, quantization, context settings, sampling parameters, runtime and application revisions, external endpoint region if known, and test time. Shared provider load remains a potential source of variability. Actual hardware is filled into the benchmark report after execution; no reference machine performance is assumed here.

Correctness corpus (spec 0.28, #32). The corpus is one versioned file, `evaluation/corpus.v1.json`, with the sandbox seeds it names. Each case has an ID, a class (simple, complex, ambiguous, mutation, or adversarial), a language (en, or zh for a best-effort Simplified Chinese version of an English case, marked best_effort and linked to its English case), the prompt, the fixture seed (a sandbox seed file under `tests/fixtures/sandbox/`, section 9), the expected permissions (the caller permissions the case runs with, section 14.1), the expected route in auto mode (local, external, or clarify), the acceptable tool calls (groups of calls of which at least one must be proposed, and further calls that may be proposed; an argument is an exact value, a list of acceptable values, or any value), the clarification answer and confirmation decision the runner gives, the expected run outcomes, the required and forbidden evidence (patterns that the final answer must and must not contain), the expected sandbox databases after the run where the case can change them, and a grading rubric in words. The prompts must not repeat a development example: the skill examples (`skills/*/examples.json`) and the prompts of the live acceptance harness are development examples and are never corpus cases. The corpus is frozen by a SHA-256 digest of the canonical JSON (RM07) of the corpus and every seed it names, kept in `evaluation/corpus.v1.lock.json`. The runner refuses a corpus or seed whose digest differs from the lock, and the export records the digest. A change to a frozen case is a new corpus version, recorded in the changelog; results of two versions are never merged.

Corpus runner (spec 0.28, #32). `scripts/run_corpus.py` starts llama-server from the manifest and, for each case and each of auto, external_only, and local_only, a new gateway with `relaymind serve --config` (section 14.1): a new store, a new sandbox working copy from the case's seed, the case's permissions, the checked-in skills, the routing cache off, and the configured external endpoint. So every mode of a case starts from the same fixture snapshot. The runner creates one native run (section 7.4) with the case prompt and the mode. When the run waits for input it resumes with the case's clarification answer (it cancels the run when the case has none); when the run waits for confirmation it approves only when the case says approve and the pending call is an acceptable call, and denies otherwise. It reads the trace back from the store. The grades are computed from the trace, the terminal state, and the sandbox state after the run:

- Task success: the outcome is one of the expected outcomes, a completed answer contains every required evidence group, no answer contains forbidden evidence, the tool calls are correct, every started tool needs only the case's permissions, no mandatory confirmation was missed, the sandbox ends as expected, and an expected clarification was asked.
- Local completion: a simple case with task success and zero external inference calls (external_calls of the terminal event is 0). The rate divides by all simple cases of the language and mode.
- Tool correctness: every model-made tool proposal of the run matches an acceptable call, and each required group is matched by at least one proposal. With prefetch on, a gateway-made proposal that was admitted also counts toward a required group, because the evidence the case needs was gathered; it is never judged against the acceptable calls (prefetch correctness is a separate measure) and it is reported apart as gateway_proposals. A run without a proposal of either kind is correct only when no group is required. Schema validity is reported separately: the proposals not rejected with unknown_tool or invalid_arguments, over all proposals.
- Escalation: a run escalated when it made an external inference call. In auto mode, a case whose expected route is external and did not escalate is a missed necessary escalation, and a case whose expected route is local or clarify and escalated is an unnecessary escalation; both are reported separately by class and language. external_only and local_only do not choose a route, so they have no escalation grades.
- Missed mandatory confirmation: a mutating tool that started without a confirmation.required event for its proposal earlier in the trace (RM11). The count is reported for every mode, together with the mutation cases where the model reached a confirmation.
- Provider calls: local and external inference calls and input and output tokens per model, from model_calls and external_calls of the terminal event.

The report gives exact numerators and denominators per mode, class, and language; Chinese results are reported apart and never merged into the English ones. A run that does not come to rest within the runner's time limit is cancelled and graded as a timeout, never dropped. In local_only a case whose expected route is external cannot succeed by design; its expected behavior is an explicit failure (needs_external), which the report counts.

Latency runner (spec 0.28, #33). `scripts/latency_benchmark.py` measures the local path: one gateway started with `relaymind serve --config`, the routing cache off, no external model, concurrency one. The prompts are `evaluation/latency_prompts.v1.json`: twelve short English prompts, each labeled as a simple tool task (expected to complete), a clarification (expected to wait for input), or a routing prompt (expected to route external, so without an external model it ends as needs_external after the local routing decision). Each request is a compatible chat request with stream true (section 7.3). Before the warm-ups the first request after the gateway starts is recorded as the cold request, apart from the measured runs. Three warm-up requests follow and are not measured; then ten runs per prompt in an order shuffled with a recorded seed. A run that waits for input is cancelled with the native cancel after its sample is taken, so the next run can start. The metrics of section 12.1 are taken this way:

- First transport event and first answer token: from the client's monotonic clock, from just before the request is sent to the arrival of the response status and of the first chunk with nonempty content.
- First model action and first useful content: from the run's trace, as elapsed_ms (the gateway's monotonic clock from run.accepted, section 8): the first validated model-made route, clarification, or tool proposal (as in the demo UI timing, section 9), and the first answer.delta after a successful tool result, or the clarification.required event.

The two clocks are never subtracted from each other. A request that has no response within the per-request time limit is a timeout; a transport error, an error status that the prompt's label does not expect, or a run that ends other than its label is an error. Both are recorded with the sample, never dropped. Each metric reports p50 and p95 by the nearest-rank method (the ceil(q n)-th smallest of the n values present), its sample count, and the timeout fraction (timeouts over all measured requests), overall and per prompt label. The hypothesis is checked on the first model action of the measured runs. Cold startup reports the llama-server start to its first healthy /health response (it includes the model load), the model load time printed by the runtime when it prints one, the gateway start to its first healthy /healthz, and the cold request. The export records the runtime's /props response (context and default sampling), whether the adapter sets cache_prompt (the runtime's prefix cache behavior), and the cached_tokens of every measured model call.

Phase 2 configurations and device classes (spec 0.31). The corpus and latency runners take the phase 2 options as labeled configurations, each a launch-file key (section 14.1), and record them in the export: answer_pass, calls_per_generation, prefetch, answer_stream, early_headers, placement.policy, the selector chain, the enabled fixed parallel workflows, and whether a local model is configured. The baseline of section 12.3 is the configuration before this revision, reproducible by the keys: answer_pass final, calls_per_generation one, prefetch false, answer_stream buffered, early_headers false, placement local_first, chain model_router; the layout and warm-up changes of sections 6 and 10 are code, so a before-and-after comparison names the application revisions. Each configuration is reported apart and never merged with another. The runners accept a timing-sample file, seeded or carried over from an earlier run, and an external model with, optionally, its declared request options (external_model.request_options of section 14.1, given as one JSON object, checked as the launch file checks them, and recorded in the export configuration only when declared, so a configuration without them keeps its identity; spec 0.46), so the placement rules can be exercised in a run that starts a new store per case; the samples used are in the export, and without them placement records predictions and skips nothing. Results are reported per machine, labeled with the class the gateway measured on it (suitable, unsuitable, or none; section 1); a class is the measurement of that machine, never a claim about other machines, and no hardware type is required for a class. A constrained configuration of one machine, such as a CPU-only runtime with fewer threads, is a valid way to measure the unsuitable class: its extra runtime arguments are recorded in the export with the thread and backend settings above, and it is reported as that configuration of that machine, never as another machine. The unsuitable class is any machine or configuration on which the warm-up or the recorded local stage outcomes show that local work does not fit its budgets (RM26, spec 0.48). The expected skill of every corpus case is a sidecar file, `evaluation/corpus.v1.skills.json`, frozen with its own digest in the lock file, so corpus version 1 and its results stay comparable and the case file does not change. A run that used an out-of-process selector is reported in its own column (RM28). The latency runner also records the cold request's first-token time and, with warm-up on, the warm-up samples; the cached-token counts it reports are the ones section 6 reads, and a run of the runner before that change is reported as not measured for them.

Release record (spec 0.28, section 14). `docs/RELEASE.md` is the release record. It lists, in separate sections, the implemented behavior, the passing deterministic tests (with the command and the count), the measured live results (each with its machine, date, and export), and the unmet or unmeasured targets, including platforms that were not run.

### 12.3 Gates and experimental targets

Hard acceptance gates are all deterministic contract and authorization tests passing, successful live local skill-and-tool traces for all three simple demo skills in English (Chinese live results are reported but do not block acceptance), correct handoff without duplicate completed operations, a working model substitution test, and a reproducible benchmark report from both target platforms.

Proposed model-quality targets are at least 80 percent successful local completion on the English simple subset, at least 95 percent correct tool-and-argument proposals on that subset, and no missed mandatory confirmation in the bounded test corpus. Chinese results, when present, are reported per language against the same targets but do not block acceptance. Report exact numerators and denominators. Passing a small corpus does not establish a production reliability guarantee.

The 500 ms target and quality percentages are experimental outcomes, not pre-existing facts. If a target fails, retain the evidence, qualify the candidate as unsuitable for that claim, and compare another model or narrower skill scope. Do not silently substitute a larger model while continuing to label results Qwen3-0.6B.

Phase 2 hypotheses (spec 0.31), stated as experimental targets, not facts: the draft answer pass raises local completion on the English simple subset without lowering task success; prefetch and several calls per generation lower the generations per completed complex run; completion criteria in the external request lower corrections per completed run; early headers give a chat client its run reference before the first answer generation; live streaming lowers first useful content in the none class at task-success parity with buffered; on an unsuitable machine, auto with the class detected has lower p95 first useful content and completion than local-first auto without it, at equal task success (spec 0.48: once the class is unsuitable, auto is managed external execution under every policy, so section 14.4.7 defines how this is measured); the example matcher's English selection Jaccard is at least the local router's, on the corpus and, under the router's eight-skill cap, on the skill benchmark of section 12.4; a selector's narrowing raises the precision of the local model's tool proposals on the tool benchmark without lowering their recall; and, in every configuration, external calls and tokens per completed task are reported next to task success, because the objective is the best use of the external model, not more of it. A missed target is a finding. Every default of this revision is the pre-revision behavior. A hypothesis that holds in its run makes its option a candidate default (spec 0.45): the next revision changes the default only when, in the same run, the option's paired full auto corpus cohort has at least as many task successes as the baseline's (gains at least losses, every planned case in the denominator), and records the numbers either way. Task success in these hypotheses means that full cohort, never a subset chosen after the run. Section 14.4.6 records the decisions of the first measured run.

### 12.4 Selection benchmarks (spec 0.31)

The ops corpus of section 12.2 has six skills and seven tools, which cannot tell a good selection system from a poor one, and no existing benchmark measures this task. RelayMind's selection benchmarks are therefore its own, built from two public datasets, SkillsBench and ToolRet-Training-20w, by the derivations below. They measure, on many more skills and tools, which system is useful: the default (the local model's routing generation, RM02, and its tool proposals in the skill stage) against the example matcher and against a third-party selector, every one run through the RM28 interface on the same frozen files, so the comparison is the same for all. They measure selection in their sources' domains (office and coding tasks, web APIs), not the ops demo, and every report says so; their results are never merged with the corpus of section 12.2.

Skill selection. The source is SkillsBench (benchflow-ai/skillsbench, Apache-2.0), pinned to the git commit and the per-task digests of its registry.json. Derivation: for every active task, the body of task.md is the request; the skills bundled under environment/skills/ are the expected set, because the task's authors chose them and the benchmark's own rule forbids naming them in the prompt; the catalog is the union of all bundled skills, deduplicated by name with the description of its SKILL.md (a skill bundled under several tasks with differing files is kept once, the first in task order, and the difference is recorded); a task with no bundled skill is excluded and counted. The derived file is evaluation/skills.v1.json, checked in with attribution, the pinned commit, and a SHA-256 lock, like the corpus. Example requests for a selector that uses them come from the other tasks that bundle the same skill (leave-one-task-out), never from the case's own prompt. The catalog offered is the whole pool for a selector without a size limit; the local model router offers at most eight skills (section 4.2), so each case is run for it with a catalog of eight, the expected skills plus distractors sampled with a recorded seed, and the report states that the router was measured under its cap. A request over the router's input cap takes the bypass (section 4.1) and is counted as such, never dropped. Metrics. The predicted set of a case is the result's candidate skill and its alternatives (empty for a result of none or clarify). The main metrics compare it with the expected set E: precision |P∩E| / |P|, recall |P∩E| / |E|, and Jaccard |P∩E| / |P∪E|, each 0 for an empty predicted set, macro-averaged over cases, with the micro averages beside them. The model router selects one skill, so its predicted set has one member, and a task that bundles several skills bounds its recall; the report says so. Diagnostics reported with the main metrics: the none-rate, the invalid-output rate of the model router, the bypass count, selection latency per case (monotonic), and the model router's input tokens. Results are reported overall and per SkillsBench category and difficulty, from the task metadata.

Tool selection. The source is ToolRet-Training-20w (the ToolRet training set, Hugging Face mangopy/ToolRet-Training-20w), pinned to a dataset revision. Its licence is not stated on its card, so no row is checked in: the tool lock, evaluation/tools.v2.lock.json, holds the pinned revision, the sampled row ids, the sampling seed, and the SHA-256 of each derived case, and the runner fetches the revision, derives the cases, and refuses a case whose digest differs. The sample is a frozen random sample of 500 rows, stratified by the number of positive tools (one, two to three, four or more). Derivation: the request is the row's query (its instruction field is recorded in the export but not given to a selector, because RelayMind requests carry no such instruction); the candidates are the row's positive and negative tools, normalized to the gateway's tool declaration (name, description, an input schema from the tool's parameters with additionalProperties false; an entry in the RapidAPI style is mapped from its required and optional parameters) and registered in a benchmark-only registry whose handlers never run; normalized names that collide get numeric suffixes in the order of each declaration's digest, with a seeded hash between equal declarations, so a name never shows whether a candidate is positive (evaluation/tools.v1.lock.json, whose suffixes followed source order with the positives first, has the same rows and is kept only to reproduce the runs recorded with it, spec 0.37.1); one synthetic skill per case allows exactly the candidates, so the selection interface and the skill stage see them as they would see any skill's tools. Two tracks. The narrowing track scores the set of tools a selector keeps against the positives with precision, recall, and Jaccard, with completeness (every positive kept) and latency as diagnostics; the default model router does not narrow, so its kept set is every candidate (recall 1, precision the share of positives among the candidates), which is the baseline a narrowing selector must beat. The track needs no model; its scoring code runs in the default test suite on synthetic fixture cases written for the test, and the real sample runs on demand, because no dataset row is checked in. The proposal track scores the set of tools that the first skill-stage generation of a live model proposes (the local model under its budgets, or the external model; several calls under calls_per_generation many) against the positives with precision, recall, and Jaccard, with schema validity (proposals not rejected with unknown_tool or invalid_arguments) and the bypass rate (a candidate set over the model's input limit is counted, never dropped) as diagnostics; the proposal track is run with the full candidate set and again with the set a selector narrowed, which measures whether narrowing helps the small model. Argument correctness is not graded, because the source carries no reference arguments. A first generation that exceeds the configured calls_per_generation limit is invalid_output and contributes an empty predicted set to precision, recall, Jaccard, and completeness. Its returned tool names, proposal count, usage, and schema-validity results remain recorded as evidence; a budget rejection does not itself make a proposal schema-invalid. This rule does not remove proposed names from the set metrics solely because their tool name or arguments fail schema validation. When tool narrowing errors, times out, or returns invalid output, the full-candidate proposal arm may still be measured; the narrowed arm records the narrowing failure with an empty predicted set and no model invocation, and remains in the denominator. A schema-valid none or clarify result is an abstention, not a narrowing failure, and may be measured with its empty tool set.

Retired plan benchmark (spec 0.47). The planned evaluation/plans.v1.json derivation and workflow-plan track are removed with RM30. The existing skill and tool selection benchmarks, their locks, and their historical results remain unchanged. OpenClaw evaluation follows section 12.5 and does not relabel managed-skill corpus results as host-tool results.

Protocol. scripts/selection_benchmark.py runs every selector through the SelectionRequest and SelectionResult shapes (RM28), the built-in ones in process and a third party over the HTTP transport, with the same files; the export records the selector's name and version, the files' digests, the seed, the machine manifest, the timing of every case, and, for a model, its manifest or endpoint; a run that times out or errors is recorded with the case, never dropped. Results of different selectors on the same file version are comparable; results on different versions are never merged, and a changed case is a new file version recorded in the changelog. A selector trained or tuned on a source's data is reported with that fact next to its scores, because its result on that source is not held out; models fine-tuned on ToolRet-Training-20w exist.

### 12.5 OpenClaw integration evaluation (spec 0.47; fixtures, arms, and grading spec 0.50)

M9 compares the same text and sandbox host-tool tasks through (1) OpenClaw with its direct external provider, (2) OpenClaw through RelayMind external_only to that same provider/model, and (3) OpenClaw through RelayMind auto on a machine with a usable local model. It also exercises a launch without a local model and reports it separately. external_only versus direct measures integration overhead; auto versus those arms tests whether local inference helps. A constrained configuration is reported separately when run, never assumed equivalent to a second machine. Freeze the bilingual task fixtures, expected tool names and arguments, host tool policy, prompts, budgets, sampling settings, and versions before measurement; report Chinese results separately as best-effort.

Record end-to-end task success with exact counts, first useful text or complete actionable tool call, completion latency including host tool time, total inference attempts, failures and cancellations, and external calls and per-model tokens per completed task. A header, role chunk, SSE keep-alive, plugin load, or proposed tool call is not proof of a completed task. Record host execution evidence separately from RelayMind's tool.proposed events; the gateway must have no tool.started/tool.finished event for a host tool. Use native per-call usage, retain unknown counts as unknown, and do not add different tokenizers into a purported unified token count. Report all attempts, including failed and retried ones.

Record the OpenClaw release and SDK, plugin revision, RelayMind revision, runtime/model manifests, OS and machine, configuration, and trace/run correlation. Deterministic transport tests and live model runs are separate evidence. Qualify the existing RelayMind target platforms individually, recording OpenClaw's actual supported runtime on each (including WSL2 if used on Windows); do not label WSL2 as native Windows. A missing platform run stays not_run. M9 succeeds when AC24 and reproducible installation pass and the measurements are recorded; reduced latency, lower cost, and local completion quality remain hypotheses, not compatibility guarantees.

Task fixtures (spec 0.50, #139). The tasks are one versioned file, `evaluation/openclaw_tasks.v1.json`, frozen by the SHA-256 digest of its canonical JSON (RM07) in `evaluation/openclaw_tasks.v1.lock.json`; the runner refuses a file whose digest differs, and a changed task is a new version. It is not the managed-skill corpus of section 12.2: its tasks run through the host, the corpus, its sidecars, and the selection benchmark files stay unchanged, and a corpus score is never reported as a host task result. The file freezes the arms, the repeat count, the sampling seed, the time limit of one attempt, and the host release. Each task has an ID, a kind (text or host_tool), a language (en, or zh linked to its English task and marked best_effort), the prompt the operator gives the host, the sandbox files written to a fresh working directory before each attempt, the host's exec policy (deny or full; a headless ask policy fails closed and is not used), the expected host tool calls (groups of acceptable calls, each argument an exact value, a suffix, a substring, or any value), the expected sandbox files after the attempt, the required and forbidden evidence in the host's final answer, and a rubric in words. The host decorates the prompt (section 7.5), so the prompt identity is the frozen text, never the user text the gateway receives.

Arms and order (spec 0.50). The arms are `direct` (the host's own provider entry for the external model), `relaymind_external_only` and `relaymind_auto` (the plugin with that execution mode, against a gateway launched with the same external model and the local model; relaymind_auto needs both, so without the external model or a usable local model it is not_run, and relaymind_external_only without a local model is launched without it and labeled so), and `relaymind_no_local` (the plugin in auto, against a gateway launched without a local model). Each arm has its own isolated host home and its own gateway; budgets and runtime defaults are the launcher's. The sampling order comes from the seed: in each repeat the tasks in a shuffled order, and for each task its arms in a shuffled order, so the arms of a task run next to each other. An arm that cannot run (no external credential, no local model) is recorded as not_run with its reason and keeps its place; the order of the other arms does not change.

Grading (spec 0.50). An attempt succeeds when the host ended within the time limit with exit status 0, its final answer contains every required evidence group and no forbidden evidence, the host reports each expected tool among its tool calls, the sandbox ends as expected, the host's reported provider and model are the arm's, and, in a RelayMind arm, the gateway started no tool, every run of the attempt is correlated, and each expected tool has a gateway tool proposal of that tool name (the name rule the host's tool report gets in every arm). The host's ok and status fields never decide a success (the pinned host reports ok true for a denied tool), and a host that ended with exit status 130 or died of SIGINT (on Windows, ended by Ctrl-C with STATUS_CONTROL_C_EXIT, 0xC000013A) is recorded as a host interrupt, apart from other exit statuses. An evaluation the operator interrupts still writes its export and report, wherever the interrupt lands (an arm's setup, an attempt, or its grading), marked aborted with the slot it stopped at, or with the arm whose setup it stopped; the attempts that ended are graded, and the interrupted slot and the slots after it, or with an interrupted setup that arm and every slot, are not run, never failures. An attempt the host answered through another provider or model is a host failover: it is counted apart and is never an arm success or a RelayMind answer. A failover needs a provider or model that the host reports and that differs from the arm's; a host report that names neither (the pinned host's error report has both null) is graded as a failed attempt, not a failover. The runs of an attempt are the runs the plugin's diagnostics records name (diagnosticsPath); a gateway run of the attempt's time window that no record names is reported as uncorrelated, and a record without a run ID stays unavailable. A gateway tool proposal is matched to the expected calls by tool name and arguments, and the result is reported, never a failure; a proposal alone is not a completed task. Both arms share one success rule, because a paired comparison compares their successes: the direct arm has no gateway trace, so no arm fails for a call's arguments or for a call outside the expected ones. Those calls are reported per arm and language instead: unexpected host tools (the host's reported tools that no expected call names) in every arm, and unexpected proposals (the gateway proposals that match no expected call by tool name and arguments) in a RelayMind arm, unknown in direct. The host's execution of the call is graded from the host's tool report and the sandbox, because neither the gateway trace nor the plugin's diagnostics records hold the host's tool result or its call ID; no check compares a call ID the gateway issued with the one the host replays (the pinned host rewrites it).

Report fields (spec 0.50). Per arm and language, with exact counts over every attempt (failed, timed-out, and retried ones included): successes, host failovers, timeouts, host interrupts, attempts the host called ok that failed, gateway runs, inference attempts (the diagnostics records; unknown in direct, where the host's assistant turn count is recorded as the host's statement), errors by code, cancellations, needs_external and context_limit_exceeded runs, completed runs without an external call (local turns), the reason codes of the runs that left the local stage (each handoff.started and each needs_external run.failed, such as context_limit or execution_mode), local and external model calls, unexpected host tools, and unexpected proposals (unknown in direct). Zero local turns is a result and is reported with those reasons, never left out; a local-inference comparison whose RelayMind arm had no local turn compares no local inference, and the report says so. Completion latency is the runner's clock around the host command, so it includes host startup and host tool time; first useful content is the gateway's clock, the elapsed_ms of the first answer text or complete tool proposal in the attempt's first run, and unknown in direct; the two clocks are never subtracted from each other. Each has p50 and p95 by nearest rank and its sample count. Tokens are counted per model from the gateway's model.finished usage, summed only over the calls that report a count, with the calls whose count is unknown counted beside the sum, over all attempts and over successful ones; host-reported usage stays apart under the host's label and is never added to a gateway count. The paired comparisons are relaymind_external_only against direct (integration overhead) and relaymind_auto and relaymind_no_local against relaymind_external_only (local inference), on the task and repeat pairs that both arms completed, beside both arms' success counts. Chinese results are reported apart. The export holds every attempt raw (the host's JSON report and output tail, the diagnostics records, the runs with their trace events, the sandbox after the attempt), the fixture digest, the machine, the application revision, the host, Node, plugin, and model identities, and the configuration; it never holds the gateway token or the external credential.

### 12.6 M10 multilingual assets and whole-task acceptance (spec 0.51)

**RM40** M10 MUST freeze reproducible original multilingual assets and independent relevance judgments and run conforming built-in/HTTP selectors through the same production v2 protocol. Compare compatible reports with full denominators; a deterministic substitute proves protocol replacement, not useful multilingual quality. Historical sections 12.2, 12.4 and 12.5 files/locks/grading remain unchanged.

Development baseline is 200 distinct skills: 100 original Chinese Matrix skills and 100 original English skills. Acceptance is that baseline plus 100 genuine distinct MCP tools, one 300-asset catalog, including at least 25 reviewed equivalent-function Chinese/English pairs. Identical duplicated assets or translated/generated stand-ins cannot satisfy scale/originality. Record IDs, source URLs, version/capture, original hashes, licenses/NOTICE, permitted acquisition, exact exclusions and language-review counts. Existing pinned SkillsBench assets may be an English starting point, with original English supplementation where domains do not overlap. ToolRet/RapidAPI fixtures are not proof of MCP provenance. If rights prohibit redistribution, lock original IDs/digests and use a permitted reproducible fetch or same-source operator export, not checked-in content. MATRIX-COHORT, MATRIX-OVERLAP and MATRIX-RIGHTS remain blocked until actual inputs qualify; one archive is not a baseline. MATRIX-REFRESH separately requires a permitted complete inventory/change/removal path, not a download URL.

Native Chinese/proficient English authors write prompts; two independent annotators separate from tuners judge acceptable alternatives, language and ambiguous/negative cases. Record pre-adjudication agreement and adjudication. Split related task/asset families across development and held-out test; freeze prompts/labels/artifact/settings before final measurement and hide test judgments from tuners. Budget real authorship/double annotation as delivery work. Repeated runs of one prompt are not independent cases. Each target can have multiple acceptable assets when genuinely equivalent; do not manufacture distinctions to improve scoring.

Required lanes are zh-Hans→zh-Hans, en→en, zh-Hans→en, en→zh-Hans and code-switch. Each has at least 200 independent positive prompts, 50 negatives/no-match and 30 ambiguous prompts. At least half of cross-language positives share no Latin word/identifier tokens with target searchable text; report those hard subsets independently under the same gates. Shared-name/API-token cases are separately labeled. Collect sufficient independent hard-subset counts before freeze to make confidence/coverage gates achievable; planned minima never waive a too-small or failing subset. Traditional Chinese is diagnostic only. Report by lane, asset kind, availability, scope, catalog size and retrieval mode; no overall average hides a failed lane.

Discovery gates apply to the full locked catalog with honest needs_setup/unknown/stale status; runtime eligibility has separate boundary tests and actual installed-asset host tasks. Importing a valid schema never makes every asset available. Keywords, misleading descriptions, injected commands, same-name collisions, changed/deleted assets, first import, cold start, rebuild, outage, embedding failure, stale choice and reset are explicit suites. No asset-need classifier is scored.

| Metric | Denominator and acceptance |
| --- | --- |
| Hit@3 | Positive prompts with an acceptable asset in the first three / all positives; failures/timeouts are misses. Lower two-sided 95% Wilson bound ≥0.90 in every lane and hard subset. |
| Confident top-1 precision | Correct confident top-1 / all confident recommendations, including false positives on negatives/missing assets. Lower two-sided 95% Wilson bound ≥0.95 in every lane and hard subset. |
| Confident coverage | Positives with confident output / all positives ≥0.50 per required lane/hard subset; user choices do not count as confident. |
| Ambiguous prompts | Frozen rubric scores supported alternatives/choice resolution separately; an unsupported confident selection fails, never becomes a relabeled positive. |
| Adaptation | At least 100 independent empty-state episodes: three distinct corrections, held-out paraphrases and unrelated controls, then reset. Corrected top-1 ≥90% of paraphrases; unrelated top-1 loss ≤2 percentage points versus identical no-adaptation arm. Missing-pool targets count as failures. Separate long-history eviction/scope/expiry/content-change/delete/injection tests and byte-identical semantic reset must pass. |

Report counts, intervals, false confidence, abstention and failures/timeouts beside all rates. Compare lexical, hybrid, adaptation off/on, and model selection only where its contract/input cap permits. Capped model experiments are labeled separately; inserting the answer into a shortlist is not end-to-end retrieval. Lexical fallback quality has its own thresholds/calibration and does not inherit hybrid quality.

The shared future CLI is `python scripts/asset_selection_benchmark.py run --config PATH --output PATH` and `python scripts/asset_selection_benchmark.py compare A B --output PATH --markdown PATH`. `compare --quality-only` explicitly permits different machines while suppressing performance superiority claims; it never permits different cases/labels/protocol. These are planned commands, not available tools in this revision. Use the production v2 validator/HTTP/coordinator/admission, no benchmark-only permissive interface or tool execution. Keep the existing v1 benchmark command/files.

Run configs freeze catalog/case/label locks, scope, selector identity/transport, candidate limit, budgets, seed, repetitions, warm-ups, fallback/adaptation policy and environment. Keep primary-only and service-level tracks distinct. Default replacement comparisons disable adaptation and share fallback policy. Export schema version, complete protocol/config/digests, model/index identities, environment, raw/final candidates/decisions, ordered attempts/fallback fraction, observed versus claimed cost and unknown coverage, per-case failures/timeouts and paired per-lane/kind/hard-subset statistics. Third-party receives the same full eligible projection, no favorable hidden shortlist. Comparison refuses incompatible catalog/cases/labels/scope/budgets/seed/repetitions/warm-ups/track/service settings with actionable reasons. Quality-only relaxes machine equality alone. Ship a deterministic HTTP v2 example and recorded equal-protocol run/compare results with malformed/timeout/identity/cancellation/incompatible-report tests; those results prove interchangeability only.

**RM44** Full M10 MUST pass quality, operational, whole-task and actual-platform gates against an intact host-native baseline. Missing/unknown evidence never passes, and retrieval-only metrics never replace whole-task measurement. No silent host context/tool pruning, permission weakening or budget increase is allowed.

At least 100 preregistered paired host tasks compare three arms: intact native OpenClaw/direct external provider; the same external model through RelayMind provider with recommendations off; that same provider with explicit recommendations/choice/use. Keep assets, models/provider, sampling, permissions and budgets equal; randomize paired arm order and retain every planned task, retry, failure, timeout and human wait/interaction. Record native search/catalog behavior first, without removing baseline tools. Count all input/output/cached/reasoning tokens with reported/observed/unknown coverage, external inference calls, success, CPU/memory, elapsed/active time and human waiting separately. Price-derived cost requires actual reported units. Preserve the existing local context and report whether any local turn occurred.

Versus native on the complete cohort, task-success gains MUST be at least losses and total external tokens and inference calls per planned task MUST NOT increase. Unknown cost cannot pass; claim a reduction only when measured. Report per-language tradeoffs and uncertainty. Early trial records costs/observations without claiming this final gate passed. A failed whole-task gate requires an explicit scope decision or a separately qualified/spec-revised supported pruning follow-up; cheap retrieval alone cannot close M10.

Operational protocol: start with the full committed 300-asset catalog and run ten clean process launches, each gateway start→first valid lexical recommendation ≤1,000 ms. An open socket/empty result/progress is insufficient. Each received local batch of 25 bounded metadata records and changed metadata becomes lexical within 1,000 ms including validation/commit. Report download, scan-detection and OpenClaw startup separately. During 60 seconds at two requests/second, concurrency one, import 100 records in batches of 25 during rebuild: warm service p95 ≤300 ms, no errors/timeouts, every batch visible within 1,000 ms, peak incremental memory ≤1 GiB above gateway without recommendations, including embeddings/workers/indexes. Count fallback and actual contention, preserving RM27 slow samples. Lexical-first development does not close this protocol before embedding/rebuild/inference contention is also measured. Resource gates apply to startup/rebuild/import/query workloads, not just an idle process. Slot timing, dispatch delay and job overlap rules follow in the two paragraphs below.

Operational scheduling and contention evidence (spec 0.53; RM16, RM38/AC29): schedule 120 request slots at t0 + n × 500 ms, n=0…119, without shifting or replaying missed slots. Record the scheduled time, actual dispatch time and terminal-response time. Dispatch may occur at or after its scheduled time, but the terminal response MUST arrive by that slot's original 500 ms deadline. Dispatch delay consumes the slot; scheduled-to-terminal-response client latency MUST also have p95 ≤300 ms. Report dispatch-to-response latency and service observed_ms separately, preserving their existing meanings and the service p95 gate. Keep delayed, missed and failed slots in the full denominator; no normalized timestamps or invented zero delays.

Source refresh and derived rebuild jobs may run sequentially on the single cooperative worker. Bind each of the four source jobs and the rebuild job to this workload and its valid stored event chain. Each source job MUST overlap its own receipt-to-visibility interval and at least one valid scheduled foreground query; the rebuild job MUST separately overlap at least one valid scheduled foreground query. Each required job MUST record observed generative warm-up or execution-slot contention. The foreground queries may differ: source and derived jobs need not overlap one another. Missing evidence remains unknown, never a pass. Lexical-only evidence does not close the embedding/rebuild/inference gate. Historical operational reports retain their original observations and grading revision; this revision does not turn prior fixture observations into qualification evidence.


Early trial requires the actual Apple M1 8 GiB macOS machine. Full M10 requires reproducible current packaged installation/use/privacy/restart/uninstall and operational acceptance on actual Apple Silicon macOS and a named native x86-64 Windows machine. Report OS/machine/artifacts/config and separate quality/device measurements. WSL2, CI, Linux functional fixtures, model doubles and historical M9 runs cannot substitute. Real interactive host allow/deny and genuine MCP round trip remain required. All missing platforms, permissions, source/label inputs and live tiers stay failed/not_run/blocked as appropriate; one M10 completion decision covers all required issues.

#### 12.6.1 Frozen retrieval inputs and judgments (spec 0.53; RM40/AC31)

The following closed contracts govern fixture, development and acceptance releases. Their schemas are planned implementation outputs under section 8.1. These definitions authorize loader and harness implementation before genuine inputs are available; they do not supply qualified assets, permissions, authors, annotations or held-out access controls. Fixture releases exercise infrastructure only. Development and acceptance releases qualify only when their actual evidence meets this section and section 12.6. Historical evaluation files and hash conventions remain unchanged.

Every shape below is closed; listed keys required, null allowed only where stated. JSON uses exact integers (not booleans), finite numbers, duplicate-key rejection, UTF-8, no executable fields. `ID` is nonempty ≤256 UTF-8 bytes; `H` is 64 lowercase SHA-256 hex; `Time` is UTC RFC3339 with Z; arrays described as sets are unique, sorted lexically by UTF-8 ID before hashing. Ordered `Case.conversation`, candidates, attempts, `planned_slots`, `results`, `Row.embeddings`, `Row.observed_costs`, `SelectorAttemptV1.cost_observations` and `EmbeddingObservationV1.cost_observations` retain order. Other arrays are sorted before hashing by these keys: `AssetCatalogSetV1.assets` by `record.asset_id`; `equivalent_pairs` by `(zh_asset_id, en_asset_id)`; `exclusions` by `(source_id, original_id, reason)`, a null `original_id` sorting before every string; `AssetCasesV1.cases` by `id`; `AssetLabelsV1.judgments` by `(case_id, annotator_id)`; `adjudications` by `case_id`; `agreement.groups` by `lane` (`all` first); `OfflineResolverV1.entries` by `(id, hash_kind, hash)`; `EffectiveConfigV1.sources` by `source_id`, `eligibility` by `asset_id`, `references` by `(role, document_id)`; `SourceProofV1.bindings` by `source_id`; `FileInventoryV1.files` by `path`; `AssetBenchmarkComparisonV1.reasons` by `(field, reason)`; every other array of objects with an `id` by `id`. Keys compare as UTF-8 bytes, element by element; duplicates are rejected. Every array whose field name ends in `_ids`, and `permissions`, `limits`, `original_languages`, `language_tags`, `requested_kinds`, `roles` and `competencies`, is a set: unique and sorted by UTF-8 bytes before hashing. `AssetBenchmarkReportV1.summaries` and `AssetBenchmarkComparisonV1.paired` are sorted by the `dimensions` tuple `(lane, kind, availability, stale, selection_scope, catalog_size, retrieval_mode, subset)`: strings by UTF-8 bytes, `catalog_size` numerically, and `stale` as null, then false, then true. `Summary.costs` sort by `(metric, provenance)`; `artifacts` by `(role, name, version, digest)`; `Environment.gpu` by `(model, device, cores)`. A null key part sorts before every string in all of these. `language_tag` follows section 14.5. `lane` means one of the six Case lane values below. Required lanes exclude zh_hant_diagnostic. `positive_int` means an integer greater than zero and `nonnegative_int` means an integer at least zero; booleans are never integers. `nonnegative_number` means a finite number at least zero. Text notes ≤8192 bytes; locators/relative paths ≤2048. Product records, requests, candidates, results, events and cost observations reuse the section 8.1 schemas and their limits, not looser benchmark copies.

Offline-reader limits: each catalog/case/label JSON ≤64 MiB, ≤10,000 cases, ≤1,000 catalog assets, ≤10,000 evidence entries; a report ≤256 MiB and ≤100,000 planned slots. Config/lock ≤4 MiB. Exceeding limits rejects explicitly; no truncation. Bounded file access refuses traversal and symlinks, checks size before allocation and rejects growth beyond the limit while reading; source originals remain under source limits, with no archive extraction or network fetch in the loader. These are safety bounds, not evidence that a genuine cohort fits. Reports exceeding limits need a reviewed format revision, never dropped rows.

Use existing `relaymind.canonical.canonical_digest` for structured documents (sorted keys, compact separators, non-ASCII unchanged, finite JSON), SHA-256 of exact bytes for original assets/evidence. Preserve this distinction: the existing corpus loader hashes canonical corpus/seed documents, while the historical selection benchmark also uses original-file hashes. Do not change either historical convention. Never hash a reserialized source and call it an original hash.

**Catalog, provenance and access evidence**

`AssetCatalogSetV1` = `{schema_version:1,dataset_id:ID,release:positive_int,purpose:fixture|development|acceptance,assets:[AssetEntry],people:[Person],equivalent_pairs:[Pair],exclusions:[Exclusion],evidence:[Evidence]}`.

- `AssetEntry` = `{record:AssetRecordV1,origin:matrix_original_zh|original_en|genuine_mcp|fixture|other_original,original_languages:[language_tag],asset_family_ids:[ID],original_evidence_id:ID,source_url:string,source_revision:string|null,rights:Rights,language_review_evidence_ids:[ID],provenance_evidence_ids:[ID]}`. Families nonempty. Record content_hash equals the exact original evidence SHA-256; schema_hash for MCP additionally matches its canonical tool schema. Record provenance/source version/capture and MCP server/tool namespace must match qualified evidence. Shared originals may legitimately contain multiple distinct tools: identical original hashes alone do not prove duplicate tools. Reject repeated source identities and canonical content/schema aliases representing the same asset; identical names on different genuine servers remain distinct. Semantic duplication still needs human review.
- `Rights` = `{acquisition:permitted|unknown|prohibited,evaluation:permitted|unknown|prohibited,retention:permitted|unknown|prohibited,redistribution:permitted|unknown|prohibited,evidence_ids:[ID],notice_evidence_ids:[ID]}`. These are recorded review assertions, not legal authentication. Real freeze requires evidence-supported acquisition/evaluation/retention permission; redistribution may be prohibited if content remains in a permitted local export. Unknown never means permitted. No runtime rights checking or new product permission is introduced.
- `Evidence` = `{id:ID,kind:original|license|notice|permission|source_provenance|language_review|authorship|independence|adjudication|split_review|access_control|derivation|qualification,access:redistributable|restricted,path:string|null,sha256:H,bytes:nonnegative_int}`. Paths, when present, resolve within the declared evidence root, with no absolute/traversal/symlink escape. Restricted evidence may have null path in public exports; the authorized custodian resolves its ID to supplied bytes offline. The lock commits its digest regardless. No automatic URL fetch and no secret URLs/credentials. Missing restricted bytes yield `unverified`, not verified qualification. Public manifests need not reveal private names or restricted content.
- `Person` = `{id:ID,roles:[author|annotator|adjudicator|tuner|custodian|reviewer],competencies:[native_zh|proficient_en],evidence_ids:[ID]}`. Opaque IDs plus custodian-reviewed evidence; a self-entered competency or ID is not proof of a native person. Empty competencies allowed for nonlanguage roles. Annotators/adjudicators used for held-out judgments cannot be tuners; two distinct actual people required per case. Do not infer identity independence merely from distinct strings.
- `Pair` = `{zh_asset_id:ID,en_asset_id:ID,reviewer_ids:[ID],evidence_ids:[ID]}`. At least one qualified review; distinct pair identity, original languages, genuine equivalent function, all acceptable alternatives retained. No generated translations to fill the quota.
- `Exclusion` = `{source_id:ID,original_id:ID|null,reason:ID,evidence_ids:[ID]}`. Record denied/oversized/unsupported/duplicate/language-excluded inputs, never silently replace them.

Development freeze checks exactly 100 original Chinese Matrix +100 original English skills. Acceptance checks those exact 200 source identities/content hashes plus 100 genuine MCP tools, exactly 300, and ≥25 reviewed equivalent-function pairs. A changed source version/content requires a new release and re-review, not silent baseline substitution. Fixture purpose cannot pass either check. Acquisition completeness/refresh and host availability remain separate gates.

**Cases, raw judgments and adjudication**

`AssetCasesV1` = `{schema_version:1,dataset_id:ID,release:positive_int,cases:[Case],split_review_evidence_ids:[ID]}`.

`Case` = `{id:ID,split:development|held_out|diagnostic,task_family_ids:[ID],target_asset_family_ids:[ID],lane:zh_hans_to_zh_hans|en_to_en|zh_hans_to_en|en_to_zh_hans|code_switch|zh_hant_diagnostic,task_text:string,conversation:[ConversationEntry from SelectionRequestV2],requested_kinds:[skill|mcp_tool],language_tags:[language_tag],code_switched:boolean,author_ids:[ID],authorship:original|translated|generated,authorship_evidence_ids:[ID],derived_from_case_id:ID|null,source_case_evidence_id:ID|null}`.

Product query caps apply. Families nonempty except target families may be empty for genuine negatives. A case's final acceptable targets must be covered by target_asset_family_ids. Translated/generated/diagnostic cases may exercise validation or diagnostics, but do not count as required original independent positives/negatives/ambiguous prompts. Chinese and code-switch authors need documented native Chinese competence; English prompts need proficient English competence. This contract does not provide genuine authored prompts.

Cases contain **no labels**. Lane labels control reporting, never a favorable catalog filter. Discovery gate runs offer the full locked catalog with both kinds; per-kind diagnostic requests are separate explicitly configured cohorts. Every acceptable alternative is judged against the full catalog, including other-language equivalents. Language lane does not force an artificial winner.

`AssetLabelsV1` = `{schema_version:1,dataset_id:ID,release:positive_int,cases_digest:H,rubric_version:relevance.v1,judgments:[Judgment],adjudications:[Adjudication],agreement:Agreement}`.

- `Judgment` = `{case_id:ID,annotator_id:ID,recorded_at:Time,class:positive|negative|ambiguous,acceptable_asset_ids:[ID],language_valid:boolean,independent_case:boolean,notes:string,independence_evidence_ids:[ID]}`. Exactly two immutable raw judgments per case, including subsequently excluded cases, made blind to each other's judgments and algorithm outputs; neither annotator is a tuner. Positive/ambiguous require nonempty acceptable sets; negative requires empty. Multiple equivalent acceptable assets do not by themselves make a prompt ambiguous: ambiguous means unresolved user intent where automatic confident commitment is unsupported.
- `Adjudication` = `{case_id:ID,adjudicator_id:ID,recorded_at:Time,class:positive|negative|ambiguous,acceptable_asset_ids:[ID],language_valid:boolean,independent_case:boolean,disposition:include|exclude,reason:string,evidence_ids:[ID]}`. Exactly one final row per case, including agreements/exclusions; raw rows never overwritten. Adjudicator may be one of the two annotators, disclosed, but not a tuner. Include requires true language_valid/independent_case and complete evidence. Exclusions frozen before measurement; changing them after seeing results creates a new release, not better old scores.
- Frozen ambiguous rubric: report (a) supported alternative in top three, (b) choice_required with supported alternatives, (c) unsupported confident output. Any confident automatic choice on an adjudicated unresolved-intent case is unsupported; do not count it as a positive or successful resolution. Actual later human resolution belongs to host tasks, not inferred from ranks.
- `Agreement` = `{method:double_annotation.v1,groups:[AgreementGroup]}`; `AgreementGroup` = `{lane:lane|all,paired_cases:nonnegative_int,class_agreements:nonnegative_int,acceptable_set_agreements:nonnegative_int,language_agreements:nonnegative_int,independence_agreements:nonnegative_int,joint_agreements:nonnegative_int,jaccard_sum:nonnegative_number}`. `jaccard_sum` is serialized as the shortest round-trip decimal of the sum of per-pair Jaccard values added in ascending `case_id` order with IEEE 754 binary64. Calculate over all raw paired cases before adjudication/exclusions, overall and each lane; exact-set equality, joint equality of all four answers. Jaccard is |intersection|/|union|, empty/empty=1. Report numerators/denominators; zero denominator gives null rates. Recompute and reject mismatched summaries: integer counts must match exactly and jaccard_sum may differ by at most 1e-12 times paired_cases when a loader recomputes it; the stored value is what `canonical_digest` commits, and a verifier compares the stored text, never a recomputed one, to the lock. No invented agreement pass threshold; spec requires recorded agreement, not a particular kappa threshold.

Leakage validation is over the locked development+held-out releases together: disjoint task-family and **labeled target-family** exposure, no shared normalized prompts (NFC, casefold, collapse Unicode whitespace), no cross-split derivation links; transitive family/paraphrase links must remain in one split. The full public catalog is common input and does not itself constitute a labeled training target. Record development targets in that ledger even when only threshold tuning used them. Actual family discovery/near-duplicate judgment requires split-review evidence; hashes cannot prove semantic independence or exclude unknown model pretraining. Freeze/record training overlap as unknown where not verifiable, never claim globally unseen model data.

Held-out prompts/judgments stay with an independent custodian until settings/artifacts freeze; judges' private labels never reach a selector, runtime environment accessible to it, or tuner checkout. Public hashes are not access controls. A readable `hidden:true` file fails this obligation. After final disclosure the release cannot be reused as unseen tuning evaluation.

Hard-subset derivation: `latin_tokens.v1` uses NFC+casefold; maximal runs of Unicode Latin letters, ASCII digits and underscore containing ≥1 Latin letter; pin Unicode database version in the lock. Compare the task text and each conversation entry's `text` (never its `role`) against every acceptable target's entire offered searchable projection: description, skill `identity.package_id` or MCP server/tool names, and tool-schema property names and `description` strings (not JSON Schema keywords or `type` values); no private indexed body. `no_shared_latin` requires disjoint token sets for **all** acceptable targets. Other cross-language positives are `shared_latin`; never drop them. Retain token intersections as recomputable diagnostic output, not author assertions. Required half/no-shared counts and the same confidence gates remain unchanged; mixed-script edge cases are validation tests. Hard-subset confident precision retains all negative and ambiguous cases in the corresponding cross-language lane alongside the no_shared_latin positive cases, so target-based hard-subset classification never discards their false confidence; hard-subset Hit@3 and confident-coverage denominators remain positive cases only.

**Lock closure, freeze and reproducibility**

`AssetLockV1` = `{schema_version:1,dataset_id:ID,release:positive_int,purpose:fixture|development|acceptance,state:provisional|frozen,frozen_at:Time|null,catalog:{path:string,digest:H},cases:{path:string,digest:H},labels:{path:string,digest:H},related_locks:[{id:ID,path:string,digest:H}],evidence:[{id:ID,sha256:H,bytes:nonnegative_int}],derivation:{revision:ID,config_digest:H,evidence_ids:[ID]},unicode_version:ID,qualification_evidence_ids:[ID],digest:H}`. Paths bounded relative within each explicitly supplied input root. Catalog/cases/labels references use canonical document digests; evidence uses original-byte hashes. `digest` is canonical_digest of this lock with only its own digest key removed; all linked documents/evidence/related release locks are transitively committed, no cycles. Labels commit the cases digest; dataset IDs/releases agree across the three documents and lock, and catalog/lock purposes agree. Provisional requires null frozen_at; frozen requires nonnull frozen_at. Referenced originals/evidence cannot fall outside the lock closure. Reject duplicate IDs, unknown targets/references, contradictory bytes/hashes and unused conflicting evidence entries.

Change any asset/case/raw judgment/adjudication/family/split/rubric/evidence/derivation input: new release plus recomputed lock. Formatting alone can leave canonical document hashes unchanged; exact source/evidence byte changes cannot. A genuine rights correction cannot be silently ignored just because metadata bytes match an old lock. No self-referential lock hash in its documents. Reports pin the lock digest; settings/artifacts freeze separately in the run configuration before held-out access.

For frozen qualification, every case has its two raw judgments, final adjudication and complete supporting evidence; declared people, roles, competence and independence require actual custodian review. Fixture/provisional inputs may exercise incomplete qualification paths and MUST remain visibly unqualified, while malformed structure, dangling references and digest mismatches are always rejected. Loading has two separate results: structural/digest verification versus externally reviewed qualification. A `frozen` field is a declaration requiring the named custodian/review record, not software-created authenticity. The loader may validate fixture/provisional infrastructure; acceptance export must mark unqualified or unavailable evidence blocked and cannot declare gates passed. A permitted export reproducibly yielding the exact originals is sufficient where redistribution is forbidden; no fake replacement content or invented fetch permission.

#### 12.6.2 Retrieval run and comparison exports (spec 0.57; RM35/RM40/RM44; AC26/AC31/AC36)

These closed contracts govern the shared future run/compare commands in section 12.6. Section 12.6.1's ID, H, Time, lane, numeric, UTF-8, duplicate-key, digest and product-schema conventions apply to every nested object below. All listed keys are required, nullable only where stated; no executable fields or unknown keys. The three schema paths in section 8.1 are future #200 implementation outputs. Publication authorizes implementation, not dataset/model qualification, activation, a numerical-gate change or an acceptance claim. Historical v1 corpus/protocol/benchmark files and their hash conventions remain unchanged.

**Configuration, bounds and production ownership**

`AssetBenchmarkConfigV1` = `{schema_version:1,lock:string,lock_digest:H,production_config:string,production_config_digest:H,split:development|held_out|diagnostic,track:primary_only|service,seed:uint32,repetitions:integer[1,100],warmups:integer[0,100],environment_lock:string,environment_lock_digest:H,preparation?:PreparationConfig}`. This is the closed TOML root. The optional preparation table is defined in section 12.6.4; omission expands its mode-none defaults before hashing or execution. uint32 is an exact integer in [0,4294967295]. Paths are nonempty relative paths, ≤2048 UTF-8 bytes, resolved against this config file; no shell expressions, absolute paths, traversal or symlink escape. The packaged JSON Schema describes the parsed TOML object, with the same exact types/closure; duplicate TOML keys are invalid. `production_config` is the existing launcher configuration parsed by its production settings validator, never a benchmark selector/config dialect. Its digest is the canonical redacted effective configuration including referenced artifact/config digests. Credentials and credential digests never enter exported configuration. `environment_lock` contains the Environment below; its digest is canonical. Observed runtime facts must match all declared known lock facts before measured execution; unknown fields remain unknown, never operator-filled measurements.

Config, environment lock and production config each have a 4 MiB input cap; reports and comparisons each have a 256 MiB cap. The corpus/lock/source limits of section 12.6.1 still apply. At most 100,000 planned slots, including warm-ups, and one result per slot. Check size before allocation and throughout bounded reads, rejecting growth beyond caps, nonordinary files and unsafe paths. No archive extraction, automatic fetch, silent truncation or discarded rows. Excess requires a reviewed format revision. Nested product arrays obey their published caps; benchmark arrays obey the slot, case, catalog, evidence and total-document byte limits above, with one summary per unique dimensions object. Artifact identities are unique by role/name/version/digest; no duplicate padding. Text notes/reason details obey the section 12.6.1 bounds; fixed reason IDs never contain exception text or credentials.

Freeze the full corpus, all included selected-split case IDs and the deterministic slot list before dispatch; no arbitrary --limit or favorable subset for gate runs. Pin disposable scope/permissions, requested kinds, budgets, source eligibility, selector identity/transport, adaptation/fallback policy, model/index configuration and compiled protocol/schema digest in the normalized export. Use a disposable evaluation scope and private DB, with no action credentials or customer identities. Discovery offers the full locked catalog with honest needs_setup/unknown/unavailable/stale state; never fabricate available/runtime assets. Runtime fixture validation is protocol-only, not qualified host evidence. Held-out access remains subject to section 12.6.1's actual custodian separation.

Primary-only requires fallback=false and adaptation disabled; it uses the exact production primary transport, boundary validation, coordinator and admission, with no floor. Service uses the recorded production policy. Default replacement comparisons require adaptation off in both arms and equal fallback policy. Use the production v2 settings parser, HTTP implementation, coordinator and admission; no benchmark-specific permissive validator, tool execution, injected answers, pruned catalog or retry. Empty catalog/zero attempts remains genuine evidence. Each attempt shares the pinned public catalog revision. Capture-time-only no-op semantics remain unchanged. Offered safe projection, task/conversation and permission envelope are byte-equivalent between comparison arms after removing request IDs and deadline countdown; retain their canonical content digests per case. Full indexed skill bodies, private labels and feedback never enter offered projections.

Slot order is warm-up repetitions first, then measured repetitions; repetitions are zero-based independently within each phase. Within each repetition sort cases by SHA-256 of canonical `{seed,phase,repetition,case_id}`, with case_id as tie-break. Ordinals are consecutive from one over the entire saved list; no implementation-specific PRNG. Each slot is attempted once. Retain all warm-ups, failures, cancellations and unstarted slots. Interruption emits explicit not_run rows for the remaining planned slots, never reruns successful subsets. **Measured repetition zero is the preregistered independent-case quality trial.** Later repetitions measure stability/latency, never enlarge Wilson denominators or replace a repetition-zero failure. All repetitions remain visible.

**Offline companion inputs and effective configuration**

A report adds required `input_manifest:{path:string|null,digest:H,bytes:nonnegative_int}`. Path, when present, is an ordinary relative path within the report directory, ≤2048 UTF-8 bytes; the writer convention is `<report-filename>.inputs.json`. Null means no public locator, never absent required evidence. The compare command additionally accepts `--inputs-a PATH`, `--inputs-b PATH` and `--resolver PATH`: each inputs option supplies that arm's exact manifest instead of its report-relative path, matching both committed digest and byte count; otherwise the declared report-relative path is the only lookup. No directory search, cwd inference, URL fetch, implicit checkout or fallback to a saved grade. A manifest is ≤4 MiB JSON and its digest is canonical_digest of the entire parsed manifest, with no report/self-digest field. Its raw byte count still matches the report reference. These are input-resolution options for the existing command, not a second comparison protocol.

`InputManifestV1` = `{schema_version:1,documents:[DocumentRef],evidence:[Evidence from section 12.6.1],source_proof_evidence_ids:[ID]}`. `DocumentRef` = `{id:ID,kind:benchmark_config|lock|catalog|cases|labels|effective_config|environment_lock|runner_inventory|protocol_inventory|referenced_config,path:string|null,digest:H,bytes:nonnegative_int,format:json|toml}`. Documents/evidence each ≤10,000 entries, unique IDs, sorted by ID; conflicting duplicate identities/digests/byte counts are rejected. Every referenced document/evidence ID resolves exactly once; unused conflicting entries, dangling links and circular digest dependencies are rejected. Evidence retains section 12.6.1's original-byte sha256/bytes/access/path rules, including null restricted paths. Document paths use the same bound/containment rules relative to the manifest directory, and null requires an authorized offline resolver. Config/effective-config/environment/inventory documents are ≤4 MiB each; corpus documents retain their ≤64 MiB caps and originals/source/code files retain the applicable source bound (ordinary code file ≤4 MiB). The companion/closure definitions are nested in the three already declared benchmark schemas, with offline schema references; no unrelated schema package is introduced.

There is exactly one benchmark_config, effective_config, environment_lock, runner_inventory and protocol_inventory document.
