Those document IDs equal their kind; each lock/catalog/cases/labels/referenced_config ID is its kind plus `:` plus its digest. The root lock is the lock matching report.lock_digest; related_locks and all lock catalog/cases/labels references resolve by kind and digest in this inventory, preserving the complete section 12.6.1 lock/evidence closure. Lock path strings remain bounded logical names; compare uses the manifest/resolver binding, not an unapproved path outside it. Benchmark_config is the parsed closed AssetBenchmarkConfigV1 object (JSON or TOML) with its three locator values normalized to `lock.json`, `production.toml`, `environment.json`; these logical names bind the root lock, effective_config and environment_lock respectively and are not followed at comparison time. All nonlocator values remain exact. Its lock_digest/production_config_digest/environment_lock_digest must match the corresponding verified documents and report/config. Normalizing locator names for export does not authorize a new runtime config dialect or change the original configuration used by production parsing.

Every document digest hashes its JSON semantic value: strict JSON parsing, or strict TOML parsing followed by the same closed field/type validator and canonical JSON digest. TOML dates/times, duplicate keys, nonfinite values and values outside the named JSON-compatible shapes are rejected. Original evidence/code-file hashes use exact bytes, never canonical reserialization; every entry's bytes is the actual raw count. Formatting may preserve a document's canonical digest while changing bytes, so a differently formatted supplied document requires a matching new manifest byte commitment. Referenced_config documents are only the actual schema-validated config/manifest preimages needed by the configured artifacts; their schema/version is committed by the protocol inventory, not an open benchmark settings bag. Binary artifact originals belong to original-byte evidence, never an invented JSON model manifest. The manifest need not contain artifact weights when an actual reviewed provenance source suffices for the applicable claim, but missing required provenance remains unknown/blocked.

`OfflineResolverV1` = `{schema_version:1,entries:[{id:ID,hash_kind:canonical|original,hash:H,bytes:nonnegative_int,path:string}]}`. It is an explicitly supplied local JSON file ≤4 MiB, at most 10,000 unique `(id,hash_kind,hash)` entries, sorted by that complete tuple. Paths are nonempty relative paths ≤2048 UTF-8 bytes, rooted at the resolver's own directory; no absolute/traversal/symlink escape. Lookup uses the complete committed `(id,hash_kind,hash)` tuple, never a first match by ID; the entry must additionally match the committed byte count. Same-ID/hash-kind entries with different committed hashes may coexist for different arms. Reject duplicate triples, conflicting byte counts and hash/path mismatches. A resolver can supply only committed null-path references or explicitly relocate a matching committed reference; a mismatch is failure, not another search. Source-code inventory file IDs are `file:` plus SHA-256 of the UTF-8 relative POSIX inventory path, with original hash kind; the entry hash still hashes actual file bytes. Validate ordinary-file identity and growth bounds throughout each read and all existing lock/digest/qualification rules after resolution. CLI-supplied top-level manifest/resolver filenames may locate custodian-controlled roots explicitly; references inside them remain bounded. An authorized custodian controls actual access to that directory; possession of IDs, hashes or a resolver filename does not grant permissions.

Export never copies held-out/private labels, source paths, secret configuration or restricted evidence into a public bundle or tuner checkout. A manifest may expose only the committed opaque ID/hash/size with path=null for those inputs; compare runs in the authorized custodian context with an explicitly supplied resolver. Required ground truth, split/authorship/qualification or source-policy proof that cannot be resolved makes comparison incompatible with fixed safe `input_unresolved` or `evidence_unverified` field reasons, nonzero exit and no paired grades; neither saved summaries nor --quality-only can bypass it. Restricted bytes may be verified in place without export. Qualification remains reviewed evidence, not a successful hash check or operator-entered assertion.

`EffectiveConfigV1` = `{schema_version:1,generator:{runner_digest:H,protocol_digest:H},recommendations:{enabled:boolean,user_id:ID|null,workspace_id:ID|null,total_ms:positive_int,primary_ms:positive_int,primary:lexical|hybrid|http,name:ID|null,version:ID|null,url:string|null,fallback:boolean},permissions:[ID],selection_scope:discovery|runtime,requested_kinds:[skill|mcp_tool],budgets:RecommendationBudgetsV1,job_budgets:IndexJobBudgetsV1,sources:[SourcePolicy],refresh:{local_scan_seconds:positive_int,remote_seconds:positive_int,backoff_initial_seconds:positive_int,backoff_max_seconds:positive_int},eligibility:[Eligibility],references:[{role:primary_model|embedding|adaptation|index_config,document_id:ID}],adaptation:{enabled:boolean,config_digest:H|null},artifacts:[ArtifactIdentity]}`.

This is the exact sanitized **recommendation-effective projection** generated from the production launcher/settings parser plus actual trusted scope, permissions and admission policy. It is not the unrelated chat/server/store configuration or the existing narrower RecommendationService event config digest; no existing product hash changes. `production_config_digest` in benchmark config/NormalizedConfig is canonical_digest of this entire EffectiveConfigV1. Expand every listed default from the parser and product budgets functions; values must agree with NormalizedConfig, artifact descriptors and actual requests. Recommendation scope/name/version/url retain the actual parsed nullable configured values; enabled=true still requires nonnull scope IDs and explicit sources under the production validator. Nonexecuting invalid benchmark configuration is refused before planning; do not invent scope IDs to serialize a disabled/missing-scope production config. Recommendation name/version/url retain their configured values; NormalizedConfig.selector separately records the resolved built-in/HTTP identity. HTTP URL is the production-validated credential/query/fragment-free loopback URL (≤2048 bytes), null for an unconfigured URL. Budgets retain exact section 14.5 values; refresh backoff_max≥backoff_initial. Enabled hybrid/adaptation still require their separately qualified real configs; this projection supplies no activation. References list exactly the primary/embedding/adaptation/index artifact/config document preimages actually used; a nonnull Environment.runtime_config_digest resolves separately to referenced_config by kind and digest; adaptation off requires null config_digest, on requires the referenced digest. Arrays are sorted unique by their identity keys; permissions/kinds use set order, sources by source_id, eligibility by asset_id, references by role/document_id, artifacts by role/name/version/digest. Machine/runtime facts and their config digests remain in Environment, with any required runtime-config preimage supplied as referenced_config; no full launcher credential or filesystem-path dump is needed.

`SourcePolicy` = `{source_id:ID,kind:local_directory|metadata_import|qualified_remote,locator_id:ID,adapter_contract:ID|null,proof_evidence_ids:[ID]}`. `Eligibility` = `{asset_id:ID,content_hash:H,schema_hash:H|null,eligible:boolean,proof_evidence_ids:[ID]}`. SourcePolicy covers each actual parsed configured source, and Eligibility covers **every locked asset**, including excluded-by-policy records; schema_hash is canonical MCP schema hash or null for a skill. Eligible is the actual production source/publication/operator-policy result for this scope/permissions/selection_scope, not a user label. Discovery gate inputs retain the required full common catalog; a policy-pruned cohort cannot claim that gate. No qualified_remote adapter becomes enabled merely by naming it here. Locator_id is a stable custodian-assigned alias for the actual configured ordinary source root/file or qualified endpoint; use no path-derived hash. Actual source locators, secret_ref names, environment variable names/values, credentials and their hashes are excluded from this projection. Secret configuration is never represented by a hash or placeholder that lets a changed secret masquerade as an observable policy difference.

`SourceProofV1` = `{schema_version:1,effective_config_digest:H,bindings:[{source_id:ID,locator_id:ID,locator:string}],policy_evidence_ids:[ID],reviewer_ids:[ID]}`. It is ≤4 MiB strict JSON, committed as original-byte Evidence of kind=source_provenance with access=restricted/path=null in public manifests; source_proof_evidence_ids resolve it plus the reviewed parser/policy evidence. Bindings are unique by source_id, sorted, cover exactly SourcePolicy, and use actual resolved absolute ordinary-file/directory locators (or the genuinely qualified credential-free endpoint), ≤2048 bytes, verified privately by the custodian against actual production parser input and source captures. This private locator text is an explicit exception to the public relative-reference rule; it is data, never an automatically opened/fetched path. Policy evidence records actual configured-source matching, full-record eligibility and parser-default expansion, tied to generator inventories and the effective-config digest, with no credential fields or secret-bearing original-config hash. Comparator verifies the resolved proof's exact bindings/digest/evidence closure and reviewed provenance; software cannot establish authenticity from opaque reviewer IDs. Unverifiable origin/default/policy binding is evidence_unverified, not qualified. The proof's effective_config_digest is an attestation backlink, not a recursive digest dependency: the effective document commits opaque proof IDs only, and the outer manifest commits the resulting proof bytes. This supplies private provenance without publishing raw launcher configuration or changing source authority.

The compatibility policy projection is exactly `{recommendations:{enabled,user_id,workspace_id,total_ms,primary_ms,fallback},permissions,selection_scope,requested_kinds,budgets,job_budgets,sources,refresh,eligibility,adaptation}` from EffectiveConfigV1, with source proof_evidence_ids/eligibility proof_evidence_ids omitted only from **equality comparison**, never evidence verification. Source aliases/adapter contracts, actual eligible identities/hashes, all safety limits and policies remain equal. Permitted replacement variables are recommendations.primary/name/version/url and references/artifacts, reported as the intended selector/artifact experiment; their proofs still verify. Generator digests remain equal via implementation invariants. No differing source policy may hide inside the allowed whole-effective-config digest difference.

`FileInventoryV1` = `{schema_version:1,files:[{path:string,sha256:H,bytes:nonnegative_int}]}`. Files are unique, sorted by relative POSIX repository path (≤2048 bytes), ordinary files, at most 10,000; inventory ≤4 MiB. Original-file bounds/resolution above apply. Runner inventory includes exactly `pyproject.toml`, `scripts/asset_selection_benchmark.py` and every `.py` file recursively under `src/relaymind/`, excluding generated caches; protocol inventory includes exactly `docs/SPECIFICATION.md` plus every `.json` packaged schema recursively under `src/relaymind/`. No secret/generated-data/held-out file enters either inventory. Each report implementation digest and EffectiveConfigV1.generator digest is canonical_digest of the corresponding full inventory. The inventory files themselves and reports are not inventory members, so no self-reference. Supplied source bytes recompute every file hash/count and the compiled runner/protocol identity; missing/extra membership, unknown generator or mismatched hashes prevent verified comparison. This pins both the sanitizer/default-expansion implementation and the grading/schema implementation without trusting a hash whose preimage is unavailable.

**Closed report records and identity**

`AssetBenchmarkReportV1` = `{schema_version:1,benchmark:asset_selection,created_at:Time,status:completed|interrupted|blocked,qualification:fixture|provisional|qualified,qualification_evidence_ids:[ID],lock_digest:H,input_manifest:{path:string|null,digest:H,bytes:nonnegative_int},config:NormalizedConfig,config_digest:H,preparation:PreparationRecord,environment:Environment,environment_digest:H,implementation:{checkout:ID,dirty:boolean|null,runner_digest:H,protocol_digest:H},artifacts:[ArtifactIdentity],planned_slots:[Slot],results:[Row],summaries:[Summary],limits:[ID]}`.

`NormalizedConfig` = `{split:development|held_out|diagnostic,track:primary_only|service,seed:uint32,repetitions:integer[1,100],warmups:integer[0,100],scope:{user_id:ID,workspace_id:ID},permissions:[ID],selection_scope:discovery|runtime,requested_kinds:[skill|mcp_tool],selector:{name:ID,version:ID,transport:in_process|http},budgets:RecommendationBudgetsV1,fallback:boolean,adaptation_enabled:boolean,adaptation_config_digest:H|null,production_config_digest:H,preparation:PreparationConfig}`. Permissions and requested kinds are unique sets; kinds nonempty. Scope/permissions come from trusted production policy. Fallback equals budgets.fallback_enabled. Adaptation disabled requires null adaptation_config_digest; enabled requires its actual frozen digest. Gate discovery requests use both kinds and the full catalog. No endpoint secrets. Selector-specific settings remain committed by referenced artifact/config digests; compatibility compares the named invariant fields, not equality of entire selector-specific production configs.

`Environment` = `{os:ID,os_version:ID|null,architecture:ID,cpu:ID|null,ram_bytes:nonnegative_int|null,gpu:[{model:ID|null,device:ID|null,cores:ID|null}]|null,python:ID,dependencies_digest:H,runtime:ID|null,runtime_version:ID|null,runtime_config_digest:H|null}`. GPU entries are unique model/device/cores triples within the document byte cap. Populate from actual collectors/logs, reusing evaluation.environment without changing its historical export shape: `architecture` is its `machine` value, and each GPU entry maps `model` and `cores` as reported (null when absent) and sets `device` to null, since the collectors do not report one; `cores` keeps the reported text. Unknown is null; [] GPU means observed absence. Null runtime/version/config together may mean no such runtime is used only when corroborated by the production configuration; otherwise it is unknown. Unknowns cannot prove a matching performance environment. Exclude paths, hostnames, serial numbers and secret configuration.

`ArtifactIdentity` = `{role:primary_model|embedding|adaptation|index_config,name:ID,version:ID,digest:H|null,provenance:observed|reported|unknown}`. Unknown provenance requires null digest; observed/reported digest may remain null when unavailable. No fabricated digest or model-manifest format. Record all applicable configured artifacts; an absent local model for lexical selection does not require an invented artifact. Model/index identity, license and actual qualification remain separately evidenced.

`Slot` = `{phase:warmup|measured,repetition:nonnegative_int,case_id:ID,ordinal:positive_int}`.

`Row` = `{slot:Slot,status:ready|failed|cancelled|not_run,reason:ID|null,request_digest:H|null,offered_projection_digest:H|null,catalog_revision:nonnegative_int|null,result:RecommendationResultV1|null,attempts:[SelectorAttemptV1],embeddings:[EmbeddingObservationV1],observed_ms:nonnegative_number|null,observed_costs:[CostObservationV1],resources:{cpu_ms:nonnegative_number|null,peak_bytes:nonnegative_int|null,coverage:complete|partial|unknown},grade:Grade}`. CostObservationV1 names the existing closed section 8.1 cost observation `{provenance:observed|reported,value:CostV1}`, not a new product wire shape. No loosening of RecommendationResultV1, SelectorAttemptV1 or EmbeddingObservationV1.

Exactly one Row per planned Slot, in saved slot order; reject duplicate/extra/missing rows and wrong phase/repetition/case/ordinal. Ready requires nonnull admitted result and null reason; its catalog revision, ordered attempts and embeddings agree with that result. Other statuses require nonnull fixed safe reason and null result, retaining prior actual attempts/embeddings/costs. Not_run means no dispatch/work: attempts, embeddings and observed_costs empty, request/projection/revision/timing/resource values null and resource coverage unknown. Predispatch failures may also have null request/projection/revision; never fabricate a result, call or zero cost. When an offer was constructed, request_digest hashes the canonical redacted SelectionRequestV2 with recommendation_id, task_id and deadline_ms removed, and offered_projection_digest hashes its exact ordered catalog projection; other request fields stay bound. Catalog revision must match actual offer/attempt/result evidence, including revision zero for a valid initial empty_catalog result; never synthesize revision one. Product-permitted raw rejected result evidence stays in attempts, never becomes an admitted answer. No feedback text/private derivatives, credentials or credential digests in exports.

`config_digest` hashes the entire NormalizedConfig; `environment_digest` hashes Environment. `lock_digest` resolves to the verified full transitive input closure of section 12.6.1. `runner_digest` commits the actual runner implementation and `protocol_digest` commits the protocol/schema implementation used, with a reproducible sorted relative-file/digest inventory; no self-referential report hash. A dirty checkout is recorded, never hidden by its checkout ID. A null `dirty` means git could not say; it is unknown, never clean, and cannot prove a matching implementation. Qualification is assessed with reasons/evidence independently of structural validity or status: fixture inputs yield fixture, incomplete/unverified actual evidence yields provisional; qualified requires reviewed freeze/evidence and matched execution identities, not a user flag. IDs resolve to supplied lock/run evidence under the same offline original-byte rules. `limits` records fixed safe limitation IDs, including missing qualification/identity/environment/cost coverage. Blocked execution still conserves every planned slot as actual evidence or not_run. Completed means every planned slot reached ready/failed/cancelled, not that quality gates passed; interrupted/blocked cannot claim completed acceptance.

**Grading, counts and costs**

`Grade` = `{class:positive|negative|ambiguous,hit3:boolean,confident:boolean,confident_correct:boolean,ambiguous_supported3:boolean,ambiguous_choice:boolean,false_confident:boolean}`. Class is the frozen included adjudication. Grade only final admitted output: hit3 iff positive and any first-three final candidates is acceptable; confident iff the admitted final decision is confident; confident_correct iff confident, positive and final top1 acceptable. False_confident iff confident and not confident_correct, including every confident negative or unresolved-intent ambiguous case. Ambiguous_supported3 iff ambiguous and a supported alternative is in the first three; ambiguous_choice iff that is true and final decision is choice_required. Failure/cancelled/not_run has all grade booleans false and remains a positive miss where labeled positive. All acceptable alternatives remain labels; unresolved intent is never promoted to resolved success. Grade and summaries are recomputed, never trusted from the report.

Preserve the configured primary's actual attempt result for primary metrics, independently of service final/base output. Only an admitted usable primary result can earn a primary hit/confident success; invalid/stale/rejected primary results cannot, even when syntactically retained. Its failed/absent attempt is a miss. Floor success never repairs a primary miss. Base/final ranks and decisions remain the exact RecommendationResultV1 fields; never relabel a floor as primary. Empty catalog has no producer or attempt. Failed/cancelled service rows retain all incurred primary/floor work.

`Summary` = `{dimensions:{lane:lane|all,kind:skill|mcp_tool|all,availability:available|needs_setup|unknown|all,stale:boolean|null,selection_scope:discovery|runtime,catalog_size:nonnegative_int,retrieval_mode:primary|service,subset:all|no_shared_latin|shared_latin},planned:nonnegative_int,ready:nonnegative_int,failed:nonnegative_int,cancelled:nonnegative_int,not_run:nonnegative_int,positive:nonnegative_int,negative:nonnegative_int,ambiguous:nonnegative_int,hit3:Rate,confident_precision:Rate,confident_coverage:Rate,false_confident:nonnegative_int,abstained:nonnegative_int,ambiguous_supported3:Rate,ambiguous_choice:Rate,primary_failures:nonnegative_int,fallback_used:nonnegative_int,fallback_fraction:Rate,latency:{known:nonnegative_int,unknown:nonnegative_int,p50_ms:nonnegative_number|null,p95_ms:nonnegative_number|null},costs:[CostCoverage],gate:pass|fail|blocked|not_applicable}`.

Retrieval mode IDs are `primary` and `service`, naming configured-primary and final admitted service quality respectively. Emit both for overall, each populated lane, required cross-language hard/shared subsets and populated kind/availability/stale diagnostic subgroups. Quality class counts, rates, false_confident and abstained use only measured repetition zero; positive+negative+ambiguous equals that cohort's number of independent cases. In contrast planned/ready/failed/cancelled/not_run, primary_failures/fallback_used/fallback_fraction, latency and costs cover **all measured repetitions** in that dimension. Planned equals ready+failed+cancelled+not_run; ready counts rows with status ready, not task success. These workload status counts match the actual service Row statuses for both modes; primary_failures counts slots where a dispatched primary did not produce an admitted usable result. No primary attempt on empty catalog is not a dispatched-primary failure. Primary quality is recomputed from attempt evidence even when final Row status differs. Later-repetition stability is exposed by the full rows and can be rendered per repetition, with no pooled independent-case Wilson claim. Warm-ups are retained and labeled in rows, excluded from every measured Summary field.

`Rate` = `{numerator:nonnegative_int,denominator:nonnegative_int,value:nonnegative_number|null,wilson95:{lower:nonnegative_number,upper:nonnegative_number}|null}`. 0≤numerator≤denominator. For n>0 value=x/n; for independent-case quality rates Wilson95 is two-sided with z=1.959963984540054: center=(p+z²/(2n))/(1+z²/n), half=z*sqrt(p*(1-p)/n+z²/(4n²))/(1+z²/n), lower=max(0,center-half), upper=min(1,center+half). Values/bounds finite in [0,1]. Zero denominator requires null value and interval, never a pass. No rounding before thresholds and no invented significance test.

Hit3 denominator is all independent positives; confident precision denominator is all actual confident outputs in that cohort, including negatives/ambiguous false confidence; numerator is confident_correct. Coverage numerator is positive confident outputs, denominator all positives, regardless of correctness. Ambiguous_supported3 and ambiguous_choice denominators are all ambiguous cases. Genuine admitted no_match/empty_catalog counts abstention; failure never does. Fallback fraction numerator is measured slots with an actual distinct gateway-floor dispatch, denominator all measured planned slots; a configured lexical primary is not its own fallback. Fallback fraction is a workload ratio, with wilson95=null even when its denominator is positive; repeated slots do not acquire an independent-case interval. No other interval pools repeated observations. The existing lower bounds ≥0.90 Hit@3 and ≥0.95 confident precision, ≥0.50 positive confident coverage, independent counts and ambiguous rubric are unchanged. Required lane/hard gates use repetition zero only and cannot be replaced by overall/subgroup averages. A known failed bound, unsupported confident ambiguous output or unmet required minimum fails; missing qualification/required evidence or a zero required denominator blocks a pass. Diagnostic-only subgroup gates are not_applicable. A report can demonstrate fixture protocol behavior without passing real development/acceptance gates.

Dimension membership comes from pinned requests/config: lane, selection_scope, full catalog size and retrieval mode. Positive/ambiguous kind/availability/stale subgroups use membership of **all acceptable assets**; a case may belong to several and subgroup totals are labeled non-additive. Negative cases enter only kind=all, availability=all, stale=null (no invented target). Availability=all includes unavailable; unavailable targets remain in overall/lane gates even though the fixed diagnostic enum has no separate unavailable bucket. Cross-language no_shared_latin membership derives exactly as section 12.6.1: hard Hit@3/coverage use no-shared positives; hard confident precision conservatively also retains all lane negatives and ambiguous cases, so classification never removes their false confidence. Shared_latin positives remain separately visible. No Latin token or availability filtering may reduce the required full-catalog gates.

Latency uses nearest-rank quantiles on all known measured-slot observed_ms durations, including failed/cancelled work: sorted values, rank ceil(q*n), q=.50/.95. With no known values p50/p95 are null; not_run/unknown never zero. Known+unknown equals measured planned slots. All latency/quantile values are finite nonnegative; never serialize positive infinity to conceal a timeout. Deadline/timeouts retain actual observed duration when available and reason/attempt outcome; unknown duration stays null. CPU/resource coverage comes from actual measurements: complete requires both metrics observed across the entire slot, partial records incomplete coverage, unknown means no verified resource coverage. No unavailable observation is synthesized as a zero.

`CostCoverage` = `{metric:input_tokens|output_tokens|cached_tokens|reasoning_tokens|calls|cpu_ms|peak_bytes,provenance:observed|reported,known:nonnegative_int,unknown:nonnegative_int,total:nonnegative_number|null,known_subtotal:nonnegative_number|null}`. Unique metric/provenance pairs; counts are measured-row coverage, known+unknown=planned. For a row/metric, known requires complete coverage of that metric across its actual owned work (including failed primary, fallback, retries if present in source evidence and embeddings); no-dispatch zero is known only when actually established, not merely because attempts is empty. Total is the sum for additive metrics only if every row is known; peak_bytes uses maximum, not sum. Counts/tokens/calls/bytes require nonnegative integral totals; cpu_ms may be a finite nonnegative number. Any unknown component makes complete total null. A zero-row cohort has total=null and known_subtotal=null. A zero established by actual no-inference evidence is a known component; an absent observation alone is not. known_subtotal is the sum of every known owned raw component for an additive metric, or their maximum for peak_bytes, even when some rows have incomplete metric coverage; the same nonnegative/integral metric rules apply. It is null when no component is known, including an empty cohort; do not invent zero. When all components/rows are completely known, known_subtotal equals total; otherwise total stays null and the subtotal is labeled partial. A partial subtotal never establishes a cost pass or complete bill.

Observed and reported provenance never sum together. Count unique owned inference observations once: selector claims are reported only; embedding work contained in a measured attempt is not added again to that attempt's inclusive observation. CPU/peak resource observations also must not be counted again through costs. Retain raw product identities linking local embedding/external inference and actual work; cost summaries cannot assert whole-task external cost or treat cached/reasoning counts as disjoint without source evidence. Primary and floor provenance/cost remain visible separately in attempts and rows. Unknown coverage blocks any cost pass/superiority claim. Retrieval costs are not whole-task costs.

**Comparison and narrow exception**

The future compare command validates both reports and their supplied input/evidence closures, config/environment/report digests, slot completeness and product boundary checks, then recomputes grades, dimensions, counts and intervals. Reject corrupt, contradictory or unresolvable reports rather than comparing trusted summaries. Compare canonical whole-report hashes, with no self-hash field.

`AssetBenchmarkComparisonV1` = `{schema_version:1,reports:{a:H,b:H},arms:{a:ArmDescriptor,b:ArmDescriptor},mode:replacement|quality_only,preparation:{a:PreparationRecord|null,b:PreparationRecord|null},status:compatible|incompatible,reasons:[{field:string,reason:ID}],performance_comparable:boolean,paired:[PairedSummary],gate_status:pass|fail|blocked|not_applicable}`. Reason field is a bounded ≤2048 UTF-8-byte fixed JSON Pointer-style field path (side a/b as needed), never a source value/secret; reasons sorted by field then reason and unique. A valid report's H is canonical_digest of the entire report. Incompatible output still records each parsed input's canonical hash; malformed non-document input is rejected before a comparison object can be formed, with a fixed safe CLI diagnostic. For structurally valid incompatible inputs, write the machine-readable rejected object and return nonzero; status=incompatible, nonempty reasons, performance_comparable=false, paired=[], gate_status=blocked. Compatible has reasons=[], and performance_comparable only when the normal performance equality/coverage rules hold. Copy each verified report preparation record into comparison.preparation for self-contained setup/readiness/cost rendering; null is allowed only for an unverifiable record in an incompatible comparison, never to represent stateless zero work. Markdown is rendered from this object alone, with no separate scoring logic.

`ArmDescriptor` = `{selector:{name:ID,version:ID,transport:in_process|http},artifacts:[ArtifactIdentity]}`. Copy and verify each descriptor against its corresponding hashed source report; keep it even for structurally valid incompatible inputs. The Comparison alone contains the arm identities, intended artifact variable, both arms' cost known_subtotal/coverage through paired summaries and all values needed by its exclusive Markdown renderer. No renderer source-report read or hash lookup is needed. Quality-only still labels every performance/cost value not comparable and suppresses superiority statements.

`PairedSummary` = `{dimensions:Summary.dimensions,cases:nonnegative_int,a_only_hit:nonnegative_int,b_only_hit:nonnegative_int,both_hit:nonnegative_int,neither_hit:nonnegative_int,hit3_difference:number|null,confident_precision_difference:number|null,confident_coverage_difference:number|null,a:Summary,b:Summary}`. Pair exact measured-repetition-zero case IDs within each shared dimension, not matching by position or dropping failures. Cases counts all paired independent cases in that dimension; the four discordant/agreement counts partition those cases exactly, with dual failures in neither_hit and all negatives/ambiguous lacking positive hit3. Differences are b.value−a.value for the named rates and null if either rate is null; finite in [-1,1]. Preserve both arms' full Summary and Wilson intervals; no invented significance or superiority test. Comparative gate_status reports the unchanged required absolute gates: fail on a required known failure, blocked when missing/unknown evidence prevents deciding, pass only when both arms' applicable required gates pass, not_applicable for purely diagnostic cohorts. It does not assert that b is better than a.

Compatibility requires identical transitive lock/catalog/cases/labels, included selected-split case IDs, scope/permissions/source eligibility, selection_scope/requested kinds, per-case offered projection/request content digests, budgets, seed/slot order/repetitions/warmups, runner/protocol digest, track, fallback policy and adaptation settings. A genuinely absent predispatch digest is unknown, not null==null proof of equivalent offers; immutable verified inputs/config may establish the planned envelope, with missing observed-offer coverage retained as a limitation and no invented observation. Default replacement compare requires adaptation disabled in both arms. Different selector/artifact/index identity is the intended variable, explicitly reported; do not demand equality of entire selector-specific production_config_digest. All other envelope and policy invariants remain equal, including transport behavior admitted under the same production v2 contract. Unknown artifact provenance/digest blocks relevant reproducibility claims, never equality by two nulls. Qualified comparisons require actually verified identities, not reported claims alone.

Normal replacement comparison requires equal known performance-relevant Environment facts, dependencies/runtime configuration and Python version; unknown required facts prevent performance comparability and are reported as incompatibility reasons. A confirmed inapplicable runtime or absent GPU can match as such; unknown cannot. `--quality-only` changes mode to quality_only and relaxes **machine fields alone**: os/os_version/architecture/cpu/ram_bytes/gpu. It still requires equal runner/protocol/dependencies/Python/runtime settings, cases/labels, seed/order/budgets and all service policies above. performance_comparable=false; retain raw measured latency/resource/cost quantities and label them not comparable in rendered output, suppressing all performance/cost superiority statements. Neither mode relaxes original source qualification or independent-case minima.

Adaptation-policy mismatches remain rejected by ordinary retrieval replacement comparison. Section 12.6.5 defines the separate AC33 off/on paired-ablation contract and whole-task/platform exports; ≥100 three-correction episodes, held-out probes, unrelated controls and reset are not ordinary retrieval cases. These retrieval files alone cannot close AC33/AC36/AC37. The separate contracts change no numerical threshold or host/model/source authority.

**Native runtime applicability (spec 0.61; RM16/RM35/RM40/RM41/RM44; AC26/AC31/AC32).** This paragraph refines only the runtime applicability/equality test above; no Environment, ArtifactIdentity, Row or source-proof field is added. Resolve the actual configured primary and every applicable section 6.1 manifest/section 14.5.1 semantic preimage through the existing EffectiveConfigV1 references, artifacts and offline companion rules. A configured in-process hybrid is local inference, not a lexical selector identified merely by transport=in_process. Its Environment.runtime/runtime_version identify the observed backend/version, and runtime_config_digest commits the full validated EmbeddingManifestV1 preimage as referenced_config. That manifest's runtime/backend/version/build/device, actual artifact identities and required implementation evidence must corroborate the report. A saved comparison validates those pure documents/bindings without opening their embedded artifact/config paths, loading weights, importing a native runtime or acquiring qualification on the comparison machine.

Three runtime fields all null mean verified non-use only when the resolved production-effective/configuration evidence establishes that this lexical/HTTP arm uses no local selector embedding runtime. Null without that corroboration is unknown. Comparing such corroborated non-use against a fully evidenced native runtime is permitted as the explicitly declared selector/artifact experiment; display both actual facts, never assign the native runtime or its costs to the other arm. A configured or observed local runtime cannot be relabeled unused because an attempt failed or was not dispatched. Missing native runtime/config/build/implementation evidence is incompatible in either comparison mode, rather than null-equality or an intended variable.

For two native-hybrid arms, compare the manifest's runtime.backend, runtime.version, runtime.build and device for exact equality and verify equal runtime-affecting thread/execution/pooling/implementation controls through that reviewed backend's build/implementation evidence. They are mandatory invariants in replacement and quality_only. The full manifest/runtime_config_digest may differ only for the already declared model/artifact choice (model, source, quantization, license, dimensions and exact files/roles/bytes/hashes); hybrid representation/fusion/index choices remain declared semantic experiment variables under their existing configs. Different artifact/semantic choices require their own valid evidence and qualification; they do not permit a different native runtime build, device or execution controls. There is no generic runtime-settings document or caller attestation that can waive these checks.

For 0.61 comparison output, `ArmDescriptor` is exactly `{selector:{name:ID,version:ID,transport:in_process|http},artifacts:[ArtifactIdentity],runtime:RuntimeDisplay}`. Both arms require this nonnull closed runtime display object, including incompatible output. `RuntimeDisplay` = `{use:used|unused|unknown,backend:ID|null,version:ID|null,build:ID|null,device:ID|null,config_digest:H|null}`. All six keys are required; nonnull identity strings use section 12.6.1 ID bounds (nonempty, at most 256 UTF-8 bytes), and config_digest is the existing lowercase 64-hex H. No additional keys, settings bag, embedded paths or artifact-name encoding is admitted. This projection is output evidence, never a configured flag, runtime preimage or activation authority.

The comparator constructs each projection from that arm's hashed report and already verified offline closure. backend/version copy Environment.runtime/runtime_version; config_digest copies Environment.runtime_config_digest. Build and device copy the uniquely resolved, digest-matching section 6.1 manifest's runtime.build and device, after verifying consistency with Environment and the required backend/build/implementation evidence. use=used requires all five facts nonnull and verified under the native applicability rules above; config_digest then equals the full validated manifest's canonical digest. use=unused requires all five facts null and corroborated actual local-runtime non-use from the resolved production-effective/configuration evidence, never nulls alone.

use=unknown is permitted only in an incompatible comparison. Preserve safely parsed available Environment backend/version/config-digest facts; retain build/device only from a unique digest-matching pure-validated manifest consistent with those facts. Unresolved or conflicting fields are null, and every shown value remains explicitly unverified under use=unknown. If even the Environment is unverifiable, retain the required object with use=unknown and all five facts null. Unknown never matches another unknown or establishes non-use/compatibility. Verified projections remain used/unused when the comparison is incompatible for an unrelated reason. Existing safe reason paths explain unresolved/conflicting evidence without copying source text. The exclusive Markdown renderer reads these projections directly and labels unknown as unverified; it never reopens a report, manifest, inventory or filesystem path. This explicitly closed 0.61 ArmDescriptor extension is distinguished by the exact protocol inventory; historical comparison objects keep their prior closed shape and bytes, with no injected runtime projection or silent protocol upgrade.

Machine facts, Python, dependencies_digest, runner/protocol inventories, source/eligibility/proof, case/label/request bindings, budgets, order and service-policy invariants otherwise remain unchanged. A native-versus-unused exception does not excuse a changed dependency installation or Python version. Normal performance comparison still requires the same known applicable machine facts; quality_only relaxes only its existing machine fields, never native runtime/build/device/control equality. It retains all raw setup/query/resource quantities with performance_comparable=false and no cost/performance superiority statement. Section 12.6.4 separately governs preparation policy; neither applicability rule supplies an inference, hardware, human-label or acceptance pass.

### 12.6.3 Source-grounded development benchmarks and examples (spec 0.56; RM34/RM35/RM40/RM44; AC25/AC26/AC31/AC36)

The source-grounded development profile builds a useful benchmark from actual acquired skill originals and MCP schema definitions. The implementer selects source-backed reference relevance and records the method before running selectors. It does not require a user-supplied completed dataset, a fixed catalog count, native-language author proof, independent double annotation or custodian qualification. These are development reference judgments, not fictitious Person/Judgment rows. The independent qualification profile in sections 12.6 and 12.6.1 retains its existing evidence, numerical and 200/300 cohort requirements; that profile is not a prerequisite for delivering and using this development benchmark. Generated queries and author-defined references contribute zero to its independent human-case or reviewed-pair minima.

Matrix skills are examples and a benchmark source, not an installed-library allowlist, required market or product minimum size. Users choose arbitrary supported local/imported skill and tool sources. Preserve actual originals, URLs, captured source versions/commits, hashes and applicable licenses/NOTICE; record acquisition failures, unsupported inputs and duplicates explicitly. Do not translate a Chinese skill and call its replacement an original English asset. Existing pinned original English skills and genuine upstream tool definitions may supplement Matrix. Public download, source flags or hashes do not prove rights; preserve unknown rights as unknown, respect explicit prohibitions, and do not redistribute originals under an unknown license.

**Bounded standalone acquisition.** Acquisition is separate from the offline corpus loader and product refresh. Use declared public HTTPS source/download URLs, finite requests, redirects, time and byte budgets under section 14.5; never execute fetched instructions, installers, bundled scripts or tool calls. Source inventories and selected partial acquisitions retain their actual coverage; a page count is not a complete inventory, and omission never proves removal. This path does not qualify MATRIX-REFRESH or change host authority.

A standalone benchmark inspector may read selected skill/metadata/license text from a pinned ZIP in memory: compressed archive <=10 MiB, <=1000 entries, summed declared uncompressed bytes <=20 MiB, selected SKILL.md <=256 KiB, each selected metadata/license text <=4 MiB, decompression ratio <=100 for every nonempty member. Before reading any member, validate the entire central directory and reject absolute/drive paths, parent traversal, backslashes, NUL, duplicate or NFC/case-colliding names, symlinks/special files and encryption. Enforce actual streamed expansion bounds and CRC/error checks; reject inconsistent metadata and malformed archives. Only explicitly selected ordinary text members are read. Other ordinary package members remain opaque and are not executed or installed. No arbitrary archive member path becomes an output path; retain the original ZIP and selected-file hashes separately. The frozen loader and product readers retain their current no-fetch/no-extraction rules. Add adversarial tests for every boundary before implementing inspection.

`AssetEntry.origin=other_original` is permitted only for an original skill in this source-grounded development profile, with the actual supplied `original_languages` tags (including non-Matrix Chinese or mixed-language originals). The catalog has purpose=development. This value is not permitted in the formal locked cohorts of sections 12.6–12.6.1 and supplies neither rights nor language qualification proof. Existing Matrix/original-English/MCP qualified source definitions and 200/300 acceptance counts remain unchanged.

**Closed development input.** `AssetDiagnosticV1` = `{schema_version:1,dataset_id:ID,profile:source_grounded_development,catalog_digest:H,evidence:[Evidence from section 12.6.1],cases:[DiagnosticCase],digest:H}`. The catalog is a separately supplied `AssetCatalogSetV1`; `catalog_digest` commits its complete canonical document, not a target-only projection. It may have no people or equivalent pairs; no human records are invented. `digest` is canonical_digest of this input with only its own digest key removed. Evidence IDs are unique and sorted; case IDs are unique and sorted. All original-byte hashes and declared sizes must verify within explicitly supplied safe local roots. Conflicting, dangling or tampered references reject. Existing corpus document/original/evidence and UTF-8 bounds apply; no automatic fetch occurs while loading this input.

`DiagnosticCase` = `{id:ID,task_text:string,conversation:[ConversationEntry from SelectionRequestV2],lane:lane,query_language:zh|en|mixed|unknown,requested_kinds:[skill|mcp_tool],source_asset_ids:[ID],source_evidence_ids:[ID],authorship:generated|source_excerpt|human_authored,reference_kind:source_derived|author_defined|human_reviewed,reference_class:positive|negative|ambiguous,acceptable_asset_ids:[ID],reference_evidence_ids:[ID],notes:string}`. Product query and metadata caps apply; `_ids` arrays are unique UTF-8 sorted sets. Source/acceptable asset IDs resolve in the complete catalog; evidence IDs resolve in the committed catalog/input evidence union without contradictory identities. Positive and ambiguous references have nonempty acceptable sets; negatives have empty acceptable sets. All genuine acceptable alternatives, including other-language equivalents, remain acceptable; uncertain/exhaustive relevance limitations are stated in notes. Reference_kind records the method, not authenticated human independence. Human_reviewed does not qualify the independent profile. No Person, raw Judgment, adjudicator or agreement statistic is synthesized for this input.

**Run, compare and examples.** Development execution and paired algorithm comparison use the same production v2 request, configured selector, shared coordinator/admission and full mixed catalog for every arm; labels, bodies and reference sets never enter selector input. Matrix origin and language pair do not filter the catalog or force a language winner. Support primary_only and service tracks with the existing meanings; use the same frozen cases/catalog/settings for compared arms and retain every planned case/repetition, failure, cancellation, timeout and abstention. Explicit incompatible inputs block comparison; missing observations remain unknown and do not become zero cost/latency. Repetitions do not increase independent cases. No host executor is constructed.

Cover available Chinese, English and mixed queries against actual Chinese, English and mixed descriptions for skills and tools. Report exact observed per-pair/per-kind counts, missing combinations and overall results. Chinese-query to original-English-target cases without shared Latin identifiers are an emphasized hard diagnostic, alongside English-to-English, Chinese-to-Chinese, English-to-Chinese and code-switched cases. Preserve original body language separately from projected description language. Language metadata records its source/heuristic/review method; it is not proof of native authorship. Multiple acceptable target languages/kinds give non-additive subgroups. No synthetic upcount or fixed-size prerequisite is imposed on this profile.

The initial development report is an internal evaluation artifact, not an `AssetBenchmarkReportV1` or qualified comparison document. It records `profile=source_grounded_development`, `qualification=provisional`, `acceptance_eligible=false`; case/catalog/config/implementation digests, source counts/exclusions, planned rows with production requests/results/attempts or fixed failures, reference provenance, actual latency and available cost evidence, and overall/per-language-pair/kind diagnostic top-1/Hit@3/abstention/false-confidence counts. State reference limitations and missing independent annotation without blocking development results or issuing an independent acceptance verdict. Demonstrate at least one actual acquired Matrix example and an original-English cross-language target when available; the reproducible example reports source identity/hash, query, admitted result and measured duration. The formal run/compare wire contracts and independent gates remain in section 12.6.2.


### 12.6.4 Optional third-party index preparation (spec 0.57; RM35/RM40; AC26/AC31)

An operator may explicitly prepare an HTTP primary before benchmark warm-ups or through a development command. Stateless selectors require no companion endpoint or traffic. The companion has one explicitly configured plain-HTTP loopback URL, POST JSON only, under the same URL/credential/query/fragment, no-redirect/proxy/environment and strict bounded transport rules as section 4.6.2. Never guess a URL, follow a returned URL, or automatically prepare during ordinary query dispatch. Successful protocol responses require HTTP 200 and application/json; reject malformed/duplicate-key/nonfinite JSON, incorrect media type, mismatches and decoded responses over 1 MiB using fixed safe transport/validation reasons. An exchange failure stops polling; no hidden retries.

**Closed companion records.** Section 12.6.1 ID/H/numeric/strict-JSON conventions apply. All listed keys are required; nullable only where stated. Reuse AssetRecordV1 and CostObservationV1 from section 8.1 without widening them.

`PreparationBinding` = `{selector:{name:ID,version:ID},catalog_revision:nonnegative_int,catalog_digest:H,scope:{user_id:ID,workspace_id:ID},permissions:[ID],selection_scope:discovery|runtime,requested_kinds:[skill|mcp_tool]}`. Permissions/kinds are unique UTF-8 sorted sets; kinds nonempty. catalog_digest is canonical SHA-256 of the **exact ordered offered catalog array**, not the full provenance document, a target subset or revision alone. Preparation and subsequent query offers use the same array and entire scope/policy binding. Send no task prompts, labels, feedback or private skill bodies.

`PreparationRequestV1` = `{schema_version:1,preparation_id:UUID,operation:prepare|status|cancel,binding:PreparationBinding,profile:cold|reuse,catalog:[AssetRecordV1]|null,budget_ms:positive_int|null}`. Prepare supplies the full catalog and remaining declared preparation budget; status/cancel require null catalog/budget. The whole request is ≤33 MiB and the catalog separately obeys the existing 1000-record/32-MiB offer cap. Only prepare can start work. The client generates the ID and sends prepare once, without automatic retry. Same ID plus identical prepare is idempotent; changed binding/profile/catalog/budget conflicts. Status/cancel require that exact ID/binding/profile and cannot create missing work. Cold builds an isolated fresh index without deleting another caller's active index; reuse may find an exact-compatible index or build one. Neither grants execution or inference authority.

`PreparationResponseV1` = `{schema_version:1,preparation_id:UUID,binding:PreparationBinding,profile:cold|reuse,state:indexing|ready|failed|cancelled|stale_catalog,index_revision:ID|null,reused:boolean|null,elapsed_ms:nonnegative_number|null,usage_claims:CostObservationV1|null,reason:ID|null}`. ID, full binding and profile exactly echo the request; a mismatch is invalid, never another catalog's readiness. Ready requires nonnull index_revision/reused and null reason; cold requires reused=false. All other states have null index_revision/reused. Indexing has null reason; terminal failure/cancel/stale requires one fixed server reason: build_failed, deadline_exceeded, cancelled, catalog_stale, unknown_preparation, identity_conflict or unsupported_profile. usage_claims, when supplied, has provenance=reported.

Prepare may return indexing, ready or a terminal failure. Indexing may remain indexing or become ready/failed/cancelled/stale_catalog. Failed/cancelled/stale never restart under that ID; ready may become stale_catalog if its index is lost. Rebuild needs a new ID. Cancelling already-ready work leaves ready; this is not an index deletion API. The server starts its monotonic budget on acceptance and fences publication after cancellation/expiry, reporting a terminal result. A client disconnect alone proves neither cancellation nor that server work stopped. Unknown status/cancel returns failed/unknown_preparation without creating work. A conflicting request returns failed/identity_conflict without changing the original job.

Response elapsed_ms and cost fields are cumulative **server claims for this preparation**, not increments to sum across polls. Retain every response and use only the latest verified cumulative claim; unknown values remain null. A reused result measures this lookup/preparation, not the earlier build; prior build cost and amortization are unknown without separate actual evidence.

**Configuration and bounded execution.** `PreparationConfig` = `{mode:none|http,url:string|null,profile:cold|reuse|null,total_ms:integer[1,600000],request_ms:integer[1,10000],poll_ms:integer[50,5000],max_polls:integer[1,1000]}`. Defaults are none/null/null, 60000/2000/250/240 respectively. HTTP requires an explicit validated URL and profile and a configured HTTP query primary; none requires null URL/profile. request_ms≤total_ms. This benchmark companion table, shared by development tooling, is separate from production query settings. Freeze its values; these new external preparation budgets do not change gateway/query/worker budgets.

The client freezes the exact offered catalog and plans all query slots first, then prepares once before all warm-ups/measured slots. Its total monotonic clock includes initial transport, server work, polling and poll delays. Cap each request timeout by both request_ms and remaining total time. If indexing, wait cancellably up to poll_ms and send status, checking the total deadline and max_polls before every poll. Stop on ready, terminal state, failed/invalid exchange, poll cap, interruption or deadline. After a dispatched prepare ends non-ready, send at most one best-effort cancel under a separate fixed 1000-ms cleanup deadline; retain actual acknowledgement or its absence and cleanup duration. Total client occupancy is bounded by total_ms+1000 ms; the server's independently enforced budget still applies. Closing HTTP is never a successful cancellation claim.

If required preparation does not reach verified ready, preserve its transcript and **every planned query slot** as not_run with a safe reason, mark the report blocked or interrupted as appropriate, and return nonzero. Never silently change catalog/index or claim a completed indexed comparison. An explicitly separate development diagnostic may query during indexing to demonstrate existing not_ready→gateway-floor behavior; its samples are not prepared-query latency. After ready, each ranked/no_match primary result must echo the pinned index_revision and catalog binding through the unchanged v2 fields. A shared optional adapter validates this before coordinator admission; a mismatch is stale/invalid primary evidence with existing fallback policy preserved. Actual not_ready/unavailable remains a recorded failure of the ready claim, never hidden by floor success.

**Preparation evidence and compatibility.** `PreparationRecord` = `{config:PreparationConfig,binding:PreparationBinding|null,preparation_id:UUID|null,status:not_applicable|ready|failed|cancelled|timeout|stale_catalog,reason:ID|null,index_revision:ID|null,observed_ms:nonnegative_number|null,cleanup_ms:nonnegative_number|null,exchanges:[PreparationExchange],observed_costs:[CostObservationV1]}`; `PreparationExchange` = `{operation:prepare|status|cancel,observed_ms:nonnegative_number,response:PreparationResponseV1|null,reason:ID|null}`. Exchanges stay in dispatch order, at most max_polls+2; null response records transport/validation failure. Record config equals NormalizedConfig.preparation. Mode none requires status=not_applicable, null binding/ID/reason/index/times and empty exchanges/costs: no lifecycle traffic and no invented zero build. HTTP uses its actual binding/ID; ready requires the verified matching ready response/index. Other statuses cannot claim an index. Reasons are the fixed server reasons above or bounded section 4.6.2 transport reasons, invalid_response, poll_limit, deadline_exceeded or cancelled. Total-deadline expiry is timeout; poll-cap failure is failed/poll_limit.

observed_ms is actual client preparation wall time excluding separately recorded cleanup; it is not server elapsed_ms or CPU time. observed_costs contains only separately observed collector evidence with provenance=observed and honest coverage. Preserve null unknown CPU/tokens/memory; do not infer zero from no local inference or add cumulative poll claims together. Preparation time/cost is separate from every query row's latency/cost, with no hidden setup in query totals. Optional observed end-to-end time adds preparation, the actual declared query workload and cleanup, without guessed lifetime/break-even amortization.

Normalized config and report/comparison shapes in section 12.6.2 include preparation explicitly. This is a 0.57 extension of those closed schema-version-1 formats, distinguished by their required protocol inventory digest; implement schema validation, normalization, exports and comparison together. Omitted config tables normalize to none. Historical pre-0.57 reports retain their actual bytes and semantics; never inject a fictitious preparation transcript or silently compare incompatible protocol digests.

Comparisons render preparation state/profile/reuse, actual time and observed versus reported costs separately from query summaries. Stateless versus indexed arms may compare with explicit not_applicable versus measured preparation. When both use HTTP preparation, require equal profile and total/request/poll/max-poll budgets; endpoint and selector identity are intended arm variables. Cold-versus-reuse differs in preparation policy and is incompatible in **both** replacement and quality_only modes; inspect separate reports for that startup comparison. Quality-only continues to relax machine facts alone. Equal cases/catalog/query policy and all existing evidence/qualification requirements remain mandatory. The source-grounded development internal report records the same normalized preparation content; it does not become a formal qualified report.

**Local native preparation variant (spec 0.61; RM16/RM35/RM38/RM40/RM41/RM44; AC26/AC29/AC31/AC32).** In addition to the unchanged none/HTTP variants above, PreparationConfig admits mode=local. This is an explicit gateway-owned model/index preparation mode for a configured section 14.5.1 in-process hybrid with independently qualified exact manifest/config/runtime/device identity. It never activates an unqualified artifact. The same closed seven config keys remain required after normalization. Local requires url=null, profile=cold, total_ms an integer in [1,60000], and exactly request_ms=2000, poll_ms=250, max_polls=240. These three HTTP fields retain their defaults but are inapplicable to local execution; request_ms≤total_ms applies only to the existing none/HTTP variants. No local URL, polling, reuse policy, plugin, server response or hidden query-triggered preparation is admitted. Omission still normalizes to the exact none variant; a prepared native benchmark must explicitly choose local. Query defaults 500/150 ms, each worker job's original 60-second deadline and all source/worker/CPU/GPU limits remain unchanged.

The mode-discriminated local PreparationRecord has exactly the existing ten keys plus required `local:{job_events:[IndexJobEventV1],readiness:RecommendationReadinessV1|null}`; local has exactly those two keys. No other mode may contain local; the old none/HTTP record keys and meanings remain byte-compatible. Config equals NormalizedConfig.preparation. Local requires nonnull actual PreparationBinding, preparation_id=null and exchanges=[]: no fabricated HTTP exchange, UUID, remote acknowledgement or reuse claim. The binding is the exact full frozen offered catalog/revision, resolved hybrid selector identity, scope, permissions, selection_scope and requested kinds, including a genuinely empty catalog/revision zero. Existing catalog, ID/H/Time/finite-number/UTF-8/duplicate-key limits apply. Both report and comparison remain bounded by 256 MiB. job_events has at most 100,000 entries in actual captured event order; enforce the cap during capture/read/serialization, refusing overflow rather than dropping or summarizing events to fit.

Validate every local event against the published IndexJobEventV1 including its closed payload and EmbeddingObservationV1 references. Per job, (job_id,seq) is unique, seq starts at one and is consecutive, exactly one queued precedes started, batch numbers are consecutive positive integers while started, and at most one actual published/failed/cancelled terminal ends the stream. No post-terminal events or copied query observations. Preserve original job IDs, timestamps, actual dispatch order and terminal/failure evidence; do not manufacture a successful job from batch counts or reorder by arbitrary UUID. Queued budgets/config identity and all observed catalog revisions must bind the existing actual worker and pinned offer; published semantic index identities must agree with the retained index used. Local setup reuses the production store, scheduler, semantic factory and single worker in a fresh disposable evaluation store. No upstream acquisition, task prompt, labels, correction history or feedback authority supplies preparation inputs; the existing explicit locked-catalog evaluation limitation remains labeled.

Local status has the existing six values with these exact invariants:

| status | reason / index_revision | local.readiness and decision |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ready | reason=null; index_revision is the actual nonnull usable semantic index ID. | Nonempty bound offer; actual qualified engine loaded; all scheduled setup work settled, pending_jobs=0; readiness nonnull, state=ready, reason=usable_catalog, lexical_ready=true, exact binding catalog_revision/catalog_size, semantic_coverage.total=catalog_size and 1≤semantic_coverage.ready≤total. |
| not_applicable | reason=null; index_revision=null. | Only a genuinely empty bound offer; readiness nonnull, state=empty, reason=empty_catalog, catalog_size=0, exact bound catalog_revision, semantic_coverage.ready=total=0 and pending_jobs=0. Preserve actual lexical_ready/failed_jobs and any actual work; normally no jobs. No invented engine, index or inference. |
| failed | reason=build_failed; index_revision=null. | Actual setup/runtime/validation failure or no compatible usable semantic index after work settles. Readiness is the actual collected value or null when unavailable; never lexical readiness substituted for native usability. |
| timeout | reason=deadline_exceeded; index_revision=null. | Original total preparation deadline exhausted; no ready publication or later query dispatch is authorized. |
| cancelled | reason=cancelled; index_revision=null. | Actual interruption/cancellation; retain owned work and its cleanup even when cancellation wins a simultaneous completion/deadline. |
| stale_catalog | reason=catalog_stale; index_revision=null. | Exact frozen binding/current eligibility changed or cannot be retained through preparation. No hidden rebase, different offer or retry. |

Readiness always validates against its existing published closed schema; when nonnull its catalog_revision/catalog_size and coverage describe this bound offer, with 0≤ready≤total=catalog_size. pending_jobs/failed_jobs are actual counts, not derived by counting published events alone. A ready partial compatible index is permitted under RM41 after work settles, with failed jobs and partial coverage retained; it does not mean full coverage and cannot bypass the existing full-coverage confidence rule. A failed job remains failed even if an earlier compatible subset remains usable. Ready/not_applicable contain complete settled event streams; non-ready records may retain an actual interrupted prefix, never an invented terminal. No-ready cases retain their actual readiness rather than forcing a synthetic unavailable schema value. After ready, the unchanged per-query revision/index/current-admission checks remain mandatory; native ranked/no_match must match the prepared binding/index. Existing native-primary→lexical-floor fallback stays separately attributed and cannot retroactively make required setup ready.

Freeze all input/slot planning before worker preparation and perform local setup once before every warm-up/measured query. Start its timing at the first native semantic preparation activity, including actual runtime verification/import and artifact verification, not after the engine/index exists. Pure corpus/source-input acquisition/validation is separately labeled and excluded. observed_ms is actual nonnegative finite wall time through the preparation decision, nullable only when no timed stage began; ready/not_applicable require a measured value. Any static semantic verification performed outside that measured interval must be explicitly declared by report.limits containing `native_static_validation_outside_timing`; retain any available collector evidence, and do not claim complete startup/lifecycle timing or substitute zero. Cold means a new engine/index in the fresh private store; it makes no claim about OS file-cache or Python/import-cache coldness.

The preparation total clock and each original job clock are never reset by load/index/cleanup, and no new native dispatch or usable publication follows the deadline/cancellation fence. Opaque in-flight native work remains owned until drained. Record actual decision time even if scheduling/native return exceeds a target; failure stays failure. cleanup_ms separately measures the nonoverlapping owned drain after that preparation failure/cancellation decision through actual return, or null when not observed/not performed; a measured no-work cleanup may be zero. No fixed HTTP 1000-ms cleanup bound or total_ms+1000 occupancy promise applies to local execution. Raw job events may extend into this cleanup and must be retained. CPU-window drain, total native return and whole cleanup are distinct measurements; one never substitutes for another.

Local observed_costs contains zero or one existing observed CostObservationV1: a single inclusive stage aggregate covering preparation plus its owned cleanup, with complete per-metric coverage only when established. It contains no reported claim and does not copy cumulative batch observations as additional aggregates. Raw job embeddings remain exclusively in local.job_events; each actual invocation is identified by (job_id,seq,zero-based embedding-array ordinal) and counted once, including failed/cancelled work. Complete additive aggregate metrics sum their known owned components; peak_bytes is the maximum actually observed peak over the covered ownership interval, not a sum of process/job peaks. A missing component or incomplete lifetime coverage makes that complete metric null; unavailable CPU/memory/tokens never become zero. No-dispatch zero requires actual evidence. Preserve known raw components in job events even if no complete aggregate exists. For preparation rendering, show that complete aggregate when available; otherwise derive a clearly partial known subtotal from the raw known owned components once (sum additive metrics, maximum peak), leaving complete total unknown. Never add the inclusive aggregate to its explanatory raw components, add observed to reported, copy document inference into Row.embeddings, or invent a preparation CostCoverage/Row dialect. Query Summary/PairedSummary cost/count arithmetic remains unchanged and excludes setup.

No local failed/timeout/cancelled/stale preparation dispatches any planned query: retain every warm-up and measured Slot as its existing not_run Row, with no attempts/embeddings/cost/timing/observed offer fabricated. Report status is interrupted for cancellation, otherwise blocked; return nonzero and retain all measured setup/cleanup/job evidence. A genuine empty local not_applicable may proceed to the existing empty_catalog rows. Preparation failure never removes repetition-zero misses or enlarges independent-case denominators. Optional whole-lifecycle wall time sums actual nonoverlapping preparation, declared query workload and cleanup only with coverage established; no guessed earlier build, lifetime amortization, incomplete startup/resource cost superiority or acceptance pass.

Comparisons copy this exact local record into their existing preparation.a/b slots; the exclusive Markdown renderer uses that self-contained data. None versus local-cold is an explicitly displayed stateless/indexed experiment, not a zero-build assertion. Local-cold versus local-cold requires equal total_ms; local versus HTTP requires equal profile=cold and total_ms, while HTTP transport limits are separately declared/enforced and need not equal local's inapplicable defaults. Both HTTP arms retain their existing equality of profile/total/request/poll/max-poll policy. Any cold-versus-reuse pairing remains incompatible in replacement and quality_only; local reuse is unsupported. All other case/catalog/query/source/evidence/runtime equality rules remain in force. Unknown or incomplete setup/resource coverage stays labeled and cannot establish complete startup/lifecycle cost superiority. Query performance_comparable remains subject to the unchanged applicable environment/workload/timing rules; performance_comparable remains false in quality_only. The source-grounded development report uses the same normalized local content and remains provisional/acceptance-ineligible; no genuine human panel or target-machine dataset is a prerequisite for implementing/testing this tooling.

This is an additive 0.61 extension of the same three closed schema-version-1 benchmark documents, distinguished by their required exact protocol inventory. It changes no existing Row, Environment, Cost, artifact/config/source-proof or episode/host/platform contract, and no selector/model/source/action authority or numerical acceptance threshold. Historical none/HTTP reports and all 0.60 episode/evidence bytes remain unchanged; do not fabricate a local record or silently upgrade an old protocol digest.

### 12.6.5 Adaptation episodes, whole-host trials and platform exports (spec 0.60; revised 0.65; RM16/RM38–RM44; AC29–AC37)

These contracts authorize the shared episode/host/platform drivers and offline graders before genuine customer, person or hardware inputs are available. They do not activate an adapter or establish measured acceptance. Early and final host wrappers use the same driver/grader, with different frozen task sets and eligibility gates. Reuse section 12.6.1 ID/H/Time/lane, strict numeric/JSON, canonical hashing and evidence rules, and section 12.6.2 Environment, Rate, Row and ArtifactIdentity. Objects are closed; listed fields are required, nullable only where stated. Inputs are ≤64 MiB, reports ≤256 MiB and planned observations ≤100,000; all other applicable product/source limits remain. Public exports contain no credentials or private feedback content.

**Shared offline inputs and evidence**

Every report below adds required `input_manifest:{path:string|null,digest:H,bytes:nonnegative_int}` with exactly section 12.6.2's companion ownership, bounds, canonical manifest digest, explicit top-level input override and OfflineResolverV1 rules. No cwd/checkout inference, directory search, network fetch, saved-grade fallback or default reconstruction is allowed. S3 uses the same closed InputManifestV1/DocumentRef shapes, extending DocumentRef.kind **only within these S3 schemas** with `host_tasks`, `host_freeze`, `episodes`, `episode_freeze`. Their IDs are kind plus `:` plus canonical document digest; JSON only. Retrieval manifests and their kinds/cardinalities remain unchanged. S3 requires exactly one environment_lock, runner_inventory and protocol_inventory, the exact root input named by report.input_digest and its freeze document, and their complete transitive lock/configuration/evidence closure. Retrieval-only benchmark_config/effective_config singleton requirements do not apply to S3; actual effective configurations resolve as referenced_config documents by digest. Input/freeze documents and corpus documents retain the 64 MiB limit; configurations/inventories/manifests retain 4 MiB limits.

Configuration references resolve to complete preimages validated by the applicable existing gateway or pinned native-host parser/schema, whose exact schema/source/version and extraction implementation are committed by the protocol/runner inventories. They are the actual execution-effective configuration, with all effective defaults, provider/model/sampling, policies and budgets; no arbitrary settings bag or omitted relevant setting is permitted. Secrets are omitted using the existing redaction contract and never replaced by secret hashes. The source-bound extraction and actual native configuration/inventory capture are evidence obligations, not an operator assertion. Required unknown or unresolved inputs make grading explicitly unverifiable/blocked with fixed safe reasons and nonzero CLI exit, never a passed gate. Structural/digest contradictions reject the report. Environment must match environment_lock. S3 runner inventory includes the actual driver/grader, package metadata and shared modules it imports; protocol inventory includes these S3 schemas, actual referenced native/gateway schemas and the specification, with the existing source-bound inventory/preimage verification.

**Native configuration validation (spec 0.65; RM16/RM39/RM40/RM44; AC31/AC35/AC36/AC37).** Every host_config_digest resolves to the complete execution-effective host_config preimage validated with the pinned host's own validator: for OpenClaw 2026.10.7, `openclaw config validate` against the captured configuration and its pinned native schema/validator. Capture the exact schema bytes and SHA-256/length, host distribution/version, validator implementation and invocation, validation result, and source-bound extraction/default expansion in the existing protocol/runner inventories and evidence registry. The validated preimage must be the one committed by the digest and used for that arm; validating an unrelated config, merely parsing JSON/TOML or checking that its digest matches is insufficient. Offline grading resolves these original inputs/results without invoking a live host or fetching a schema. A missing native validation or unbound schema/validator result leaves host_config unverified and blocks qualified acceptance.

Use the pinned host's own validator, whose regular-expression dialect is captured with its implementation identity. A validator using another regex dialect, including Python re without demonstrated equivalence to the captured native semantics, MUST NOT claim host-schema validation; rejecting a valid native pattern or skipping its check is not a valid substitute. Preserve the pinned host's actual defaults, refinements and plugin-schema checks as well as its schema identity. A generic JSON Schema pass alone cannot replace the native validator. Validate private execution-effective inputs at their owning boundary before export; apply the existing secret omission rule without invented secret values or secret hashes. Bind the exported preimage to that validation through a captured source-bound extraction/redaction. If omission prevents the verifier establishing the relevant configuration or validation relation, mark it unverified rather than reconstructing secrets or declaring success.

provider_config_digest, sampling_config_digest and each task's budgets_digest likewise require the exact applicable schema/parser name, source/version/digest and validation/extraction evidence for the actual effective values. No universal standalone provider, sampling or native-budget schema is declared by this revision. If the configuration is a projection of the pinned host configuration, commit the parent schema and validated parent preimage plus the exact extraction implementation and paths; do not call an arbitrary settings object a validated projection. Gateway-owned values use their applicable existing gateway parser/schema, with the same source binding. Until such bindings are supplied and verified for each referenced document, those documents stay **unverified**, and qualified grading stays blocked. Digest consistency, fixture success or another arm's validated host_config cannot promote them. These are evidence obligations; this revision enables no native verifier or host operation and leaves historical fixtures explicitly fixture-qualified.

`EvidenceRef` = `{id:ID,path:string|null,sha256:H,bytes:nonnegative_int,provenance:observed|reported|fixture}`. Every report has `evidence:[EvidenceRef]`, unique and sorted by ID. These IDs occupy one report-wide namespace, including dedicated references and all nested evidence_ids. Each resolves to the same ID/original-byte hash/length in the companion evidence registry; access restrictions and explicit resolvers remain authoritative. Dedicated references duplicate the exact matching registry entry, never introduce another identity. Dangling IDs, conflicting hashes/lengths, duplicate IDs or changed bytes reject. A nonnull EvidenceRef.path is the companion evidence locator, relative to the resolved manifest directory under its ordinary-file/no-follow rules; null requires the explicit authorized resolver. Provenance labels alone establish neither authenticity nor independent observation.

**Closed host freeze and deterministic plan**

`HostFreezeV1` = `{schema_version:1,corpus_lock_digest:H,configurations:[HostArmConfig],task_policies:[{task_id:ID,permissions:[ID],budgets_digest:H}],native_inventory_digest:H,packages:[{name:ID,version:ID,sha256:H,bytes:nonnegative_int,evidence_id:ID}],host:{name:ID,version:ID,sdk_version:ID,distribution_evidence_ids:[ID]},asset_bindings:[{asset_id:ID,content_hash:H,schema_hash:H|null}],rubric_evidence_ids:[ID]}`. `HostArmConfig` = `{arm:native|provider_only|recommendations,provider:ID,model:ID,provider_config_digest:H,sampling_config_digest:H,host_config_digest:H,recommendation_config_digest:H|null}`. `freeze_digest` is canonical_digest of this entire validated object, with no removed fields or self-digest. Configurations contain exactly the three named arms, sorted by arm ID. Each configuration and policy digest resolves by digest to its schema-validated referenced_config preimage.
