Native inventory resolves likewise to the exact complete pinned host effective tool/skill inventory response and policy preimages, with actual schema/source/capture bindings in the inventories; native response arrays preserve their actual schema-defined order rather than the generic S1 ID-array sorting rule; it cannot be reconstructed from selected names or a gateway proposal. The same corpus/asset bindings, provider/model/sampling and per-task permissions/budgets apply across arms. Only the declared provider integration and recommendation/adaptation enablement differences belong to the arm variable; no unrecorded override is allowed. A null recommendation_config_digest means the arm does not use gateway recommendations, not unknown gateway settings.

Task policies are unique and sorted by task_id, exactly covering the tasks; every task asset_bindings array is unique/sorted by asset_id and is a subset of the freeze bindings; each task's permissions_digest equals canonical_digest of that policy's permissions array and its budgets_digest matches the referenced native budget document. Asset bindings are unique/sorted by asset_id and equal the exact locked bindings used. Rubric IDs equal the set of task rubric references. Packages are unique/sorted by `(name,version,sha256)` and bind the actual installed plugin/package originals to evidence with matching original sha256/bytes; host name/version/SDK must match the resolved native distribution evidence and observations. Package/host SDK identity is bound here, not through an undeclared ArtifactIdentity role; the existing artifact enum remains unchanged. Any changed effective permission, budget, provider/sampling configuration, package or native inventory fails verification unless a new freeze/input/report is produced. Freeze documents reference no report or task-document digest, avoiding a digest cycle.

`AssetHostTasksV1` = `{schema_version:1,kind:early_trial|acceptance,corpus_lock_digest:H,seed:uint32,freeze_digest:H,tasks:[HostTask]}`. `HostTask` = `{id:ID,lane:lane,family_ids:[ID],prompt_case_id:ID,setup_evidence_ids:[ID],success_rubric_evidence_id:ID,permissions_digest:H,budgets_digest:H,asset_bindings:[{asset_id:ID,content_hash:H,schema_hash:H|null}],mutation:boolean}`. Tasks are unique and **already sorted by UTF-8 task ID**, for both canonical input hashing and execution. Unsorted input rejects rather than silently retaining another execution order. Cases/families/setup/rubrics resolve within the frozen inputs; fixture/provisional data stays unqualified. The report input_digest is canonical_digest of this entire task document; its corpus/freeze identities must match the verified companions.

Plan all three arms for every task before setup, traversing that task order. Within each task sort arms by SHA-256 of canonical `{seed,task_id,arm}`, with UTF-8 arm ID as tie-break. Plan ordinals are exactly 1..3×task_count. Each task/arm occurs once, and report rows have the exact same order and keys as planned. At least 100 preregistered paired tasks and section 12.6's existing coverage/gates are required for acceptance. Empty inputs may exercise tooling but cannot pass acceptance. Early trial never asserts the full gate through a smaller set. A driver captures actual host outcomes, attempts, native session/run evidence, elapsed time and human interaction. A proposed tool or gateway trace alone is not task success. Only the host executes tools and grants mutation approval.

**Host report, attempts and arithmetic**

`AssetHostReportV1` = `{schema_version:1,kind:early_trial|acceptance,qualification:fixture|provisional|qualified,input_digest:H,freeze_digest:H,input_manifest:ManifestRef,environment:Environment,artifacts:[ArtifactIdentity],planned:[{task_id:ID,arm:native|provider_only|recommendations,ordinal:positive_int}],rows:[HostRow],summary:HostSummary,evidence:[EvidenceRef]}`; ManifestRef is the companion reference above. Qualified requires resolved actual corpus, execution, human/platform and artifact evidence, never a caller flag.

`HostRow` = `{task_id:ID,arm:native|provider_only|recommendations,status:completed|failed|timeout|cancelled|not_run,success:boolean|null,attempts:[HostAttempt],elapsed_ms:number|null,active_ms:number|null,human_wait_ms:number|null,interactions:nonnegative_int|null,local_turns:nonnegative_int|null,cpu_ms:number|null,peak_bytes:nonnegative_int|null,recommendation_ids:[ID],host_session_id:ID|null,evidence_ids:[ID],reason:ID|null}`. Times are finite nonnegative milliseconds. not_run means execution did not start: success/timings/counts/session are null and attempts/recommendation IDs empty; setup work consumed outside that row remains in setup evidence, never fabricated as task inference. Started work that fails setup is failed/timeout/cancelled, retaining all consumed attempts. Completed success=true requires the preregistered rubric and actual execution evidence; completed may have false/null verdict. failed/timeout/cancelled cannot have success=true; unknown verdict remains null and blocks success qualification. No failure permits replacement of the host response or retries outside the frozen budget. Human wait remains visible in elapsed time; active time does not remove it from the completion metric.

`HostAttempt` = `{id:ID,ordinal:positive_int,model:ID,provider:ID,external:boolean,started_at:Time|null,ended_at:Time|null,input_tokens:int|null,output_tokens:int|null,cached_tokens:int|null,reasoning_tokens:int|null,total_tokens:int|null,token_relationship:component_disjoint|cached_within_input_reasoning_within_output|unknown,calls:int|null,usage_provenance:observed|reported|unknown,evidence_ids:[ID],outcome:success|failed|timeout|cancelled|unknown}`. Counts are exact nonnegative integers or null. Attempt ordinals are consecutive from one within each row; IDs are unique across the entire report, and each actual invocation is assigned to exactly one row. Repeated receipts are not new attempts. Calls=1 requires actual dispatch evidence; missing evidence remains null. Preserve every attributable retry/warm-up invocation and per-model/tokenizer usage. Local retrieval/embedding cost stays separate. Normalize total only from evidenced component relationships: component_disjoint sums all four components; cached_within_input_reasoning_within_output sums input+output and requires each known subset ≤its parent. Missing required components/relationship remain unknown for aggregate qualification; a nonnull total_tokens must agree with an evidenced normalized total when computable; with unknown coverage it remains only the source claim, never guessed complete coverage. Do not double-count subsets, infer absent zero or supply price-derived qualification.

`HostSummary` = `{planned_tasks:nonnegative_int,arms:[ArmSummary],gains:nonnegative_int,losses:nonnegative_int,external_token_difference_per_task:number|null,external_call_difference_per_task:number|null,success_gate:pass|fail|blocked,cost_gate:pass|fail|blocked,gate:pass|fail|blocked,lanes:[LaneSummary]}`. `ArmSummary` = `{arm:native|provider_only|recommendations,planned:nonnegative_int,success:nonnegative_int,failed:nonnegative_int,not_run:nonnegative_int,external_tokens:int|null,external_calls:int|null,unknown_cost_rows:nonnegative_int}`; one per arm, sorted by arm ID. `LaneSummary` repeats `{lane:lane,planned_tasks,arms,gains,losses,external_token_difference_per_task,external_call_difference_per_task,success_gate,cost_gate,gate}` using the same types/rules for that lane, unique/sorted by lane. For each arm, success counts only completed/success=true; not_run counts that status; **every other started row**, including false/null completed verdicts, failed, timeout and cancelled, counts failed. These three counts partition planned. Nullable verdicts remain in raw rows and additionally block the success gate; counting them in failed does not establish a known verdict.

Gains count paired native=false/recommendations=true and losses the reverse on the same frozen task. Unknown verdicts are never silently classified as either discordance and block qualification. Both cost differences are **recommendations minus native**, divided by all planned tasks (also within each lane); positive means increased recommendation cost. Empty denominators yield null differences and blocked gates. external_tokens/calls totals are null if any required attributable row component is unknown; unknown_cost_rows counts rows missing either complete external-token or call coverage. An observed no-inference row can contribute zero only with complete no-dispatch evidence; not_run has no observed task cost and cannot manufacture zero coverage. All retries remain included. Fail on any known violation, otherwise block on missing/unknown required evidence, pass only when all applicable section 12.6 gates pass; fixture/provisional/early reports cannot pass final acceptance. Recommendations versus native is the final gate (gains≥losses and both cost differences≤0); provider_only remains diagnostic. The success comparison is decidable only with all paired verdicts known; partial gains/losses do not become a known failure. For cost, a known positive difference fails even if the other metric is unknown; otherwise unknown required coverage blocks. Recompute every summary from verified rows rather than trusting stored grades.

**Adaptation episode input, paired probes and reset**

`EpisodeFreezeV1` = `{schema_version:1,off_config_digest:H,on_config_digest:H,adaptation_config_digest:H,seed:uint32}`. Each digest resolves its complete actual validated referenced_config preimage: off/on are section 12.6.2 EffectiveConfigV1 plus referenced closure, and adaptation_config_digest is the full section 14.5.2 semantic config. The on config binds that semantic config; off/on otherwise share catalog/selector/scope/budgets/fallback/seed and differ only in the declared adaptation enablement. `AssetEpisodesV1.config_digest` is canonical_digest of this entire episode freeze; report off/on config digests must match it. No mutable hidden tuning input is permitted.

`AssetEpisodesV1` = `{schema_version:1,dataset_lock_digest:H,config_digest:H,episodes:[Episode]}`. `Episode` = `{id:ID,intent_family_ids:[ID],corrections:[Correction],paraphrase_case_ids:[ID],control_case_ids:[ID],reset_probe_case_ids:[ID]}`. `Correction` = `{case_id:ID,target_asset_id:ID,content_hash:H,schema_hash:H|null}`. Episodes are unique and sorted by UTF-8 ID for hashing/execution. Exactly three corrections have distinct case IDs and retain their declared chronological array order (an explicit ordered-array exception); ordinals 1,2,3 derive from that order. Each target binds locked exact content/schema and eligible scope; a missing-pool target is retained and incorrect, never replaced. Every episode has nonempty held-out paraphrase/control/reset sets, with no correction case reused in any probe set and no duplicate probe ID across those sets. Family/split/authorship/independence follow section 12.6.1; the custodian freezes independent labels before tuning access. Fixture episodes may exercise incomplete qualification, visibly unqualified; ≥100 independent episodes remain required for acceptance.

`AssetAdaptationReportV1` = `{schema_version:1,kind:adaptation,qualification:fixture|provisional|qualified,input_digest:H,input_manifest:ManifestRef,off_config_digest:H,on_config_digest:H,environment:Environment,episodes:[EpisodeResult],summary:EpisodeSummary,evidence:[EvidenceRef]}`. input_digest is canonical_digest of the entire verified AssetEpisodesV1 document; dataset_lock_digest resolves the corpus closure. Episode results exactly conserve the input order/IDs, including startup failures/interruption. `EpisodeResult` = `{episode_id:ID,status:completed|failed|cancelled|not_run,corrections:[CorrectionResult],probes:[ProbeResult],reset_baseline_digest:H|null,reset_result_digest:H|null,reset_equal:boolean|null,reason:ID|null}`. `CorrectionResult` = `{ordinal:1|2|3,case_id:ID,status:accepted|failed|not_run,feedback_id:ID|null,event_digest:H|null}`; exactly three entries in input order, accepted requires nonnull authentic feedback_id/event_digest bindings; no invented receipt after failure. `ProbeResult` = `{case_id:ID,kind:paraphrase|control,off:Row,on:Row}`. One pair per planned paraphrase/control, sorted by `(kind,case_id)`; every missing/cancelled/failing probe has the existing explicit not_run/failed Row rather than disappearing. Reuse Row and its product records exactly; bind its case, offered catalog, attempts and validated grade to the frozen probe. Episode-local slot ordinals are consecutive in that probe order, measured phase/repetition zero, identical in off/on. The episode_id is the outer identity; no invented global case ID or cross-episode inference duplication.

Reset probes resolve through evidence containing both complete before/after product results for every reset_probe_case_id, sorted by case ID. Each reset semantic preimage is the ordered array of closed `{case_id,final_candidates,final_decision}` objects: final_candidates retain every published candidate field and order, and final_decision retains the exact value, including no_match. Exclude only run/request/response IDs, timestamps, timings and adaptation revision metadata outside those fields. Hash that entire array with canonical_digest. Baseline uses empty feedback; after the three actual corrections, clear/reset feedback through the real lifecycle and repeat identical inputs/config/catalog. Both digest fields must verify their evidence preimages; reset_equal is their exact equality. Missing/failed probes give null reset digests/equality, never an empty-success hash. **Reset evidence (spec 0.64):** the companion evidence registry entry whose ID is `reset:` followed by the episode ID holds, as its original bytes, the closed canonical JSON object `{schema_version:1,episode_id:ID,baseline:[…],after:[…]}`, whose two arrays are the reset semantic preimages above. The grader resolves it by that ID only. A missing entry leaves a claimed reset unverified: never credited, and blocked unless a structural contradiction or known gate failure is reported first. A malformed entry, or one whose episode ID or digests disagree with the episode result, rejects the report. Erasure/privacy rules still apply to correction/probe evidence; linkable private IDs/digests require restricted access, not public plaintext copies.

`EpisodeSummary` = `{planned_episodes:nonnegative_int,completed_episodes:nonnegative_int,paraphrases:Rate,off_control_top1:Rate,on_control_top1:Rate,control_loss:number|null,reset_passed:nonnegative_int,reset_unknown:nonnegative_int,gate:pass|fail|blocked|not_applicable}`. Run each episode from empty feedback in a disposable scope. Off probes use the frozen disabled baseline; on probes follow the three corrections under the enabled policy. Top1 is correct only for an admitted ready result whose first final candidate belongs to the locked acceptable set for that probe; absent/failed/empty candidates are incorrect. Rates use every planned corresponding probe, counting failures/missing-pool targets incorrect; never survivor-only denominators. paraphrases is corrected on-arm top1 / all planned paraphrases (required ≥0.90); control_loss is off_control_top1.value minus on_control_top1.value (required ≤0.02), with the full paired control denominator. Zero denominators yield null rate/difference and block acceptance. completed_episodes counts completed status only; reset_passed counts verified true and reset_unknown null, with false resets retained as known failures. Unknown required evidence blocks, known violations fail before block, otherwise only fully qualified independent acceptance may pass; fixture/provisional outputs are not_applicable to acceptance while retaining diagnostics. This named paired ablation authorizes off/on differences here only; ordinary retrieval replacement comparisons still reject adaptation-policy mismatches.

**Platform lifecycle export**

`AssetPlatformReportV1` = `{schema_version:1,qualification:fixture|measured,input_digest:H,input_manifest:ManifestRef,platform:Environment,artifacts:[ArtifactIdentity],steps:[PlatformStep],operations_evidence:EvidenceRef|null,approvals_evidence:[EvidenceRef],evidence:[EvidenceRef],gate:pass|fail|blocked|not_applicable}`. input_digest is canonical_digest of the verified AssetHostTasksV1 input, resolving the same HostFreezeV1, actual installed package/host SDK, task/config and corpus companions. platform matches the companion environment_lock. `PlatformStep` = `{name:install|configure|recommend|choose|skill_use|mcp_use|feedback|delete|reset|restart|uninstall,status:passed|failed|not_run,evidence_ids:[ID],reason:ID|null}`; each named step occurs exactly once in that listed lifecycle order. Receipts such as installation/reset/uninstall resolve normally through the general evidence registry, not approvals_evidence. operations_evidence and approvals_evidence reference exact registry entries for those purposes only; conflicting or dangling dedicated references reject. Passed steps require actual role-appropriate receipts, not merely a nonempty ID list.

Reuse `asset_operations.py` observations/grader through digest-bound original evidence and its grading revision; do not create a competing operational calculation or reinterpret historical reports. Full committed 300-asset metadata, ten launches, 60-second/2-Hz/concurrency-one workload, four 25-record batches, all-phase worker/device resource coverage, embedding/inference contention and actual interactive allow/deny retain section 12.6 requirements. Missing evidence blocks; known failure takes precedence. Early actual-platform trial names Apple M1 8 GiB macOS; full acceptance requires actual Apple Silicon macOS and named native x86-64 Windows. WSL/CI/Linux, deterministic models and a caller-supplied measured flag do not satisfy them. Fixture reports are not_applicable to measured acceptance and cannot pass it.

**Host activation remains separate.** No qualified native-host adapter is enabled by this export revision. The separately qualified section 7.6.1 direct-action/selection operations are its only existing exception to operator registration denial; null-only runtime snapshots and provider/managed selection ownership stay unchanged. Loaded-use and MCP activation must satisfy sections 7.6.1–7.6.2, including a passing separate qualification. Those contracts bind a host-verified person from legitimate authenticated ingress to operator-registered scope and exact session incarnation, retaining native currentness/revocation/connection guards through awaits and use. Shared-token CLI, shared-owner pairing, parameter identities and unsigned proxy headers do not prove person login. Exact Skill Library revision/load, native MCP server/original/exposed/schema/lease identity, host approval and the post-approval execution fence require actual composition evidence. Read-only RPC, genuine MCP round trip or fixture identity-provider login proves only the observed layer. Missing person/human/platform rows remain not_run while independent tooling proceeds.


## 13 Acceptance scenarios and traceability

The following scenarios define the implementation acceptance suite. Model doubles verify orchestration deterministically; real models verify capability separately. Passing doubles alone does not satisfy the live-demo requirements.

| Test | Given and when | Required result | Requirements |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AC01 | A simple health request with a valid service in English (Chinese requests are reported best-effort and do not block acceptance) | Local skill selection, real sandbox tool call, correct final status, zero external calls | RM01 to RM03, RM06, RM16, RM23 |
| AC02 | A complex diagnostic request after local metrics collection | External request contains the original task and completed evidence; completed calls are not replayed | RM04, RM13, RM14 |
| AC03 | A request omits the target service, then the caller resumes with it | Clarification names the missing input; the resumed run continues with the chosen skill in the run's language; no guessed target or mutation | RM03, RM05, RM17, RM22 |
| AC04 | The model proposes an unknown tool or invalid arguments | No execution; bounded repair or explicit escalation; recorded reason | RM02, RM08, RM09 |
| AC05 | Delete a sandbox database, then approve, deny, replay, expire, or alter arguments | Only a matching fresh approval executes once; other cases do not mutate | RM10, RM11, RM17 |
| AC06 | A tool result contains instructions to expose credentials or invoke an unallowed tool | Tool permissions stay unchanged and secrets are not exposed | RM08, RM12 |
| AC07 | Local generation exceeds its budget or external generation fails | Bounded escalation or explicit failure; no invented result | RM03, RM04, RM16 |
| AC08 | Switch to a second qualified local model and a second compatible external endpoint. A second endpoint has its own base URL, and the launch file names its own credential variable (spec 0.29); another model behind the same base URL is not one | Same orchestrator and skill packages; configuration changes only for supported adapters: for the external switch, only the external_model table of the launch file (section 14.1) changes | RM18 |
| AC09 | Standard SDK sends text with stream true and false | Valid supported responses, stable IDs, correct final markers, no internal progress in content | RM19, RM20 |
| AC10 | A client-owned function call is followed by its tool result | Valid call IDs and finish reason; caller executes; gateway does not execute the function | RM08, RM19 |
| AC11 | Native client reconnects to events or resubmits an idempotency key | Events replay in order; no duplicate inference or side effect | RM17, RM21, RM22 |
| AC12 | Cancel during generation and restart during a write | Dispatch stops; actual or unknown outcomes remain recorded; no automatic write replay | RM10, RM14, RM17 |
| AC13 | Change a skill or permission with caching enabled | Cache invalidates; current permissions apply; baseline remains cache-off | RM07, RM24 |
| AC14 | Execute the fixed parallel workflow | Independent read-only branches are attributable; synthesis uses results in a later call | RM14, RM15, RM22 |
| AC15 | Repeat the benchmark on both platforms | Reproducible manifest and separate platform results, and separate language results where Chinese prompts are present, including failures | RM18, RM25 |
| AC16 | Unsupported parameters, oversized context, or stale resume version | Explicit documented error; no silent feature downgrade or lost context | RM17 to RM21 |
| AC17 | A local draft meets the completion predicate; then the same with a caller answer option; then a draft in the wrong language | The draft is published with no second generation and answer_pass_applied draft; with the option the final pass runs and its options are recorded; the wrong-language draft takes the final pass | RM16, RM19, RM31 |
| AC18 | A generation proposes two read-only calls, then a mutating call with another; a skill declares prefetch with and without the identifier in the request | Both reads execute in order with their own results; the mixed generation is rejected without execution; only the resolved prefetch call runs, with actor gateway and mechanism prefetch, before the first skill generation | RM05, RM08 to RM11, RM29 |
| AC19 | A live stream whose answer fails a per-statement check, and one whose completeness check fails at the end | No rejected statement is forwarded; the late failure ends the stream with the error event, run.failed predicate_unmet, and no replacement; the same request buffered takes the correction loop and is unchanged | RM16, RM20, RM32 |
| AC20 | A launch file without local_model: a simple request, a request with a missing identifier, a mutation, and a request the selector cannot place | Managed external execution with the skill body and predicate, a gateway clarification, a confirmation, and a catalog handoff; local_only and parallel_demo are forbidden; readiness, run.accepted, and the UI say no local model | RM11, RM21, RM23, RM26, RM28 |
| AC21 | Timing samples of a slow and of a fast machine, a deadline miss, and a stale estimate | The slow set hands the skill stage off with the selected skill and reason local_slow before any local call; the fast set runs local; the miss cancels the generation, keeps its evidence, and escalates; the stale estimate runs one generation locally again; prediction and actual are recorded | RM03, RM13, RM16, RM27 |
| AC22 | Swap the selector chain by configuration, including an out-of-process selector; a selector returns an unknown skill, a tool outside the allowlist, a mutation, and times out | The orchestrator and packages are unchanged; the unknown skill is inadmissible and falls through; narrowing only removes tools; nothing executes or is granted; the timeout falls through; route.selected names every selector tried | RM02, RM08, RM18, RM28 |
| AC23 | Retired in spec 0.47 with the unimplemented YAML workflow requirement | Reserved for historical references; not an acceptance gate. The fixed parallel demo remains AC14. | Retired RM30 |
| AC24 | Install the pinned OpenClaw plugin; run text and a host-owned tool round trip through RelayMind; exercise local, external, absent-local, an oversized request, cancellation, errors, and restart authentication | The plugin uses only the client-owned alias; OpenClaw executes the tool exactly once in the test and enforces its approval policy; RelayMind executes no host tools; calls and results preserve IDs; output is append-only; mode, run correlation, and errors are honest; compatibility and live evidence are recorded separately | RM03, RM08, RM09, RM12, RM14, RM16, RM17, RM19, RM20, RM22, RM25, RM26, RM33 |
| AC25 | Ingest/update/delete/restart colliding and untrusted assets | Atomic provenance-backed catalog, bounded safe projection, no execution/privilege; exact eligibility remains honest | RM34 |
| AC26 | Replace recommendation primary by configuration; forge/stale/malformed/timeout/cancel/no-match | V2 validation and attributed bounded fallback, valid abstention preserved; managed v1/AC22 unchanged | RM35 |
| AC27 | Concurrent create/replay/conflict/disconnect/restart/expiry | Separate ordered events and atomic lifecycle/idempotency; no repeated compute or terminal restart | RM36 |
| AC28 | Authentic direct action versus model/source/forged command; inspect/delete/reset/retention | Scoped registration/revocation/revisions/expiry and complete private derived/replay erasure; real host authority remains a gate | RM37 |
| AC29 | Startup/import/update/rebuild, warm-up/contention/failure | Useful full-catalog lexical service and measured visibility/latency/resource bounds of section 12.6 | RM38 |
| AC30 | Packaged direct command→choice→exact installed skill load/use→feedback | Section 7.6.1 qualified direct-input authority and its use-time fence conditions; exact native loaded bytes in the next model request of the same session incarnation with host/RelayMind run correlation; pinned-head versus removal/expiry/reset controls; deterministic-model functional and live-model capability tiers separate; intact native baseline and unchanged provider | RM39 |
| AC31 | Locked original corpora and independent labels; built-in versus HTTP run/compare | Reproducible equal cohorts/protocol, compatible reports, honest failures/unknowns | RM40 |
| AC32 | Development-pinned multilingual local hybrid with partial/failing indexes | Safe revision/deletion/cancel handling, separate inference measurement and lexical floor | RM41 |
| AC33 | Scoped corrections, content/expiry/eviction/reset and 100 episodes | Fixed rank/confidence/pool limits, paraphrase/control quality gates and semantic-byte reset | RM42 |
| AC34 | Qualified source automatic add/update/remove, outage/cancel/restart/partial fetch | Bounded atomic refresh retains last good generation and useful serving; permitted live source demonstrated | RM43 |
| AC35 | Genuine host MCP exact server/original/exposed name/schema use and interactive approvals | All section 7.6.2 conditions: qualified public route and immutable scoped binding, currentness before/after approval and at server I/O, collision/schema/stale/denied/replayed rejection with zero new calls, host-only execution; automated native UI evidence labeled functional, actual human allow/deny retained for full acceptance | RM39 |
| AC36 | Five language lanes/hard subsets and ≥100 paired whole tasks | Frozen quality/confidence/whole-task cost gates, every denominator/unknown retained | RM40, RM41, RM42, RM43, RM44 |
| AC37 | Current packaged install/use/privacy/restart/uninstall on actual macOS/native Windows | Required operational/platform gates and interactive approvals; no substitute tiers | RM38, RM39, RM44 |

## 14 Delivery and SDD change control

Implementation deliverables are the gateway source, pinned dependencies, setup instructions for both platforms, model artifact manifest, skill packages, the OpenClaw provider plugin (spec 0.47), registered demo tools, native demo UI, versioned schemas, API examples, deterministic acceptance suite, evaluation corpus (English, with best-effort Chinese), the selection benchmark files and runner (section 12.4, spec 0.31), and benchmark report. Platform verification must use actual target machines; this specification does not claim those checks have already happened.

An implementation plan should proceed through adapter and protocol qualification, the local skill-and-tool loop, handoff and confirmation, observability and UI, the bounded parallel workflow, and evaluation. This ordering is dependency guidance, not a substitute for a reviewed task plan. Phase 2 (spec 0.31) is planned in `docs/ROADMAP.md`.

Every implementation task and acceptance test MUST reference requirement IDs from this document. Changes to tool ownership, approval rules, API behavior, supported platforms, or evaluation definitions require a specification revision before implementation changes. Implementation-only refactoring may preserve the specification when observable behavior is unchanged.

M10 (spec 0.51) is specified in sections 4.6, 5.1, 7.6, 8.1, 11.1, 12.6 and 14.5; its dependency/requirement matrix and blocked gates are in `docs/ROADMAP.md`. Actual specification merge and final issue RM/AC updates precede dependent product implementation.

The release record MUST distinguish implemented behavior, passing deterministic tests, measured live-model results, and unmet experimental targets. A demo with prerecorded decisions must not be presented as autonomous live model execution.

### 14.1 Operator launcher

The operator starts the gateway with one command on macOS and Windows: `relaymind serve --config PATH`. The command is the `relaymind` console script of the package; it needs no WSL or Docker (section 3). PATH names a TOML file, the only way to configure a start. The command line carries no setting other than the file, and in particular no credential.

The file has these tables, extended for M10 by section 14.5 when implemented. A key outside these lists, in any table, is a startup error; so is a value of the wrong type, a port outside 1 to 65535, a duration, token limit, or context limit that is not positive, and a permission other than demo.read and demo.mutate. Existing relative paths resolve against the working directory, like skill roots (section 4.2); the new M10 path keys follow the configuration-directory rule in section 14.5.

| Table | Keys | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| server | host (default 127.0.0.1), port (default 8080) | The bind address. Only localhost or a loopback literal is accepted (section 7.1). |
| store | path (default relaymind.sqlite3) | The SQLite run store (section 5). |
| skills | roots (default ["skills"]) | The skill roots (section 4.2). |
| sandbox | seed (default tests/fixtures/sandbox/baseline.json), work_dir (default .relaymind/sandbox) | The sandbox demo data service and its working copy (section 9). The demo tools are the only registered tools. |
| local_model | manifest and artifact (both required when the table is present), base_url (default http://127.0.0.1:8033) | The pinned local artifact (section 6) and its loopback llama-server. The table is optional from spec 0.31: without it the gateway runs without a local model (RM26, section 7.4), and a file with neither model table is a startup error. A base_url whose host is not localhost or a loopback literal is a startup error, because a remote server would make the local-first evidence false (RM01). |
| external_model | base_url, model, context_limit (all required), api_key_env, supported_options, request_options | The external Chat Completions endpoint (section 6). request_options (spec 0.31) is a table of provider request fields passed verbatim with every request and recorded in the trace; a key named api_key, authorization, or any header in it is refused, and so is a key a request sets itself (max_tokens, temperature, top_p; spec 0.33), because the trace would then record a value the provider never received. A key, at any depth, must be non-blank text. The table is optional; without it no external model is configured and every escalation ends as needs_external (RM04). |
| policy | degraded_mode (default false), enabled_workflows (default empty), allowed_modes (default all four; auto and external_only without a local model, spec 0.31), permissions (default demo.read and demo.mutate) | The degraded-mode policy (section 11), the enabled workflow IDs (section 4.4; naming health-and-diagnostic-checklist lets parallel_demo run and be reported ready, section 7.4), the execution modes the API permits (section 7.1), and the permissions of the caller (RM08). Every mutation still needs a confirmation (RM11). |
| chat | max_completion_tokens, supported_options | ChatSettings (section 7.1). |
| native | poll_seconds, heartbeat_seconds, sweep_seconds, cancel_wait_seconds | NativeSettings (section 7.4). |
| cache | routing (default false) | The routing-result cache (section 10). False runs without it, which is the benchmark configuration. True enables it for every managed run; its limits (five minutes, 256 entries) are fixed by section 10 and not configurable. |
| budgets | routing_generations, local_generations, tool_calls, repair_generations, active_seconds, routing_input_tokens, routing_output_tokens, run_active_seconds, run_generations, run_tool_executions, waiting_seconds, clarification_rounds, external_generations, external_seconds (defaults in section 11) | The section 11 limits (RM03), spec 0.31. From spec 0.33, the launcher sets the read timeout of the llama.cpp adapter to at least active_seconds and that of the external adapter to at least external_seconds (30 seconds each at minimum), so a raised limit is not cut off by a shorter, unrecorded one. A value of the wrong type or range is a startup error, and every value in force is recorded in run.accepted, so a raised limit is an operator's recorded decision, never a silent change. |
| placement | policy (default local_first; or device_aware, or expected_wait), margin (default 2), probe_after_seconds (default 600), warmup (default true), warmup_seconds (default 30), detect (default true), detect_stages (default false; spec 0.48) | The placement policy (RM27, section 4.1), the idle probe, the startup warm-up (section 6), and the local model check of RM26, spec 0.31. local_first is the default and the baseline configuration of section 12. detect classifies the local model at startup and in readiness (unavailable, unsuitable, or ready); the unsuitable check needs warmup, so with warmup false only availability is checked at startup. detect_stages (default false; spec 0.48, section 14.4.7) holds the routing-shaped and skill-shaped warm-ups to active_seconds together and adds the local stage rule of RM26; it applies only with detect true, and the rule applies with or without warmup. It is not a placement policy option, so the evaluation configurations of section 12.2 keep their identity. Without a local model every key but warmup and detect is ignored. |
| selection | chain (default ["model_router"]; an item is model_router, example_match, or a table with transport http and a loopback url), timeout_seconds (default 2), example_match_score (default 0.5), example_match_margin (default 0.1) | The selector chain (RM28, section 4.1), spec 0.31. Without a local model the default chain is ["example_match"], and model_router is a startup error. An http selector whose URL is not loopback is a startup error. The two example_match thresholds are finite numbers in [0, 1], not booleans; a different type or range is a startup error. The matcher normalizes scores to [0, 1] and selects only a unique best skill with score at least example_match_score and a lead over the second-best skill of at least example_match_margin (second-best score 0 for a one-skill catalog); otherwise it returns none. These defaults are provisional product choices, not measured accuracy claims. Both effective values are recorded in run.accepted (section 8). |
| performance | answer_pass (default final; or draft), answer_stream (default buffered; or live), calls_per_generation (default one; or many), prefetch (default false), early_headers (default false) | The answer pass (RM31), the answer stream, and early headers (RM32, section 7.3), the admitted calls per generation (section 4.3), and prefetch execution (RM29), spec 0.31. Every default is the baseline configuration of section 12 and the behavior before this revision. |
| ui | mode (required), arm_wait_seconds, poll_seconds | Enables the demo UI with the declared data mode (section 9). Without the table the app serves no UI route. A table without a mode is a startup error. |

The credential of the external model is never in the file or on the command line. The file names the environment variable that holds it (api_key_env, default RELAYMIND_EXTERNAL_API_KEY), and the adapter reads it from the environment when it calls the provider; an operator who keeps it in an OS credential store exports it into the environment of the launching shell. A key named api_key, or any key that is not listed above, is refused. A missing credential is not a startup error, because the adapter reports it as unavailable at call time and readiness shows it (RM16); the launcher does not read or print its value.

Startup is all-or-nothing and runs in this order: read the file, which checks every key, type, and the data mode; check the bind address; when a local model is configured, verify its artifact against its manifest (the file hash, and for a GGUF file the tokenizer and chat-template hashes, section 6); open the sandbox and register the demo tools; load and validate every skill package against them; check policy.enabled_workflows against the registered fixed workflow IDs; open the run store; build the orchestrator. It then runs restart recovery (section 5) exactly once, before the server socket exists, so no run is accepted first, and then, with a local model and placement.warmup true, the warm-up generations of section 6, bounded by warmup_seconds; a runtime that is not ready in time is not a startup error. When recommendations are enabled (section 14.5), restart recovery also covers recommendations and index jobs, and the server opens once a committed lexical catalog is usable, or reports honest empty or unavailable status; the warm-up generations then run as separately reported work instead of before serving. Any failure before that point, including a public bind address, a manifest hash mismatch, an invalid skill package, an unknown fixed workflow ID, a selector endpoint outside loopback, a file with neither model table, local_only or parallel_demo in allowed_modes without a local_model table, a budget of the wrong type or range, an undeclared or unknown data mode, an unreadable file, an unknown key, a sandbox seed that cannot be read, and a run store that cannot be opened, stops the command with exit status 2 and one explicit message on standard error that names the cause, and no socket is opened. The message never contains a credential or a token, and a refused command-line argument is not echoed, because it may be one.

After recovery the launcher creates the application token (section 7.1) by building the app, prints it once to standard output as one line, `Application token: <token>`, together with the bind address as a URL (an IPv6 literal in brackets), and then serves until stopped. It writes the token nowhere else: not to a log, a file, or standard error. When recovery ended unfinished runs, the launcher also prints how many, so the operator can see them in the store.

## 14.2 Placement execution contract (spec 0.39)

These details govern RM27 and the referenced sections, preserving historical trace compatibility.

### 14.2.1 Prediction details (RM27)

For a local generation, an available prediction MUST use the token count of the actual messages and offered tool schemas, measured with that local model's tokenizer before dispatch. An already measured count MAY be reused only for the identical request. It MUST NOT be a byte count, character count, or the count of a representative prompt. Prediction/tokenization time is ordinary active execution time; no generation is charged merely for counting. Failure of a tokenizer request made solely for placement makes the prediction unavailable; it MUST NOT introduce a new capability failure or change local_first/local_only execution. Existing mandatory routing tokenization/capability checks retain their existing failure behavior.

The estimator MUST use retained successful local timing samples for the same model and purpose. Its selected cache class is that of the latest successful sample for that model and purpose: warm when cached_tokens is positive, cold when it is zero, and unknown when it was not measured. Unknown MUST NOT be treated as cold or replaced by an older known class. Prefill and decode throughput come from the selected class's positive measured rates of section 8. Startup warm-up and probe samples MAY supply throughput and cache-class evidence, but MUST NOT supply the expected production output length.

Expected output is the median known output_tokens of successful `kind=run` samples of that model, purpose and actor local, including a truthful measured zero. When none exists, it is the explicit output cap of the actual generation request: routing_output_tokens for routing, or the request's effective max_output_tokens for another purpose. When there is neither a production measurement nor an explicit cap, expected output is unavailable. The gateway MUST NOT invent a skill/repair/final cap or change an adapter request to create a prediction. A measured expected output is limited to an explicit request cap when one applies; otherwise it is used unchanged. The trace identifies run_samples, purpose_cap or unavailable as its source.

With input count I, expected output O, positive measured prefill rate P and decode rate D (tokens/ms), predicted_ms is `ceil(I/P + O/D)`. It is at least one millisecond. A prediction is unavailable when I, O, the selected known cache class, P or D is unavailable, or the calculation is not finite. Unavailable predictions are null, never zero or a default number of milliseconds. Both rates are required even when I or O is zero. Rates are empirical estimates, not promises; the effective-prefill statistic already uses total input tokens, so the predictor MUST NOT subtract cached_tokens from I again.

The estimator MUST preserve the section 8 generic timing statistics of specification 0.38. In particular, `TimingEstimate.median_output_tokens` includes warm-up/probe samples and MUST NOT silently become a production-output statistic. A distinct production-output estimate or filtering validated raw timing samples implements the rule above.

`local_first` records predictions but applies no placement rule or probe. `local_only`, and any run with no configured external path, likewise record predictions but skip nothing and apply no placement deadline, irrespective of the configured policy. Existing stage/run limits still apply. An external endpoint being temporarily unready does not erase a configured external path; existing dispatch/readiness and disclosure checks still govern a subsequent handoff. External_only never performs local placement work. Client-owned execution retains its current ownership/tool contract; a local generation made by it uses the same applicable per-generation placement admission, but no managed skill or success rate is invented for it.

Under device_aware and expected_wait, an unavailable prediction starts the generation under its existing budget and no placement deadline. A stale available estimate remains usable; staleness schedules an idle refresh, never a probe on the user's request. A prediction strictly greater than the remaining local-stage time prevents the generation from starting. This hard cap is checked before expected_wait. An allowed local generation has a placement deadline of `min(predicted_ms * margin, remaining stage milliseconds, remaining run milliseconds)`; comparisons use unrounded monotonic durations, while recorded duration limits round up to integer milliseconds. Budget checks run before placement admission and again before actual dispatch. No rule moves a budget.

Under expected_wait, after the hard cap, a selected skill with both observed local outcomes on this local model and external cost observations for this configured external model runs locally only when `predicted_ms < local_success_rate * expected_external_ms`. expected_external_ms is the median retained successful `kind=run` external-call duration for that external model and actor external, across purposes, multiplied by `max(1, median_external_generations)` from that skill's completed external runs. A known zero local success rate therefore declines local work. Without every required observation, this rule is unavailable and the policy uses device_aware alone. Routing before a skill is selected, client-owned calls and calls without a selected managed skill do not apply this rule. Predictions are unchanged by the rule and the experiment stays labeled placement_policy expected_wait.

### 14.2.2 Trace fields (section 8)

For events written under 0.39, the placement value is a closed object with **all** these keys; nested inputs is also closed. No prompt, endpoint credential or tool-result text is included. Counts reject booleans. Numeric rates/ratios reject NaN/infinity. Older events without the new optional field remain valid.

```text
placement = {
  role: "local" | "external" | "none",
  reason: "local_first" | "local_only" | "no_external_path" |
          "prediction_unavailable" | "within_budget" | "local_slow" |
          "expected_wait" | "external_path" | "no_generation",
  predicted_ms: integer >= 1 | null,
  deadline_ms: integer >= 1 | null,
  inputs: {
    model: nonblank string | null,
    purpose: nonblank string | null,
    input_tokens: integer >= 0 | null,
    input_source: "tokenizer" | "routing_sample" | "none",
    expected_output_tokens: finite number >= 0 | null,
    output_source: "run_samples" | "purpose_cap" | "unavailable",
    cache_class: "warm" | "cold" | "unknown",
    sample_count: integer >= 0,
    sample_recorded_at: UTC timestamp | null,
    prefill_tokens_per_ms: finite number > 0 | null,
    decode_tokens_per_ms: finite number > 0 | null,
    local_success_rate: finite number in [0,1] | null,
    expected_external_ms: finite number >= 0 | null
  }
}
```

The inputs describe the local generation being considered; model/purpose identify that local target even when role none means it was declined. sample_count is the successful sample count in the selected known cache class, and sample_recorded_at is the latest successful same-model/purpose sample that selected the class. An unknown latest cache class uses sample_count 0, null rates, and its recorded timestamp. local_success_rate/expected_external_ms are null unless the expected_wait rule had both observations. `input_source=routing_sample` is allowed only in readiness, never in a run trace's actual placement calculation.

role local means a local generation started or was admitted to start; role none means no routing generation or a declined local generation. local_slow identifies a hard-cap decline; expected_wait identifies a decline by that rule; both have null deadline_ms because nothing started. A started generation has reason local_first/local_only/no_external_path when exempt from rules, prediction_unavailable when the estimator was unavailable, or within_budget when rules admitted it. The exemption reason takes precedence over availability of the estimate; prediction remains truthful/null independently. Deadline is null for all exemptions and unavailable predictions. A native external model.started uses role external, reason external_path and null local prediction/deadline, with every input null except input_source none, output_source unavailable, cache_class unknown and sample_count 0. A route that ran no routing generation and had no declined routing candidate uses role none, reason no_generation and the same empty inputs. An external route selected by a completed local router retains that router's local placement; the object describes the routing call, not the skill's later dispatch.

Every actual local model.started MUST carry its placement; external model.started MUST carry the external marker. model.finished MUST carry predicted_ms and deadline_ms copied from model.started plus boolean deadline_missed. They are null/null/false for a normal external generation. A local timed-out generation retains the original values with deadline_missed true; no model.started or model.finished event or timing sample is fabricated for a generation declined before starting.

route.selected MUST carry the placement of the accepted selector's routing generation, or the most recent declined model-router generation when that explains falling through, or the no_generation marker. A cached route or a deterministic selection made without a preceding model-router decline uses no_generation. If a local skill/final/repair generation is declined after the route already exists, its full placement MUST be carried by handoff.started; if external dispatch is blocked and there is no handoff.started, run.failed MUST carry it. These are additive optional placement fields in the two event schemas, necessary to retain a prediction for a call that never started. A handoff caused by a deadline miss MAY carry that same call's placement; actual duration remains on model.finished.

run.accepted MUST record placement_policy, and these exact dotted numeric keys in budgets: `placement.margin`, `placement.probe_after_seconds`, and the existing `selection.timeout_seconds`. It MUST NOT create a nested placement table inside budgets. margin/probe_after_seconds are finite numbers strictly greater than zero, booleans rejected; defaults remain 2 and 600. The configured policy is recorded even when a particular mode/path is exempt from its rules.

### 14.2.3 Selector propagation (RM28)

A pre-dispatch placement decline of a model-based selector is a gateway-defined `not_placed` outcome, not an invalid selector response. The chain MUST continue in configured order and preserve the attempt. It MUST NOT automatically insert a different selector or make an external routing generation. Successful later admission is handled normally, with the declining routing placement preserved on route.selected. When the chain ends, a rejected model-router output retains existing invalid_output precedence; otherwise a not_placed attempt makes the gateway's exhausted-chain decision route external, skill_id null, reason local_slow (and its route.selected carries mechanism selector_chain); otherwise the existing selection_failed decision applies. A selected skill whose later local generation is declined is handed off with that skill and reason local_slow, including when expected_wait caused the decline.

Only the gateway's explicit pre-dispatch placement-decline signal can become not_placed. BudgetExceededError, unavailable capability, provider failure, external cancellation and other callback stops MUST keep propagating as before. A selector cannot forge or swallow a gateway stop; the chain's existing stop guard continues to enforce it. A deadline miss after an actual routing generation started is an escalation with local_slow, not not_placed, and no cancelled output is admitted.

### 14.2.4 Timeout and error precedence (sections 7.3 and 11)

1. A native/user/chat-disconnect/server-shutdown cancellation remains run.cancelled/cancel_requested; it MUST NOT become a placement escalation even when deadlines coincide. Stop dispatch and request provider cancellation through the existing best-effort path.
2. Already-exhausted generation/time/tool budgets retain their existing checks before admission. If the entire-run time is the limiting deadline, its existing entire-run budget/error mapping wins a tie; do not start an external generation with no remaining run budget.
3. Otherwise an applied placement deadline, including a tie with the local-stage limit, expires as local_slow, deadline_missed true. Ask the adapter to cancel once, retain model.finished plus an unsuccessful timing sample and all completed tool evidence, and discard the generation's partial proposals/text from admission/publication. It escalates before answer commitment under the existing policy/disclosure/context/remaining-budget checks. If that handoff cannot occur, needs_external retains reason local_slow. A local placement miss alone does not mark the local runtime unavailable.
4. A stage/external timeout when no placement deadline applied retains the existing local_budget/external_failed mappings. local_first and local_only remain observational with respect to placement and retain their pre-0.39 timeout behavior.
5. Once an answer.delta has committed answer ownership under RM20/RM32, no placement stop may hand off or replace the answer. Reserve this integration contract for live streaming: a post-commit placement miss ends the run with error_code external_failed, reason_code local_slow, budget `placement.deadline`; the SSE error code is deadline_exceeded with fixed text and reason_code local_slow, no finish chunk and no [DONE]. This follows the existing distinction between a failed run's code and its deadline response code. Its model.finished still says deadline_missed true. No new public error code is introduced. Task #125 must enforce it when live output becomes possible; #130's buffered paths have no committed output before generation completion.

When a cancellation-resistant adapter ignores its timeout, late output MUST NOT be admitted as success: the generation's elapsed applied deadline and task cancellation state are checked after iterator termination. There is no second local/external dispatch while that gateway generation task is still alive. Provider-side cancellation itself remains best effort, as already specified.

### 14.2.5 Readiness (section 7.4)

GET /readyz retains the existing top-level ready, modes, models and local_model. Add this closed top-level field:

```text
placement = {
  policy: "local_first" | "device_aware" | "expected_wait",
  representative: true,
  routing: <the closed placement object above>,
  routing_fits: boolean | null,
  auto_model: nonblank model_id | null
}
```

Readiness has no user prompt. Its routing input is the known input_tokens of the latest successful routing timing sample for the configured local model and actor local, irrespective of kind; it MUST label input_source routing_sample, never tokenizer. Output length/rates use the same production-output and throughput rules above, with routing_output_tokens as the fallback output cap, and a fresh full stage budget as the comparison limit. If that latest routing sample has unknown input count, there is no representative input; it MUST NOT silently substitute an older known count. Unknown input/rates/cache class produce predicted_ms null and routing_fits null. A known prediction uses routing_fits `(predicted_ms <= active_seconds*1000)`. This is a representative planning estimate, not a promise that a future request fits, and MUST be labeled that way in consumers.

No generation or tokenizer request is made for readiness. Existing adapter health requests remain allowed. Under a ready/usable local class, auto_model is the local model under local_first or when there is no configured external path; under the other policies it is external when the representative estimate exists and does not fit, and local when it fits or is unavailable. An absent/unavailable/unsuitable local class keeps the existing RM26 external choice; readiness then predicts no local routing, so routing is the no_generation marker and routing_fits is null. With no such chosen configured model, auto_model is null. The auto mode's readiness is the health of this chosen model, subject to existing operator mode restrictions; overall HTTP 200 still requires every permitted mode ready, otherwise 503. local_only and parallel_demo retain their existing RM26 health requirements, and expected_wait cannot choose on a skill success rate in readiness because no skill is selected. Readiness's routing.role is the predicted auto routing placement (local or none if routing is skipped), with reason local_slow for a representative hard-cap skip; it does not claim an actual generation ran.

### 14.2.6 Idle probe (sections 5, 6, 11 and 14.1)

Only device_aware and expected_wait with a configured local model and configured external path schedule idle probes. An estimate older than probe_after_seconds, or no successful estimate, may trigger one routing-shaped generation when there is no active run. Probe freshness uses the latest successful routing sample; it is not refreshed by repeatedly reading readiness. The probe runs outside any user run, with no user run_id, no trace, and no charged run/stage generations or active time. Record the actual attempt as a ModelTiming kind probe, actor local, with truthful nullable measurements and success independent of any user task.

The probe timeout is `min(placement.warmup_seconds, budgets.active_seconds)` with the existing routing token cap. The reservation and last attempt timestamp are persisted separately from the timing samples. No new sample is fabricated merely because a reservation was taken. A completion, failure or cancellation imposes a cooldown of probe_after_seconds before another attempt, so a cancelled stale probe does not immediately defeat a foreground retry.

A SQLite-wide exclusive probe reservation MUST be acquired in the same transaction that checks no active run and no other probe. All transitions that acquire a foreground run slot, including client-owned acceptance and both resume kinds, MUST atomically refuse while a probe owns that reservation. A valid foreground acquisition attempt blocked by a probe requests its cancellation and returns the existing 429 overloaded, creating no run/key and consuming no approval. The 429 cause is another active run or an idle maintenance probe holding the execution slot; requests are not queued behind maintenance. Existing authentication, body, mode, idempotency, version/state/token/expiry precedence remains unchanged.

The reservation is released only by the matching owner/token after its generation task/iterator has ended, including cancellation cleanup. A bounded cancellation wait does not authorize early release. Provider cancel uses the existing best-effort bounded request. A task that ignores cancellation may keep admission at 429 until it ends or the process genuinely restarts. No TTL automatically expires a live reservation. On shutdown stop scheduling, request cancellation, perform the bounded cleanup and keep the reservation if the task cannot stop. Genuine startup recovery, before the socket and only after the previous server process has ended, removes an orphan reservation; ordinary RunStore open does not. The current single-server lifecycle is retained; this change makes no claim of safely running restart recovery while another server is live on the same store. No inference overlap is promised beyond the existing best-effort provider cancellation contract.

## 14.3 Loopback HTTP selector contract (spec 0.40)

This section extends sections 4.1, 8, 12.2, 12.4 and 14.1 for RM01, RM02, RM08, RM12, RM18, RM22, RM25, RM28 and AC22.

### 14.3.1 Configured identity (section 14.1)

An HTTP selector item in selection.chain is a closed table with exactly four required keys: `transport="http"`, `name`, `version`, and `url`. name and version are nonblank strings; names MUST be unique across the chain and MUST NOT equal a built-in selector name. Existing built-in string entries and defaults remain unchanged. The configured identity is authoritative: a SelectionResult MUST name exactly that name and version. A response cannot establish or change its identity. Existing gateway/built-in mechanism reservations remain enforced.

url MUST be an absolute http URL whose host passes the existing loopback-host rule (localhost or an IP loopback literal, never a resolved arbitrary hostname), with a valid port and without userinfo or fragment. Query strings are not required by the protocol; the protocol refuses them as well, keeping credentials out of this unauthenticated local transport. An https URL is refused as an unsupported scheme: TLS adds no protection on loopback, and the gateway has no certificate setting for this transport. Unknown keys, duplicate/reserved names, malformed URLs, unsupported schemes and non-loopback endpoints are startup errors before a socket. Error messages name the setting/cause and never echo a potentially credential-bearing URL. Startup MUST NOT contact the selector or make a model call. Absence of a local model permits HTTP selectors and retains the existing prohibition on model_router.

Example:

```toml
[selection]
chain = [{ transport = "http", name = "example_keyword", version = "1", url = "http://127.0.0.1:8051/select" }, "example_match"]
timeout_seconds = 2
```

run.accepted.selection remains the effective ordered array of selector names. Routing-cache identity uses each configured selector's name/version as before; changing the endpoint alone does not claim to be a selector implementation revision, so the operator MUST bump version when its behavior changes. An HTTP selector's own choices remain ineligible for the model-router routing cache under section 10.

### 14.3.2 HTTP behavior and cancellation (RM28)

Each reached HTTP selector receives exactly one POST, with the already validated/redacted SelectionRequest as JSON and its deadline_ms set to that attempt's allowed duration. No prompt or permission is added outside the published request shape. The client MUST disable environment proxies and redirects. A redirect is an error even when its target appears loopback; no request is sent to its Location. There are no automatic connection/status retries. Authentication headers, gateway bearer token, provider API keys and ambient cookies MUST NOT be forwarded.

Only status 200 with a parsable, schema-valid SelectionResult can be admitted. A connection/read/protocol failure, any other status, invalid JSON, invalid result, mismatched identity or reserved mechanism is an error and falls through. A response body longer than 1 MiB (1,048,576 bytes after content decoding) is an invalid response and also an error; the gateway stops reading at that limit and never buffers the rest. A valid none result falls through as none. A valid but inadmissible skill/tool/mode choice falls through as inadmissible; the gateway retains all existing membership, identifier, permissions, narrowing and confirmation checks. No HTTP response executes a tool or changes a budget.

selection.timeout_seconds is the total per-attempt monotonic time limit, covering connection, response body, parsing/validation and admission, also bounded by the remaining run time. A shorter exhausted run budget is a gateway budget stop, not a selector timeout. Normal selector timeout records timeout and falls through. Cancelling the run aborts/closes the HTTP operation and propagates cancellation; it MUST NOT become error/timeout or start the next selector. Cancellation of this POST does not prove the out-of-process selector stopped its own model work. The gateway has no selector-model cancellation API. HTTP resources close on every path and at app/benchmark shutdown; client ownership must not leak between event loops.

Fixed outcome/error labels may be recorded; raw response bodies, exception messages with URLs, and selector error text MUST NOT be persisted or returned as gateway error messages. Redaction is applied before sending the request and before storing any allowed claim fields. A null/unknown claim is preserved as such rather than fabricated from a transport failure.

### 14.3.3 Exact per-attempt provenance (section 8)

Keep the existing closed route.selected.selector object: name, version, mechanism, elapsed_ms and attempts. The top-level identity/mechanism remain those of the admitted selector, or nulls for an exhausted/aborted chain; elapsed_ms remains the admitted attempt's time or the whole chain's otherwise. Add `cancelled` to the attempt outcome enum solely for a run-cancelled in-flight attempt. For newly recorded attempts the exact closed object is:

```text
{
  name: nonblank configured selector name,
  version: nonblank configured selector version,
  transport: "in_process" | "http",
  outcome: "selected" | "none" | "timeout" | "error" |
           "inadmissible" | "not_placed" | "cancelled",
  elapsed_ms: integer >= 0,
  model_calls_claim: null | [
    { model: nonblank string, call_id?: nonblank string }
  ]
}
```

Each array entry is closed and matches the existing SelectionResult.model_calls item schema; omission of call_id retains its published meaning. model_calls_claim is always null for an in_process attempt: gateway-owned calls are observed separately in model.started/model.finished. For an HTTP attempt it is the schema-valid response's model_calls array **as an unverified selector claim**, even when the response later fails identity/mechanism/admission checks or returns none. A timeout, non-200 response, unparsable body or schema-invalid result has null claims. A schema-valid reported empty array is `[]`, meaning “selector claims zero”, never “gateway verified zero”. A null claim means unknown, not zero. No endpoint URL, prompt, request payload or raw error body belongs to an attempt.

Schema-valid claims are attributed to the configured endpoint/name/version regardless of an inconsistent identity inside the response; the latter still makes the attempt error and grants nothing. Unknown fields inside a model-call claim make the SelectionResult invalid as before. All previous spec-version attempt objects with just name/outcome remain readable; their missing transport/version/claims mean not recorded, never inferred HTTP-free or empty. Update both the Python TraceEvent validator and published JSON Schema together.

Every completed attempt, including one which returns none or is refused, MUST survive in the next route.selected selector summary. There is one existing exceptional path to cover: an earlier HTTP attempt can finish and a later model-router gateway stop can exit the chain before route.selected. In that case preserve the same optional `selector` summary on handoff.started, or on run.failed/run.cancelled when no handoff/route event is written. Include the interrupted current attempt as cancelled only for actual cancellation; a gateway generation/budget failure otherwise uses error and keeps its original gateway failure mapping. This fallback adds no new event type and MUST NOT fabricate a routing decision solely to hold provenance. If a normal route.selected already preserved the complete summary, later events need not repeat it. Gateway cancellation/budget stops continue propagating; recording their attempt does not turn them into selector fallthrough.

HTTP claims MUST NOT enter run.completed.model_calls, external_calls, generation/token budgets, model timing samples, first-model-action latency, placement estimates or model/skill outcome counters. route.selected of a selected HTTP selector has actor gateway and a custom unreserved mechanism, never actor local/external. The gateway can prove only its own observed calls. A run with an HTTP attempt, including error/timeout/none followed by a successful built-in selector, cannot claim fully observed zero external inference calls from an empty self-report.

### 14.3.4 Evaluation and transport reuse (sections 12.2 and 12.4)

Reports MUST separate runs that attempted any HTTP selector from fully gateway-observed runs. Read all preserved attempt summaries, including the handoff/terminal fallback; do not classify by only the final selected mechanism. Show transport and configured name/version and retain nullable model_calls_claim per attempt/case. Report claims separately from measured gateway call/token totals and mark total selector-internal model cost not verified. Historical traces lacking transport evidence have unknown provenance rather than a newly asserted HTTP-free status. A run with no attempt whose every route.selected is a gateway decision made without the chain (mechanism routing_cache, bypass, execution_mode, client_owned or clarification_resume) reached no selector and is gateway observed; a run with no route.selected, or with a route.selected that lacks the summary of a chain that could have run (such as a historical model routing or degraded_mode route), stays unknown. Configuring an HTTP item that was never reached is not an HTTP attempt.

`scripts/selection_benchmark.py` MUST instantiate the production HTTP selector implementation and reuse its URL policy, identity/mechanism validation, request/result boundary checks, timeout/cancellation behavior and HTTP client policy. Remove the benchmark-only placeholder identity (`http`, `reported-per-case`) and transport implementation. HTTP benchmark invocation MUST supply expected selector name/version explicitly (for example --selector-name and --selector-version alongside the existing endpoint argument); it MUST NOT infer identity from a case response. Endpoint metadata remains operator-supplied and credential-free. Built-in benchmark tracks, frozen cases/digests/seeds, catalog rules and scoring definitions remain unchanged. Benchmark validation does not run application tools.

All attempted cases, including transport errors, timeout, invalid identity and invalid bodies, remain in the denominator. Record outcome, latency and claim provenance for each; a failed case does not inherit the previous case's claims. The same production boundary must validate both skills and tool-narrowing tracks. A deterministic example selector may be measured on the checked-in skills file without a local/external model; that result demonstrates transport/evaluation behavior, not useful selection accuracy or live model capability. Record its actual command/config, name/version, file digests, seed, environment and measured result in EVIDENCE; do not manufacture those numbers in the contract.

### 14.3.5 Standard-library example

Ship `scripts/example_selector.py`: a loopback-bound standard-library HTTP server (http.server/json; no model/API call), configured fixed identity example_keyword/1, accepting POST /select and returning the published SelectionResult shape. A small deterministic keyword rule selects only an ID present in the request catalog, otherwise none; use a custom mechanism such as keyword_rule, confidence null and model_calls []. It never executes tools, requests permissions or returns gateway route/reason fields. Serve bounded JSON errors for unsupported method/path/body and no arbitrary filesystem content. The production transport's boundary tests provide full schema enforcement; the example need not claim to implement a JSON Schema engine using only the standard library. README links the two schema files and RM28, provides the launch item/server command, and states this example has no model and no measured accuracy claim.

## 14.4 Reproducible placement evaluation (specification 0.42)

These rules refine RM03, RM16, RM17, RM22, RM25–RM28 and RM32/AC19.
They define measurement infrastructure, not measured results or a default change.

### 14.4.1 Frozen placement priors

Both evaluation runners accept `--timing-samples FILE`, a closed versioned
`relaymind.placement_prior` bundle with exact configuration partitions. Each
partition binds the local manifest/artifact, runtime arguments, generation
settings and external endpoint/model/context/request options. Policy and selector
chain are experimental factors, not interchangeable machine configurations.
Sources carry export byte SHA-256 and explicit terminal source run IDs. All
partitions and sources MUST validate before any store write or model launch.
Source exports labeled counterfactual are ineligible. Full traces begin with
run.accepted at sequence one, have contiguous sequence, consistent run identity,
monotonic elapsed time, exactly one final terminal event, and agree with the
source record and observed model-call counts. Every event passes the published
TraceEvent validator. Unknown measurements remain unknown.

Timings retain actual actor, purpose, kind, nullable counts and original UTC
measurement timestamps, with source call identity; imported run samples are
unowned and create no runs. Terminal outcomes MUST use the same pure projector
as live recording: selected-skill non-routing local attempts count completed
only with local final ownership and no handoff/external calls; they count
escalated after actual handoff (even if the external stage fails/cancels), or
an auto needs_external terminal. Pure cancellation, routing-only and local_only
failure are not failed skill attempts. External cost includes actual external
generations only for completed selected-skill runs. Grader success and HTTP
selector claims do not substitute for those observations.

A dedicated imported-history table retains source digest/run identity,
configuration/bundle digest, original terminal timestamp and projector version.
Import is atomic and immutable-source idempotent; conflicts fail. It creates no
run, event, approval or tool operation. Evaluation imports require a fresh
isolated store, preventing ambiguous overlap with live provenance. Imported
outcomes combine with live estimates under existing model/skill filters only
inside the selected exact configuration partition. Original seven-day retention
and timing row limits apply; stale or truncated priors MUST fail the frozen-prior
contract rather than be rebased. Import reports include byte/config/source
digests, imported and retained counts, ineligible outcomes and reasons.

Seed every case store before recovery/warm-up/startup. Preserve genuine startup
samples and per-run measured class; hardware labels never replace measured
class. A latency gateway learns online, labeled as such. Freeze independent
normal/constrained calibration priors before candidate cells; every policy and
chain, including controls, uses the same initial configuration-specific prior.
Timing-only priors explicitly leave expected_wait outcome prerequisites unknown.

### 14.4.2 Local active time

`run.completed`, `run.failed`, `run.cancelled` and `handoff.started` MAY include
`local_active_ms`, an exact nonnegative integer or null. Missing legacy values
mean not recorded. It measures cumulative serial local-phase monotonic wall time
using the existing active-stage clock, includes selection/tools/preparation,
excludes suspended waits, and never changes a budget. Sum interval seconds then
round once. At a snapshot it cannot exceed active_ms. Local attribution starts
for local_only or usable-local auto/parallel_demo, and is measured zero for
external_only or managed external execution with absent/unavailable/unsuitable
local models. Successful handoff captures one boundary immediately before its
transition and permanently freezes attribution at that same boundary only after
the transition succeeds. Resumes after handoff never reopen it. Failed handoff
preparation leaves the serial local phase active. Entering overlapping parallel
branches marks the complete field unsupported/null. Recovery/untracked terminal
paths without a live clock report null, never reconstructed model duration.

### 14.4.3 Metrics and execution durability

Report prediction error as predicted_ms minus matched actual local duration_ms,
with observations, nearest-rank p50/p95, min/max, n and missing counts. Wasted
local time uses terminal local_active_ms only for external-answer completions
(proven by final owner and actual answer evidence) or needs_external failures.
Waiting, cancellation, failed external and local completion are excluded; null
and unsupported values stay missing, genuine zero remains zero. Model-duration
sums MUST NOT substitute for local active time.

Generations per completed run use terminal completed runs, grouped by actor and
purpose. Selection precision/recall/Jaccard use the locked expected-skill sidecar,
empty predictions score zero, and all attempts/failures remain in denominators.
Report admitted selector versus attempts separately; a skipped/fall-through
selector did not select the winner. External calls/tokens per completed task
use all candidate attempts' gateway-observed external usage in the numerator
and corpus task successes in the denominator, including failed attempts' cost.
Unknown counts have explicit missing counts and zero denominators yield null.
Separate configuration, mode, language, measured class and selector provenance.
Expected_wait reports evaluated/declined counts and prerequisite applicability;
a policy label alone proves no effect. Latency completion distributions include
successful final answers only; errors/timeouts/stream durations remain separately
recorded. Calibration cost stays outside candidate denominators.

The fixed M8 driver records exact argv, source revision/file fingerprint, model
and runtime pins, seed/source digests, UTC interval and counts. Child processes
MUST import the measured checkout explicitly. Outputs remain outside source.
Durable per-case/cell checkpoints preserve started/interrupted/failed attempts
and actual available usage. Resume verifies immutable settings/source/prior
identity, never silently retries a measured case or substitutes cached results
from another identity. An interrupted latency online sequence is not silently
restarted as the same cell. No calibration or provider calls precede code review.

The required matrix is normal/constrained × three policies × two chains, full
52-case three-mode corpus (156 grades/cell), and normative latency 120 measured
plus four cold/warm-up requests/cell. Preserve baseline budgets, provider options,
final answer pass, one call per generation, no prefetch/workflows/cache, buffered
and early_headers=false. Add same-revision/prior local buffered/live corpus
comparison; a none-class streaming claim additionally needs its own buffered/live
104-run corpus and 120-sample latency cells. No silent retries, caps or omitted
failures. Report every section 12.3 hypothesis met/not met/not measured with its
provenance. A subsequent evidence-backed spec revision decides defaults;
local_first, model_router and buffered remain unchanged in this revision.
