### 14.4.4 M7 measurement and comparison coverage (specification 0.43)

M7 reports (RM16, RM25, RM26, RM28, RM29, RM31, RM32) retain the
run-level tool rubric and additionally count correct model tool-and-argument
proposals over observed model proposals. A prefetch-only run has model proposal
n=0. Prefetch correctness counts resolved tool.proposed events whose mechanism
is prefetch against acceptable_calls, whether admitted or rejected. Workflow
proposals, unresolved bindings and budget-skipped declarations are excluded;
admission, rejection and execution counts remain separate diagnostics.
Completed answer owners are local, external, gateway or unknown, with completed
eligibility and missing evidence counts. Legacy owner inference stays labeled;
new measurements require a proven local owner to claim local completion.

Corrections retain every terminal completed run in the denominator. A missing
trace or metric is unknown, never zero; report known count, measured completed
runs, missing completed runs and missing rejected durations. A population with
missing coverage has no complete-population correction rate or duration total.
First-token observations join model.started to model.finished by call_id and
retain started-but-unfinished calls with null timings. Report model, actor,
purpose, call_id, n/eligible/missing and distributions; never derive first token
from total duration. Cold requests, launcher warmup, runner warmups and measured
requests are separate populations. Legacy token totals are known subtotals.

Corpus selector scores refer to the initial request, before clarification resume.
Repeated progress histories of the same round do not duplicate attempts. Report
per name/version/transport attempted, failed, skipped, scored and unobserved
counts. Earlier failed selectors receive empty predictions, never the fallback
winner; never-reached selectors are skipped. Singleton scores from built-in
selectors do not claim unrecorded third-party candidate-set accuracy. Missing
selection observations are explicit and prevent a complete-population score.
Regrading shares live metric enrichment, preserves measured application and
configuration plus previous analysis provenance, and records the source-byte
digest and analyst revision in a distinct output artifact.

The M7 matrix is an offline plan unless execution is explicit, and uses the
same checkpoint, immutable identity, reconciliation and cost rules as M8.
At reviewed revision R it includes baseline (156 corpus runs), draft (104),
many calls (104), prefetch (104), draft+many+prefetch (156), and no-local with
example_match (104); two local-only latency cells each have 120 measured,
one cold and three runner-warmup requests, with early_headers changed only in
the second. Neither latency cell configures an external provider or a prior.
An optional controlled criteria-and-named-correction ablation uses a separately
reviewed explicit revision R-minus-criteria (104 auto/local_only runs), never a
product flag. For this experiment only, the section 4.4 requirement to send
fixed/value completion criteria and name failed checks in corrections is waived:
R-minus-criteria omits those messages and uses the existing generic correction.
Its completion predicates, evidence, permissions, budgets, layout and local path
remain those of R. This exception is not a shipping configuration or default.
Without it the isolated criteria hypothesis is not measured.
Baseline settings and budgets stay unchanged in every other dimension.
Reuse is limited to baseline and no-local corpus exports with exact source,
effective configuration, runtime/model/provider, corpus/sidecar and fixture
identity, including an explicitly null prior. Seeded M8 controls cannot be M7
baselines. Shared physical attempt cost is counted once by immutable identity,
and all failed/interrupted predecessor attempts retain cost.

Analysis validates immutable exports, renders missing coverage and comparisons,
and enumerates section 12.3 hypotheses as met, not met or not measured. Missing
required metrics fail reporting acceptance; a measured missed hypothesis is a
valid finding. Historical before/current after comparisons retain actual hashes
and language groups and are explicitly confounded, not isolated causal evidence.
No default changes or live capability claims follow from deterministic tests.

### 14.4.5 Matrix controller recovery (specification 0.44; RM16, RM17, RM25)

A matrix controller MAY run from a separately reviewed, clean committed checkout
using explicit `--measurement-root PATH`. The recorded measurement checkout stays
immutable: runner/configuration/package imports, subprocess working directory,
child import path, manifest, source fingerprint and exact per-cell arguments MUST
refer to it. A separately declared experimental checkout/revision remains the
source for its own cells. Controller code and shared reconciliation helpers come
from the controller revision, never silently from the measurement checkout.

Controller replacement MUST preserve the index's measurement identity, plan,
settings, original attempts, raw checkpoints, exports and costs. Record separate
durable credential-free provenance binding the controller revision/file digests,
measurement identity, recovery reason, time and pre-recovery index/artifact digests.
Later resume MUST verify that provenance. No controller change relabels measured
source or permits a different model, runtime, provider, prior or budget.

Validate raw checkpoint identity exactly. Compare exported identity against the
expected identity after the runner's defined export serialization (including
repository/work path scrubbing); never rewrite evidence or accept arbitrary path
or source differences. Validate export/checkpoint digests, finalized unique request
counts, configuration and prior retention before marking a cell complete. Exact
unseeded C0/C5 reuse obeys these same checks against its original physical attempt.

Both matrix controllers provide `--reconcile-only`, mutually exclusive with
execution/restart: it reads no provider credentials, starts no inference, creates
no attempt, and validates retained completed artifacts before publishing recovered
completion/provenance and any required frozen calibration prior. Missing, stale,
partial or changed evidence refuses recovery without changing coordinator state.
A finalized cell recovered this way is skipped on execution resume; an incomplete
cell still requires the existing explicit new-attempt procedure. Plan counts,
run order, scoring, denominators and runtime defaults remain unchanged.

### 14.4.6 Phase 2 default decisions (specification 0.45; #134, #128, #125)

The M8 matrix (31 cells) and the M7 matrix (9 cells) ran on measured revision
R = 741a19175430e83702a360c454a581b2829e3cb0, one Apple M1 machine, Qwen3-0.6B
Q8_0 on llama.cpp b11785 and Z.ai glm-4.6, one pass per cell, 25 to 26 October
2026. Every cell is complete and both analyses pass reporting acceptance. The
evidence, digests and environment notes are in `docs/EVIDENCE.md` under
"2026-10-26 — Phase 2 M7 and M8 measurements and default decisions". This
revision keeps every default. No code default changes.

| Default | Decision | Paired evidence (full cohort, gains/losses) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| placement.policy local_first | keep | router chain: device_aware 83/156 vs 88/156 (1/6) normal, 80/156 vs 83/156 (1/4) constrained; expected_wait 84/156 (3/7) and 82/156 (5/6). With the example-router chain the policies were within two cases of local_first except constrained expected_wait, 79/156 vs 89/156 (1/11). Every case of both configurations measured `ready`, so the unsuitable-machine hypothesis is not measured |
| selection chain model_router | keep | example-router corpus success equal or higher in five of six pairs (local_first: 88/156 = 88/156 normal, 89/156 vs 83/156 constrained), lower first-useful p50 (1869 vs 8947 ms) but higher p95 (51338 vs 22241 ms); the Jaccard hypothesis needs the section 12.4 capped benchmark and is not measured |
| answer_stream buffered | keep | live 74/156 vs 88/156 (2/16) with 14/156 `predicate_unmet`; none class 73/104 vs 77/104 (2/6), first-useful p95 49521 vs 54260 ms but p50 16980 vs 15827 ms and 8 vs 6 timeouts: not met |
| answer_pass final | keep | draft raises English simple local completion 4/12 to 6/12, but auto 36/52 vs 39/52 (0/3): not held under section 12.3 |
| calls_per_generation one, prefetch false | keep | complex generations per completed run 5.125 to 3.17 (many) and 3.0 (prefetch): held; auto 38/52 vs 39/52 for both (3/4, 1/2) and for the combined cell with the draft pass (2/3; it also lost 0/5 in external_only), so no candidate meets the section 12.3 condition |
| completion criteria in the external request | keep (not an option) | the R-minus-criteria ablation had 0.43 vs 0.094 English and 0.33 vs 0 Chinese corrections per completed run |
| early_headers false | keep | headers arrived before the first answer token in all 39 eligible requests; the before-first-generation clock is not measured |

Single passes carry provider drift and sampling uncertainty; the decisions are
evidence on this machine and configuration, not reliability guarantees, and no
Windows performance is claimed. A later revision may change a default only with
a new run that meets section 12.3, preferably repeated passes of the candidate
and its baseline. The model-quality targets stay separately reported: English
simple local completion 4/12 normal and 0/12 constrained (not met; the
constrained configuration stayed `ready` although it completed nothing
locally), model tool proposals 12/12 (met), no
missed confirmation in any cell (met), and p95 first model action 500 ms (not
met in any router latency cell).

### 14.4.7 Unsuitable detection from local stages (specification 0.48; #170)

The phase 2 run of section 14.4.6 measured the constrained configuration
`ready` in every case, although it completed no simple case locally in auto.
Two causes were found in the recorded run stores. The startup check timed only
the routing-shaped warm-up, while a local stage spends the same active_seconds
on a routing generation and at least one skill generation. And the runner kept
one runtime across cases, so after the first case every warm-up found its
prompt in the runtime's prefix cache. This revision adds, under
placement.detect_stages, the skill-shaped warm-up to the startup check and a
rule that reads what local stages actually did (RM26). The key is false by
default, because the measurement below did not support the unsuitable path on
this machine; the stage outcomes are recorded either way.

A local stage outcome is recorded once per run, when the run's local stage
ends, for a run that made at least one local generation of its own (routing
included; a parallel workflow branch is not one, spec 0.38.1) in local_only, or
in auto with the local model ready. The stage ends with handoff.started, or with
run.completed or run.failed while the run is still local. The outcome is "ran out
of local time" when that event has reason_code local_slow (RM27) or names the
budget active_seconds or run_active_seconds, and "fit" otherwise, including a
stop on a generation or tool limit, which measures the model and not the device.
A cancelled or interrupted run, a run.failed with error code internal_error, and
an event with reason code capability_missing (RM04: a local model that failed,
whether the run then ends as needs_external or hands off in degraded mode,
section 11), record no outcome. A stage that waits for clarification or
confirmation records its outcome when it ends. The outcome carries the local
model ID, the run ID, and the time of the ending event; it is derived from the
trace event that the store writes in the same transaction, so the trace proves it.

With placement.detect_stages true, the rule of RM26 reads the latest three outcomes of the configured local model,
in recorded order. When all three ran out of local time and the latest ended
less than placement.probe_after_seconds ago, the class is unsuitable with a
cause that names the count, the budget, and the time of the latest stage.
Otherwise the class is the one startup detection and readiness set. The rule
never changes a budget, never applies to the absent or unavailable classes, and
applies only with placement.detect true. Because a stage that fits ends the
streak and a stale streak expires, one local stage per probe_after_seconds is
the price of noticing that the machine became fast enough again. The idle probe
of section 14.2.6 is unchanged; a model it restores that keeps running out of
local time is classified again by this rule.

Measurement. Under every placement policy, auto with an unsuitable class is
managed external execution, the same path as auto without a local model
(RM26), apart from the class fields of run.accepted and route.selected. A
deterministic test MUST prove that equivalence on the same request. The
hypothesis of section 12.3 is then measured on recorded evidence of one machine
and one revision: the local-first auto cases of a configuration that the rule
classifies unsuitable (control) against the auto cases of the none-class cell
with the same selector chain without model_router (candidate), paired by case
and by latency schedule. The report names both cells, their revision, and that
the arms ran at different times, so provider latency may have drifted, and it
states that startup warm-up time is in neither arm. A live check on the
measuring machine confirms that the startup line and run.accepted report the
class on the configuration. The offline replay of both rules over the recorded
stores is reported per cell, with the longest run of consecutive outcomes that
ran out of local time in each configuration, so the margin of the threshold is
visible. Recorded handoff.started events before this revision carry no budget,
so the replay treats a local_budget handoff whose local_active_ms reached
active_seconds as one that ran out of local time, and says so.

Evaluation runners that start a new run store per case, as the section 14.4
matrices do, see only the startup check: their stores hold no earlier stages.
placement.detect_stages is a launcher key, not a placement policy setting, so it
is not a runner option and the recorded configurations keep their identity.

Result (27 October 2026, `docs/evidence/2026-10-27-unsuitable/`, derived from the
phase 2 package without rerunning a cell; `scripts/analyze_unsuitable.py`).
Replay: the stage rule fires in all 7 constrained corpus attempts, from the
tenth accepted run of the cell in six of them and the thirty-seventh in the
seventh, and in none of the 8 normal ones; the longest run of
consecutive local stages that ran out of local time is 7 to 26 in the
constrained cells and at most 2 in the normal cells, so the margin of the
threshold of three is one stage. The startup check of this revision classifies
the one cold case of each constrained cell unsuitable (3315 + 2281 ms against
4000 ms) and every other case ready, because the runner kept one runtime and the
later warm-ups were served from its prefix cache; the check of spec 0.47
classified every case ready. Hypothesis: not met. Against local-first auto on
the constrained configuration, managed external execution had 43/52 against
45/52 auto corpus successes (3 gains, 5 losses), p95 first useful content
54260 against 53087 ms and p95 completion 55569 against 53424 ms (p50 15827
against 10358 ms and 17605 against 11794 ms), with 6 timeouts and 16 errors
against 0 and 9. The cause is in the traces: a constrained local stage that runs
out of local time has usually run the skill's tool already, and the handoff
carries the result, so the external stage needed 0.83 calls per simple latency
request against 2.27 without a local stage, at 7.4 and 8.1 s per call. On this
machine and external model, running out of local time did not mean that local
work did not help. So placement.detect_stages stays false, every default stays
as in 0.47, and the section 12.3 hypothesis is recorded as not met with these
numbers. A later revision may revisit it with a faster external model, repeated
passes, or a rule that counts the work a local stage leaves for the external one.
A live check on the same machine (`live-check-apple-m1.json` in the same
directory) confirmed both rules with placement.detect_stages true: a cold
constrained runtime was classified unsuitable at startup (3356 + 2271 ms), a
warm one started ready and became unsuitable after three local_only stages that
each ran out of local time, after which auto ran as managed external execution;
the normal configuration stayed ready.

## 14.5 M10 configuration, startup and source lifecycle (spec 0.51)

**RM38** Useful lexical recommendations MUST become ready independently of generative warm-up, with atomic compatible index publication, bounded background contention and honestly measured startup/update/rebuild/resource gates. Existing run budgets and inference readiness remain unchanged.

The following new tables extend section 14.1 only when implemented after this specification merges. Unknown keys/wrong types are startup errors. Every relative path in these tables (each assets.sources path, user_actions.registrations_path, asset_hybrid.manifest and config, and asset_adaptation.config) resolves against the directory of the launch configuration file. Recommendations default disabled; enabling requires nonempty active scope and explicit source configuration. It does not remove the existing requirement for a model table or change existing launcher defaults. All settings in force are recorded with the recommendation/job config digest; sources/secret references are redacted in evidence. Fixed safety caps cannot be silently enlarged. Positive timing overrides are operator-recorded custom configurations and cannot claim the standard protocol unless equal to its frozen settings.

| Configuration table | Exact keys/types/defaults |
| --- | --- |
| recommendations | enabled:boolean=false; user_id:string and workspace_id:string required when enabled; total_ms:positive integer=500; primary_ms:positive integer=150 and ≤total_ms; primary:string=lexical (lexical, hybrid or http); name/version nonempty strings required for http; url loopback HTTP required only for http; fallback:boolean=true. Disabling fallback is custom and cannot claim service availability guarantees. |
| assets | sources:array of closed `{source_id,kind:local_directory\|metadata_import\|qualified_remote,path:string\|null,adapter_contract:string\|null,secret_ref:string\|null}`; source_id unique. Local/import require path resolved relative to the launch configuration file directory, with null adapter_contract/secret_ref. local_directory reads ordinary root/immediate-child SKILL.md packages under section 4.6.1; metadata_import reads one AssetMetadataImportV1 JSON file with its parent as capture root. Remote requires a qualified permitted adapter_contract reference and optional secret_ref, with null path; no Matrix remote adapter is qualified in 0.51. No embedded credentials or executable source instructions. |
| user_actions | registrations_path:string, optional private operator-owned registration file; default no registrations. File is a schema_version:1 envelope with registrations array of the closed records in section 7.6; no plaintext secrets. No HTTP provisioning. Production OpenClaw registrations are enabled only through the qualified section 7.6.1 contract and the exact adapter revision in the shipped registry; no other registration is enabled. |
| asset_refresh | local_scan_seconds:positive integer=30; remote_seconds:positive integer=3600; backoff_initial_seconds:positive integer=60; backoff_max_seconds:positive integer=3600, ≥initial. Source-required longer intervals take precedence; a configured faster interval is refused. |
| asset_hybrid | manifest:string and config:string required only for hybrid; files pin model artifact/license/digest/device/representation/tokenization/index/fusion/threshold settings frozen from development. No automatic artifact replacement; generation config digest records every setting. Selecting hybrid before those files qualify is a startup error. |
| asset_adaptation | enabled:boolean=false; config:string required if enabled, pins qualified similarity artifact/threshold/version; no hidden training or external feedback. Limits below fixed. |

These fixed field/resource limits are authoritative; KiB/MiB are powers of 1024. JSON strings are checked by UTF-8 bytes in addition to schema checks. A configured custom deadline cannot silently change a fixed safety cap.

| Limit | Value/rule |
| --- | --- |
| Description / canonical tool schema / indexed skill text | 8 KiB / 64 KiB / 256 KiB respectively; no full indexed skill body in selector projection. |
| IDs, source/package/server/tool names, version/mechanism/reason labels / locator | Nonempty ≤256 UTF-8 bytes / ≤2048 UTF-8 bytes. Hashes, UUIDs and fixed enums also follow their exact formats. |
| Language/dependency metadata | ≤32 unique language tags, each 1–64 ASCII letters/digits/hyphens; ≤64 unique dependency identifiers. |
| Repetition | Reject record if any identical NFC-normalized trimmed nonempty line occurs more than three times; ignore blank lines; retain exclusion/coverage evidence, preserve originals. |
| Query | task_text 1–8192 UTF-8 bytes; ≤32 ordered conversation entries, combined text ≤32768 UTF-8 bytes; 1–2 unique requested kinds; language tags as above. |
| Catalog/transport | ≤1000 offered records and ≤32 MiB canonical full projection; ≤1 MiB selector response; ≤512 KiB create body (task, conversation and fixed fields; 0.51 allows no snapshot payload, and a later revision that admits one sets its own bound). Exceeding offer caps refuses selection, never silently chooses a hidden subset. |
| Native RPC request | ≤512 KiB UTF-8 for the whole outer native request frame of every section 7.6.1–7.6.2 operation, including its params envelope, all arguments and fixed native frame fields; any stricter qualified native transport bound still applies. This native cap applies first, before the inner create body is validated. A create body near its own 512 KiB cap can therefore be refused at the native boundary because the outer request exceeds 512 KiB; both bounds hold. |
| Candidates/evidence | Internal pool/candidate_limit=10; displayed≤3; ≤32 evidence_refs per candidate, each a ≤256-byte JSON Pointer to that candidate's offered fields. |
| Pagination | limit 1–1000, default 100; opaque cursor/feedback event IDs follow ID bounds. |
| Idempotency | Format per section 7.4; required, exactly once, on every state-changing POST or DELETE. |
| Action credential | At least 32 cryptographically random bytes, separate from application bearer. |
| Slots/scheduler | One nonqueued recommendation slot, distinct from existing execution slot; one background worker. CPU batch target ≤50 ms; no background GPU embedding concurrent with llama-server. |
| Choice / private retention | 600 seconds from ready_at / 30 days from created_at (feedback bounded by parent). Sweep interval 30 seconds, with lazy logical expiry on every fresh read/action; retention time never extends on replay. |
| Adaptation | Latest 20 eligible corrections in 30 days/scope; ≤3 votes/similar intent; ≤2 positions moved inside primary pool; equal weights, no insertion/confidence promotion. |
| Source exchange/job | One in-flight request per source/job; 10 seconds/exchange including body; 60 seconds total job including requests/validation; ≤1000 requests and ≤1000 distinct candidate records. |
| Source bytes | ≤100 MiB received/job; ≤10 MiB/original asset; ≤4 MiB/inventory/metadata response; streamed buffer ≤1 MiB. First exhausted limit aborts stage. Same record/byte caps for local import jobs. |
| Metadata-import input | Manifest ≤4 MiB UTF-8 bytes; records≤1000, removals≤1000, records+removals≤1000 operations/document. original_path≤2048 UTF-8 bytes, under section 4.6.1 path rules. Aggregate manifest+original bytes consume the existing source job cap; a reused physical staged original counts once and is hashed once; every record's content_hash is compared with that digest. |
| Local directory input | ≤1000 immediate entries/root enumeration and ≤1000 packages/scan; SKILL.md original≤10 MiB, frontmatter≤4 MiB, instruction body≤256 KiB UTF-8 before/after normalization (frontmatter excluded); YAML nesting≤16 levels. Optional strict JSON-object manifest≤4 MiB. All staged bytes consume the 100 MiB/job cap, ≤1 MiB buffer and ≤60-second scan/job. |

Publish validated metadata and its lexical projection atomically before asynchronous vectors. Offer projection cache (spec 0.52; RM24, RM34, RM38). Each eligible record is validated and canonically serialized once, when its revision is published. The offered projection for a catalog_revision, a selection_scope and a requested_kinds set (order does not matter) is the ordered list of those stored record bytes; dispatch reuses them and validates only the per-request envelope fields and the section 14.5 caps. A committed change to an offered record's stale value, its availability or its policy eligibility is a changed committed generation, so it increments catalog_revision under section 4.6, in the same transaction that publishes the new projection. A result computed on an older offer is therefore stale_result under the section 4.6.2 binding check and admission's revision recheck. A capture-time-only observation (section 4.6.1) keeps catalog_revision and the projection unchanged: the projection carries the captured_at of the published generation until the next revision. One recommendation keeps its pinned catalog_revision for every attempt, including the lexical fallback. If a new generation's projection cannot be prepared, that generation is not published; if the last published generation no longer matches committed stale, availability or eligibility state, the service has no usable catalog (section 11.1): readiness reports unavailable and create returns recommendation_unavailable at step 4 of the section 7.6 precedence list. Vector keys include exact content/representation/model/config identities. Query pins a coherent catalog and compatible vector entries; additions without vectors are lexical immediately; old/deleted versions are filtered against authoritative current catalog. Failed generation never replaces the last usable one. Corruption with no usable snapshot is unavailable, not ready. Recovery rebuilds derived state from catalog without re-downloading all source originals or clearing feedback.

For recommendations enabled, startup still validates all static settings/artifacts and performs run/recommendation/job restart recovery before accepting work. It opens the service when a compatible committed lexical catalog is usable (or exposes honest empty/unavailable status), then runs generative warm-up as independently reported work. Section 14.1's startup order states this step; disabled configuration keeps the existing order. No inference request bypasses its own readiness/placement policy. A healthy socket or warm-up progress is not useful lexical acceptance (section 12.6). Missing/disabled service returns recommendation_unavailable on create, with unavailable readiness; invalid bodies are still rejected under section 7.6 precedence.

Background dispatch pauses during generative warm-up or active execution-slot work. An in-flight CPU batch must drain under its measured bound; no unsupported preemption promise is made. Busy query embedding reports not_ready for lexical fallback. Record overlap/batch drain, scheduler settings and RM27 actual contended samples; never discard slow samples or relabel them idle. Indexing cannot consume the execution slot or create its spurious 429. The operational gates include all serialization/admission/persistence.

Local rescan detects changes within its configured scan interval, separate from receipt-to-lexical visibility. External jobs run only for a qualified permitted configured source. Failure schedules initial backoff, doubles after each consecutive failed job up to max, resets on success; no same-job retry loop. Refresh staging is bounded under the table, with import visibility workload batches of 25 metadata records. Publish a valid complete source generation atomically. Invalid/incomplete/cancelled/oversized/timed-out stages keep last good revision and record failure counts, last success time and stale status. For initial bounded explicit imports, coverage remains partial until all declared batches complete; that partial import never authorizes omission-based deletions. Larger source inventories must fit explicitly qualified bounds; do not auto-split an over-limit full refresh and infer completeness.

Only explicit complete inventory or documented source tombstones prove removals. A missing page/item in a failed partial fetch does not. Restart marks interrupted jobs and derives replacement work under the same budgets/backoff; shutdown cancels staging, never commits a partial generation. Successful fetch says nothing about current host permissions/availability. Source secrets resolve only inside acquisition transport and never enter metadata, vectors, logs, model context or exports. Acquisition permissions and archive safety are separate from byte integrity. MATRIX-REFRESH remains a named blocker; these finite builder limits do not qualify its interface or legal cadence.

### 14.5.1 Hybrid semantic configuration (spec 0.58; RM38, RM41)

`asset_hybrid` retains exactly its existing launch keys, `manifest` and `config`; both paths resolve relative to the launch configuration directory. The manifest is section 6.1. The config is `AssetHybridConfigV1`, a closed UTF-8 JSON object with all fields below required. Apply section 6.1's 64 KiB/parser/string/hash rules and strict finite numeric types. No tuning defaults are supplied: all values MUST be frozen from development before acceptance and covered by the activation registry.

| Config field | Exact value/type |
| --- | --- |
| schema_version | Integer 1. |
| identity | Closed `{name,version}` identity of the configured primary. |
| manifest_digest | Full section 6.1 manifest digest; MUST equal the actual validated manifest. |
| representation | Closed `{version:"asset_fields.v1",normalization:"NFC",query_prefix:string,document_prefix:string,max_tokens:integer,overflow:"unavailable"}`. Prefixes are each 0..256 UTF-8 bytes; max_tokens is 1..8192. |
| index | Closed `{version:"exact_cosine.v1",batch_size:integer}`; batch_size is 1..25. |
| fusion | Closed `{version:"weighted_rrf.v1",lexical_weight:number,semantic_weight:number,k:integer,min_cosine:number}`. Each weight is finite and >0; their binary64 sum differs from 1 by at most 1e-12; k is 1..1000; min_cosine is in [-1,1]. |
| confidence | Closed `{min_score:number,min_margin:number}`, both finite in [0,1]. |

`asset_fields.v1` uses only the same offered projection as other v2 selectors. The query is the current redacted `task_text` alone. A skill document's fields, in order, are `description`, `identity.package_id`. An MCP tool document's fields, in order, are `description`, `identity.server_id`, `identity.tool_name`, `identity.exposed_name`. Normalize each field to NFC without trimming or case folding. The query bytes are the exact query_prefix followed by the normalized task_text. The document bytes are the exact document_prefix followed by those normalized field values joined with one LF (U+000A); there is no leading/trailing LF added by the format. Prefix bytes are opaque pinned text and are not implicitly normalized. Field-internal LF is preserved. No body, conversation, schema, label, feedback, target hint or extra catalog field enters this representation. Each complete constructed input, including prefix and separators, is at most 16 KiB UTF-8.

Tokenization preserves field boundaries: tokenize prefix and each normalized field independently without adding per-segment special tokens, placing the tokenizer's separately encoded LF token sequence between document fields, then apply its pinned sequence-level special tokens once. Empty prefix contributes no tokens. Thus token pieces cannot merge across prefix/field or field/LF boundaries. The pinned tokenizer bytes and reviewed runtime implementation define segmentation and sequence-level special tokens; a backend unable to implement this policy is incompatible. Count the exact resulting token sequence including its special tokens against max_tokens before inference. Unknown tokenization, input-byte excess or token excess is unavailable, never assumed to fit, silently clipped, summarized or retried with another representation. The representation bytes digest remains SHA-256 of the exact constructed UTF-8 bytes. It is not by itself a complete identity for independently tokenized inputs: every derived vector key and compatibility check MUST additionally bind the segmentation digest defined below. The config/manifest digests continue to bind the tokenizer policy, prefixes and limits.

The internal ordered segment descriptor is the closed object `{representation_revision,segments}`. `representation_revision` is exactly `asset_fields.v1` or `task_text.v1` as applicable. `segments` is an ordered array of closed `{field,value}` objects. Its first entry is always the exact unnormalized prefix, including an empty prefix: field is `document_prefix` for asset documents and `query_prefix` for primary/adaptation queries and retained adaptation intents. Remaining entries are the NFC-normalized source field values in construction order. A skill document uses field names `/description`, `/identity/package_id`; an MCP document uses `/description`, `/identity/server_id`, `/identity/tool_name`, `/identity/exposed_name`. Every query/retained intent uses the logical field name `/task_text`, whether its source is current redacted task_text or a correction's redacted_task_text. No other field or source value is added. Separator LF tokens and the single sequence-level special-token policy are fixed by the representation/config/manifest; they are not additional source fields or duplicated in this descriptor. `segmentation_digest` is `canonical_digest` of this entire descriptor, preserving array order and original prefix bytes. It is internal derived-state identity, not an EmbeddingObservationV1 or public request/result field. Query, document and private adaptation vector/cache compatibility binds both the constructed-byte digest and this segmentation digest; neither substitutes for the other or for the existing complete config/manifest identities.

For example, with empty document_prefix, skill fields `description="a\nb"`, `identity.package_id="c"` and `description="a"`, `identity.package_id="b\nc"` both construct bytes `a\nb\nc` (SHA-256 `ea7fb08b7a2dc4619ffb7c7bb38d95a2047935fa165d71b12efd3852a2e6d0cc`). Their ordered descriptor values differ, so their segmentation digests and complete vector keys MUST differ even when scope/asset ID/content hash/source version/model/config remain equal. The old vector is incompatible with the updated fields. Identical ordered normalized fields, prefix and representation revision produce identical descriptors/digests and, with all other identities equal, identical keys. Do not strip valid field-internal LF or change the input/tokenization algorithm to avoid this distinction.

`exact_cosine.v1` admits exactly one vector of `dimensions` finite numeric components per input, with exact batch count, no booleans, nested/ragged values, zero norm or excess components. Reject output shape/count before copying to trusted buffers; check count×dimensions×element-size before allocation, with batch≤25, dimensions≤4096 and offer≤1000. No ANN or hidden candidate shortlist is used. The backend may use a reviewed native numeric representation, but gateway normalization/scoring converts components to IEEE 754 binary64; overflow on conversion is invalid.

Gateway numeric work uses binary64 round-to-nearest/ties-to-even and dimension order 0..dimensions-1, rounding each operation separately, without fused multiply-add, parallel reduction or intermediate extended precision. To normalize, let m be the maximum absolute component; refuse m=0/nonfinite. Set y_i=x_i/m, accumulate s from 0 by `s = s + y_i*y_i` in order, then set u_i=y_i/sqrt(s). Refuse any nonfinite result. Cosine is the ordered sum from 0 of u_i*v_i, clamped to [-1,1] only for rounding overshoot. Use this same operation order in adaptation. Backend-generated vectors may vary with an artifact/runtime/device; those exact identities remain frozen and are not a claim of cross-runtime bit-identical inference.

Vector association/key closure binds scope, asset_id, exact content_hash/source_version (and schema_hash for MCP), representation bytes digest, segmentation_digest and representation version, full manifest digest, artifact_hash, runtime/device identity and full hybrid config digest. Derive keys from the trusted pinned offer, never from supplied vector labels. A metadata change affecting representation invalidates the vector even when original content_hash is unchanged. Deleted, old-hash, wrong-scope, policy-ineligible or incompatible vectors are excluded before ranking. One pinned offer includes additions immediately in lexical coverage, even if their vectors are pending. Partial/failing generations retain only still-compatible entries and follow section 14.5 publication/recovery/scheduler rules. No compatible semantic vectors means not_ready/selector_not_ready to the coordinator, even if lexical overlaps exist; there is no successful hidden lexical implementation called hybrid.

For `weighted_rrf.v1`, use the validated frozen weights directly; do not renormalize them within the validation tolerance. Form the lexical list using the published projection-only lexical algorithm, keeping all positive-overlap records before pool truncation. Form the semantic list from all compatible offered vectors whose cosine is ≥min_cosine, including equality. Each list sorts descending by its score, then ascending by unsigned UTF-8 asset_id bytes. For every member of their union compute, with one-based ranks,

`score = lexical_weight/(k+lexical_rank) + semantic_weight/(k+semantic_rank)`.

An absent-list contribution is exactly zero. Evaluate lexical term then semantic term and add in that order using the binary64 rules above. Sort fused scores descending, ties by the same asset_id order, and retain at most the request's candidate_limit (fixed 10 in the core). No supported union member gives valid no_match with no candidates. All candidate identities/hashes/versions come from the offer; `score_kind` is `weighted_rrf.v1`, not probability. For a candidate present in the semantic list (cosine ≥min_cosine), evidence_refs MUST include every offered field actually used to construct its document vector. For a candidate present in the lexical list, also include precisely those offered fields with positive lexical overlap under the published lexical algorithm. Emit the union once in the document representation-field order above. Evaluating a vector that is excluded by min_cosine adds no semantic evidence pointers; a lexical-only candidate has only its positive-overlap pointers. Identity-field pointers occur only when those exact fields were constructed as model input or supplied a positive lexical overlap. Prefix/config/descriptor/separator metadata is not an offered field and has no evidence pointer; do not invent a schema.json or other metadata pointer. Semantic attribution names supplied input, never hidden model reasoning. Every pointer obeys existing candidate bounds.

For a nonempty ranked pool, confident additionally requires a valid compatible semantic vector for **every eligible offered record**, top fused score ≥min_score, and top-minus-second score ≥min_margin. If there is no second candidate its score is zero. Otherwise decision is choice_required. Full coverage is a necessary condition even when the absent vector would probably rank low. Config thresholds are development-frozen; no held-out tuning or implicit confidence promotion is permitted. Query embedding/index failures use section 4.6.2 declared unavailable/not_ready/timeout/cancel behavior; only the coordinator may invoke the separately recorded lexical floor.

### 14.5.2 Adaptation semantic configuration (spec 0.58; RM37, RM42)

`asset_adaptation` retains exactly `enabled:boolean=false` and `config:string` required when enabled. An explicitly supplied config must validate and qualify even if enabled=false; disabled adaptation performs no feedback read or similarity inference. An omitted config while disabled is allowed. The launch config path resolves against the launch configuration directory. Its `AssetAdaptationConfigV1` document is closed, ≤64 KiB, uses section 6.1 parsing/type/string/hash rules, and has all following fields required:

| Config field | Exact value/type |
| --- | --- |
| schema_version | Integer 1. |
| identity | Closed `{name,version}` adaptation identity. |
| manifest | Nonempty path string, ≤2048 UTF-8 bytes, resolved relative to this adaptation config's directory. It references section 6.1's manifest, not another model format. |
| manifest_digest | Full digest of that validated manifest. |
| representation | Closed `{version:"task_text.v1",normalization:"NFC",query_prefix:string,max_tokens:integer,overflow:"unavailable"}`; prefix 0..256 UTF-8 bytes; max_tokens 1..8192. |
| similarity | Closed `{version:"exact_cosine.v1",threshold:number}`; finite threshold in [-1,1]. |
| ranking_version | Exactly `bounded_votes.v1`. |

`task_text.v1` represents the current redacted task_text and each retained correction's redacted_task_text with the exact query construction, independent prefix/field tokenization, byte/token caps and no-truncation rule of section 14.5.1, using this config's prefix/token limit and verified tokenizer. No conversation, target text, action evidence or primary-specific hidden input is added. Similarity matches iff the section 14.5.1 exact cosine is ≥threshold. Batch/output validation uses the same 1..25 batch and exact dimension rules; the current task plus at most 20 retained intents fits that cap without a second worker. The actual artifact/threshold and all representation settings are development-frozen; this revision selects no numerical threshold.

`bounded_votes.v1` is the following deterministic rule; its fixed limits cannot be enlarged by config:

1. Read eligible authenticated persisted explicit corrections in the active user/workspace scope against the pinned/current catalog. Apply current content/schema/version/policy eligibility, deletion and actual retain_until/parent retention **before** truncation; expiry is effective at now≥retain_until. Historical configured_primary is provenance, not an extra selector-name filter. Replayed feedback is one stored record. Sort ascending by recorded_at instant, then unsigned UTF-8 feedback_id bytes; retain the latest 20. Take records and active feedback_revision in one coherent store-owned snapshot.
2. For the current task, compare those retained intents using the qualified representation/similarity. Among matching records take the latest three **total**, ordered by that same key. Each contributes one equal vote to its exact `(asset_id,content_hash)`. Out-of-pool targets consume a vote and cannot be inserted or replaced by an older helpful record. No per-target three-vote allowance, negative vote, decay, training or hidden intent cluster exists.
3. Start from the exact primary order of at most 10 candidates and preserve each one's base position. Make adjacent passes left to right. Swap right before left only when the right has strictly more votes and **both** resulting positions are within two of those candidates' own base positions. Ties never swap. Repeat until a pass has no swaps; each swap removes a strict vote inversion, so there are at most 45 swaps for 10 candidates.
4. Preserve the candidate set and every candidate field/score/evidence byte; only order may change. If top identity changes, final decision is choice_required; otherwise retain the base decision. Never promote confidence. `changed` is true iff final candidate order or decision differs from base, not merely because feedback exists.

Apply only after syntactically valid configured-primary output has passed its offer/current binding checks and before final admission. Preserve that immutable primary result. Do not apply to the gateway lexical floor or empty_catalog; adaptation is null there. A valid primary no_match stays unchanged, performs no similarity calls and may record the configured adaptation with current feedback_revision and changed=false. A ranked primary with no eligible history likewise requires no inference and records changed=false. These no-op stages still pin/recheck the feedback revision and expiry/currentness before ready.

Private derived keys additionally bind active scope, feedback_revision, exact retained correction IDs/content bindings and representations (including constructed-byte and segmentation digests), current public catalog revision and exact similarity manifest/artifact/runtime/device/config identities. Register all transient task/history/vector/cache buffers with synchronous erasure ownership before use. Expiry/delete/reset invalidates access before acknowledgment, removes derived copies and fences pending work from republishing. Do not persist plain text lookup copies or plain erased-text hashes; use only section 5.1's existing keyed tombstones where prescribed. Reset retains no trained state and reproduces baseline candidate/order/decision semantic bytes for identical deterministic inputs/configuration; IDs/timestamps/timings and truthful adaptation revision metadata may differ.

### 14.5.3 Digest closure and startup (spec 0.58; RM38, RM41, RM42)

Each semantic config digest is `canonical_digest` of its entire validated closed JSON object; no default, field or threshold is omitted. `manifest_digest` resolves and binds the entire referenced manifest; hybrid's manifest launch path is a locator, not another semantic setting. Adaptation's manifest locator is part of its explicit config digest. Absolute resolved installation roots are not injected into artifact/config digests. A relocation preserving declared semantic documents and verified files may reuse qualification; changing an explicit locator value changes the full config digest and must match a reviewed registry identity. No mutable referenced semantic setting is outside this digest closure.

The existing accepted/job `config_digest` MUST cover the redacted effective launch configuration, both active semantic config digests/full manifest digests/artifact identities, effective budgets/scheduler settings and active implementation identities. Do not add a second public config_digest field or silently change meaning to only a weights hash. Source/secret references follow existing redaction. Keys for derived state carry all applicable identities above even if a caller supplies the same model name. Exact identities are recorded before serving work; startup refuses missing, malformed, incompatible, mismatched or unqualified supplied semantic files. Qualified bytes/runtime/device are verified before activation; no heavy load precedes checking registry authority. A semantic artifact/representation/config/runtime/device change requires requalification, not an automatic replacement.
